Fixed via a new `app.transform.edi_common.sanitize_x12_text(value)` (replaces `*`/`:`/`~`/newlines with a space - a disclosed, deliberate lossy simplification, not a guess: X12 defines no universal escape-character convention this app's own parser understands, so losslessly preserving a literal reserved character would need extending the parser itself, out of scope for a reverse-direction fix) applied everywhere free text enters a segment: `build_org_nm1`/`build_person_nm1`'s own name/identifier fields (used by every EDI family via `org_or_person_nm1`), `remittance_835.py`'s own N1 builder and `CLP01` claim id, `edi_271.py`'s own `EB05` description, and the `BHT03`/`TRN02` trace-number field (`bht_reference`/`trace_number`, built from `Bundle.identifier.value`) independently duplicated across `edi_270.py`/`edi_271.py`/`claim_status.py`/`prior_auth.py`/`claim_837p.py`/`claim_837i.py`/`claim_837d.py`/`remittance_835.py` - the identical unsanitized pattern recurred in all eight, caught by grepping for it once the first instance was found, not fixed one-by-one as separately-discovered bugs. `test_edi_837p_sanitizes_x12_reserved_characters_in_organization_name` is the regression test.
    - **Money fields formatted via `str(Decimal)` are vulnerable to silent trailing-zero loss through the documented Bundle-JSON round trip**: `fhir.resources`'/pydantic's own JSON serialization writes `Decimal("100.10")` as the bare JSON number `100.1` (confirmed directly), and `app/routes/convert.py::_run_transform`'s own `json.loads()`→`Bundle.model_validate()` path (the real code behind the documented Convert → "Use Bundle above" → Transform UI flow, and the equivalent `/api/convert`→`/api/transform` API flow) reconstructs it as `Decimal("100.1")` - `str(value)` on a money field then silently emits `"100.1"` where the original X12 element was `"100.10"`. Not a bug in `app/transform`'s own logic originally, but directly reachable through a real, documented user flow and affecting every `str(x.value)` call on a `Money` field. Fixed by formatting every money field (never quantity fields, which are legitimately bare integers in this app's own X12 output) with `:.2f` instead of `str()` - `CLM02`/`SV1-02`/`SV2-03`/`SV3-02` charge amounts, `BPR02` payment amount, `CLP03`/`CLP04` charge/paid amounts - immune to the JSON round trip since it always re-quantizes to 2 places regardless of the Decimal's own current precision, and arguably more correct anyway (X12 money elements are conventionally 2-decimal). `test_edi_837p_preserves_trailing_zero_in_money_via_fixed_precision` is the regression test, reproducing the real JSON round trip directly rather than just constructing a pre-truncated Decimal by hand.
    - Verified via the full test suite, pyflakes, a fresh 200-seed fuzz sweep (reverse-then-forward, zero failures) for every EDI family the fix touched (270, 271, 835, 837P, 837I, 837D) and a fresh 300-seed fuzz sweep for every C-CDA document type (CCD, Discharge Summary, History and Physical) - none of the pre-existing fuzz sweeps regressed, confirming the fixes are purely additive safety nets, not behavior changes for well-formed input.

The previously-"remaining" HL7v2 trigger events for these four message types are now all implemented: ADT A38 (cancel pre-admit - the A05 sibling of A11/A13's cancel pattern), ORU R31/R32 (the remaining point-of-care variants alongside R30/R40), and MDM T08/T10/T11 (edit/replacement/cancel notifications) all shipped following the exact same patterns as their already-implemented siblings - see the per-type extension-point notes below for what (if anything) each one needed beyond a trigger-event string swap. Any future HL7v2 trigger event for one of these four types (there's no exhaustive list being tracked - add one if/when it's needed) can follow the same precedent. For C-CDA, Medications/Allergies/Immunizations now ship alongside Problems, Discharge Summary and History and Physical Note now ship alongside CCD, Vital Signs/Results/Procedures now ship alongside all three document types, Discharge Summary's own Hospital Discharge Diagnosis/Discharge Medications sections now ship too, and Discharge Summary's remaining two required sections (Hospital Course, Plan of Treatment) and History and Physical's own nine required narrative sections (Reason for Visit/Chief Complaint, History of Present Illness, Physical Exam, Assessment, Plan of Care, Review of Systems, Social History, Family History, General Status) now ship as well, each converting to its own `DocumentReference`+`Binary` pair rather than a full FHIR-Document `Bundle(type="document")`+`Composition` (see the C-CDA subsection below, `app/cda/narrative_sections.py`) - every IG-required section either document type has is now recognized, closing what had been this pillar's own last standing forward-conversion gap. EDI support now spans all five "big five" HIPAA transaction-set families - 270/271, 276/277, 278, 835, and 837 in all three of its Professional/Institutional/Dental variants - see the EDI subsection below. Bundle deduplication now ships too (see `app/dedup.py` above). Bidirectional transformation has also started (see `app/transform/` above) - FHIR → HL7v2 ADT (now all nine triggers, including the three cancel triggers: A01/A02/A03/A04/A05/A08/A11/A13/A38), FHIR → HL7v2 SIU (now all six triggers: S12/S13/S14/S15/S17/S26), FHIR → HL7v2 ORU (now all five triggers: R01/R30/R31/R32/R40), FHIR → HL7v2 MDM (now all six triggers: T02/T04/T06/T08/T10/T11), FHIR → C-CDA CCD (now header plus all seven general-purpose sections: Problems, Medications, Allergies, Immunizations, Vital Signs, Results, Procedures), FHIR → C-CDA Discharge Summary and FHIR → C-CDA History and Physical (both reusing that same header+section infrastructure as a second and third document type), and FHIR → X12 270/271/276/277/278/835/837P/837I/837D prove the reverse-direction architecture generalizes across all three input formats this app supports, reuses cleanly within a format (the 270/271 sibling pair, 276/277's own, 278 reusing 270/271's own shared resolvers directly, 835's own genuinely simpler no-HL-hierarchy shape, and 837I/837D both reusing 837P's own resolution logic and loop shape directly - 837D's own arrival being what finally promoted the by-id resolution helpers 837P/837I had each independently duplicated into `app.transform.edi_common`, the reverse-side mirror of the forward direction's own `resolve_837_loops` promotion - confirming that shape genuinely generalizes across the whole 837 family; CCD/Discharge Summary/History and Physical), scales to full trigger-event breadth for all four HL7v2 message types (ADT, SIU, ORU, MDM) - every HL7v2 trigger event this app's forward direction supports is now also a reverse target, all three C-CDA document types this app converts *to* FHIR now have a reverse target too, and every EDI transaction-set family this app's forward direction supports (the full "big five" HIPAA suite, including all three 837 variants) now has a reverse target as well - and handles genuinely different FHIR resource shapes and grouping relationships (SIU's own `Appointment`, ORU's own `DiagnosticReport`+`Observation` grouping, MDM's own `DocumentReference`+`Binary`, CCD Vital Signs'/Results' own panel-referencing-member-`Observation`s grouping in two different panel shapes - an `Observation` panel and a `DiagnosticReport` - none just an `Encounter` variant). The bidirectional-transformation pillar has now reached full family-level breadth across every format the forward direction supports; CCD's own Hospital Discharge Diagnosis section now has a reverse target too (Discharge Medications remains a disclosed, permanent limitation - the forward mapper leaves no FHIR-side marker to reverse). Forward conversion's own former narrative-section gap is closed (see above), and the *reverse* direction now regenerates every one of these `DocumentReference`+`Binary` narrative sections too (Discharge Summary's Hospital Course/Plan of Treatment, History and Physical's own nine), via `app/transform/cda_ccd.py::_build_narrative_section` - a follow-up slice shipped immediately after the forward-conversion one that added them, not left as a standing gap (see the C-CDA subsection above and `cda_ccd.py`'s own bullet). README's own remaining named pillar, test-data generation, is already fully covered across all three formats (`app/generators/`/`app/cda/generator.py`/`app/edi/generator.py`).

## Commands

No platform-specific dependencies (everything in `pyproject.toml` is pure Python) - only the venv activation step differs by shell.

```powershell
# one-time setup (Windows / PowerShell)
python -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
pip install -e ".[dev]"
```

```bash
# one-time setup (macOS / Linux, bash/zsh)
python3 -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate
pip install -e ".[dev]"
```

```
# run the app (http://127.0.0.1:8000) - identical once the venv above is active
uvicorn app.main:app --reload

# run the full test suite
pytest

# run a single test
pytest tests/test_adt_a03_mapping.py::test_discharge_sets_finished_status_and_period_end -v
```

There is no separate lint/build step configured yet.

## Architecture

**Request flow:** `app/routes/convert.py` (FastAPI routes for `/`, `/convert`, `/api/convert`, `/validate`, `/api/validate`, `/api/generate`, `/healthz`) calls `app/pipeline.py::convert_to_bundle(raw_text) -> Bundle` for conversion and `app/pipeline.py::validate_any(raw_text) -> ValidationReport` for validation. Both are thin format-sniffing dispatchers sitting above three independent, format-specific pipelines, checked in this order since the three signatures are mutually exclusive by construction and X12's check is the cheapest: `app/pipeline.py::is_x12(raw_text)` (does the text start with the literal `"ISA"`, ignoring a possible leading BOM/whitespace - deliberately not `"ISA*"`, since the element separator is discovered dynamically, not spec-guaranteed to be `*`) routes to `app/edi/pipeline.py`'s `convert_edi_to_bundle`/`validate_edi` for X12 EDI; else `app/pipeline.py::is_xml(raw_text)` (does the text start with `<`) routes to `app/cda/pipeline.py`'s `convert_cda_to_bundle`/`validate_cda` for C-CDA XML; else `app/hl7/pipeline.py`'s `convert_hl7_to_bundle`/`validate_hl7` handles HL7v2 - none of the three format-specific pipelines knows another format exists, and all three raise the same exception shapes on each axis (`MissingSegmentError`/`MappingError`/`pydantic.ValidationError` for conversion, plus a format-specific parse error for each) so `app/routes/convert.py` needs only one dispatch table per axis, not a parallel one per format. `convert_hl7_to_bundle` parses via `app/hl7/parser.py`, reads MSH-9 for the message type/trigger event, looks up a mapper in `app/mappings/registry.py`, and calls `mapper.to_bundle(message)`; `validate_hl7` parses the same way and delegates to `app/validation/engine.py::validate_message()` (see the validation extension point below). The CDA side mirrors this exactly - `convert_cda_to_bundle`/`validate_cda` in `app/cda/pipeline.py` both parse via `app/cda/parser.py::parse_document` then delegate to `get_document_builder(...).build_bundle(...)`/`app/cda/validation.py::validate_document()` respectively (see the C-CDA subsection below). The EDI side mirrors it again one level further out - `convert_edi_to_bundle`/`validate_edi` in `app/edi/pipeline.py` parse via `app/edi/parser.py::parse_interchange` then take the interchange's `first_transaction_set()` and delegate to `get_transaction_builder(st01).build_bundle(transaction_set, interchange.delimiters)`/`app/edi/validation.py::validate_interchange()` respectively (see the EDI subsection below - `delimiters` is threaded through the builder interface, not just the parser, once a real consumer needed it). `/api/generate?message_type=&trigger_event=&seed=` runs the mirror-image path through `app/generators/registry.py::generate()` and returns raw text instead of a Bundle (HL7v2, C-CDA, or EDI, all registered in the same flat registry - see the C-CDA/EDI subsections) - the UI's message-type dropdown (populated from `app/generators/registry.py::list_supported_types()`) and "Generate sample" button call it to fill the textarea. The **Convert to FHIR** and **Validate** buttons are two `type="submit"` buttons sharing one `<form>`, distinguished via `formaction` (`/api/convert` vs `/api/validate`, or their `/convert`/`/validate` no-JS form-post fallbacks) - `app/static/app.js` branches on the standard `event.submitter` property to know which one triggered the submit.

**Extension point for new message types:** `app/mappings/base.py` defines the `MessageMapper` interface (`to_bundle(message) -> Bundle`). Each HL7 message *type* gets its own module (`app/mappings/adt.py`, `app/mappings/siu.py`, `app/mappings/oru.py`, `app/mappings/mdm.py`) implementing that interface, registered in `app/mappings/registry.py` as `{(message_type, trigger_event): mapper_instance}` — no changes to routes, the parser, or templates. Cross-message-type shared logic lives in `app/mappings/common.py`:
- `build_patient(pid) -> Patient` — PID is mapped identically no matter the message type.
- `build_identifier_from_cx(repetition, fallback_system, ...)` — the CX[Identifier] datatype map: CX.1 -> `.value`, CX.4 -> `.system` (local placeholder when the assigning authority is absent), CX.5 -> `.type.coding.code`. Shared by PID-3 and PV1-5, which is what stopped PV1-5 arriving as a bare value with no system or type.
- `apply_pv1_encounter_fields(encounter, pv1)` — PV1-4/5/10/13/14/15/16/38 -> `Encounter.type`, `.serviceType` and `.hospitalization`. Called by both `build_encounter_core` and `build_minimal_encounter`: the IG's PV1[Encounter] map is message-type agnostic, so reading these only for ADT dropped every one of them from an ORU or MDM context encounter. `.hospitalization` is built lazily, so an encounter carrying none of these fields does not gain an empty one.
- **Choice-type precedence comes from the IG, not from field order.** PID-25 (birth order) -> `multipleBirthInteger` supersedes PID-24 -> `multipleBirthBoolean`, and PID-29 (death date) -> `deceasedDateTime` supersedes PID-30 -> `deceasedBoolean`, because each map states it verbatim ("IF PID-25 NOT VALUED"). A message valuing both halves of a pair is contradicting itself, which `app/validation/generic.py` flags rather than resolving silently. Y/N fields map only `Y`/`N`; anything else leaves the field unset rather than guessing a boolean.
- **Repeating fields are read on every repetition, not just the first.** PID-3, PID-13 and PV1-7/8/9/17 are all 0..-1 in the IG's own cardinality column; reading one silently discarded the rest, which is what a second identifier or a referring doctor actually is.
- `assemble_bundle(msh, patient, *resources) -> Bundle` — MSH-derived Bundle metadata + entries, generic over however many additional resources a message type produces.
- `resolve_encounter_class(pv1) -> Coding` / `build_visit_identifier(pv1) -> Identifier | None` — PV1-2 (patient class) → `Encounter.class` and PV1-19 (visit number) → an `Identifier`, used by `adt.py`'s `build_encounter_core` and `common.py`'s own `build_minimal_encounter` below. `adt.py` and `oru.py` independently hand-rolled the same `{"I":"IMP","O":"AMB",...}` mapping before it was extracted here - if you're building a third PV1-derived Encounter somewhere, use these rather than a third copy.
- `build_minimal_encounter(pv1, patient_id) -> Encounter` — a minimal, status-`"unknown"` Encounter for message types whose PV1 (when present) gives context rather than an admit/discharge lifecycle event. Used by both `oru.py`'s optional result-reporting encounter and `mdm.py`'s optional document-context encounter. This was independently duplicated byte-for-byte between `oru.py` and `mdm.py` before being extracted here - caught by code review, not the original test suite.
- `location_display(segment, field_num)` / `person_display(segment, field_num)` — PL/XCN-shaped **display-string** formatting, for when a field only needs to read as text (e.g. `Encounter.location`/`Encounter.participant` display in `adt.py`, which doesn't materialize separate Location/Practitioner resources). `person_display` falls back to the XCN id (component 1) when family/given are both empty — it must stay in sync with `build_practitioner_from_xcn`'s presence check ("id or family or given") below, since anywhere both are paired into one `Reference` (as `siu.py`'s AIP participants and `mdm.py`'s TXA-9/TXA-10 do), a field with only an id and no name would otherwise materialize a real Practitioner but produce `Reference(display="")`, which FHIR rejects (`display` must be a non-empty string when present). This exact crash shipped once (an id-only AIP, e.g. `AIP|1||5678||||`) and was caught by code review, not the original test suite — see `tests/test_siu_s12_mapping.py::test_aip_with_id_only_materializes_with_id_as_display`.
- `build_reference_with_optional_display(resource_id, display) -> Reference` — the defensive counterpart to the crash above: a `Reference` to a materialized resource with `display` omitted entirely (rather than passed as `""`) whenever the display text couldn't be resolved, so any *future* caller pairing an XCN/PL-sourced resource with a display string fails safe instead of crashing. First written inline in `siu.py`'s `add_participant`, independently re-derived in `mdm.py` for TXA-9/TXA-10, then extracted here once both existed - both now call this shared helper.
- `build_location_from_pl(segment, field_num) -> Location | None` / `build_practitioner_from_xcn(segment, field_num) -> Practitioner | None` — the same PL/XCN field shapes, but building a **real, separately-referenceable resource** instead of just a string. Used by `siu.py` (AIL→Location, AIP→Practitioner), `oru.py` (OBX-16→Practitioner performer), and `mdm.py` (TXA-9/TXA-10→Practitioner originator/authenticator). Prefer these over the display-only pair whenever the target field is an `actor`/`performer`/similar `Reference` rather than a plain string, since a real resource is more useful to any downstream consumer (they can look up the practitioner's identifier, not just read a name). These are PL/XCN-scoped specifically - don't reuse them for a differently-shaped field (e.g. CWE) just because two of its components happen to look similar; build that shape's resource directly instead (see the AIG note below for a case where this was gotten wrong once).

Generic, reusable *field-level* FHIR-fragment builders (name/address/datetime/gender parsing, `build_codeable_concept_from_cwe` for CWE-typed fields) live in `app/fhir_models/builders.py`, shared across all message types; message-type-specific field logic stays in that type's module.

**Extension point for trigger events within one message type:** `app/mappings/adt.py` and `app/mappings/siu.py` follow the same shape — a `Base*Mapper(MessageMapper)` whose `to_bundle()` does the parts identical across every trigger event for that message type (require the message type's core segments, read any optional repeating segments, build the Patient via the shared `build_patient`, delegate to an abstract per-trigger build method, assemble the Bundle via the shared `assemble_bundle`), with concrete trigger subclasses implementing only the part that actually varies:
- `app/mappings/adt.py`: `BaseAdtMapper.build_encounter(pv1, evn, patient_id) -> Encounter`. Most subclasses (`AdtA01Mapper`, `AdtA04Mapper`, `AdtA05Mapper`) just call the shared `build_encounter_core(pv1, evn, patient_id, status=...)` (`A05` pre-admit uses `status="planned"`); `AdtA02Mapper` additionally layers in PV1-6 prior-location history; `AdtA03Mapper` requires a resolvable discharge time (PV1-45 or EVN-2 fallback) and maps PV1-36 discharge disposition; `AdtA08Mapper` infers status from whether a discharge time is present. `AdtA11Mapper` (cancel admit), `AdtA13Mapper` (cancel discharge), and `AdtA38Mapper` (cancel pre-admit) all set `status="entered-in-error"` — a deliberate choice, not IG-mandated (the v2-to-FHIR IG's own A11 guidance applies no special cancel handling at all, which would make an A11-derived Encounter indistinguishable from a plain A01 one in the output) — and all three call the shared `_drop_evn2_period_start_fallback(encounter, pv1)` after `build_encounter_core`, since `build_encounter_core`'s EVN-2 fallback for `period.start` is correct for admission-lifecycle triggers (EVN-2 genuinely is the encounter's own event time for A01/A02/A04/A05/A08) but wrong for these three: EVN-2 on a cancel-trigger message is when the *cancel notification itself* was recorded, not any real admission start. Getting this wrong (only guarding `period.end` against the same hazard, which `AdtA13Mapper` did at first, while leaving `period.start` unguarded) silently mislabels the cancel-event's own timestamp as an admission start — this exact bug shipped once and was caught by code review, not the original test suite (see `tests/test_adt_a11_mapping.py::test_cancel_admit_without_pv1_44_does_not_leak_evn2_as_period_start` and the equivalent A13/A38 tests). `AdtA38Mapper` is A05's cancel-pattern sibling the same way `AdtA11Mapper` is A01/A04's and `AdtA13Mapper` is A03's — unlike A13, it does *not* map PV1-36 discharge disposition, since a pre-admission was never discharged.
- `app/mappings/siu.py`: `BaseSiuMapper.build_appointment(sch, tq1_segments, nte_segments, ais_segments, participants) -> Appointment`. `participants` is built once by `to_bundle` (via `_build_participants`, which also returns the Practitioner/Location/Device resources materialized for AIP/AIL/AIG so `to_bundle` can add them to the Bundle) since it's identical regardless of trigger event - only `status`/`start`/`end` vary, so those are the only things each subclass supplies. Two private base classes each expose a `status` class attribute overridden per subclass, rather than hardcoding status in the shared build method: `_BookedSiuMapper` (`status="booked"` by default) resolves timing via `resolve_appointment_timing()` and requires a start time (raises `MappingError` otherwise) — `SiuS12Mapper`/`SiuS13Mapper`/`SiuS14Mapper` inherit it unchanged (deliberately identical apart from which `trigger_event` string ends up in the `Appointment.extension` marker, since this converter has no persisted state to make a "new booking" vs. "reschedule" vs. "modify" distinguishable in the output), while `SiuS26Mapper` (patient no-show) overrides `status="noshow"` - a no-show necessarily refers to a specific already-scheduled time, so it shares the timing-required behavior. `_UntimedSiuMapper` (`status="cancelled"` by default) resolves timing but does not require it — `SiuS15Mapper` inherits it unchanged, while `SiuS17Mapper` (delete) overrides `status="entered-in-error"`: the HL7 standard draws an explicit, deliberate distinction between S17 "removing an error" and S15 "cancelling a valid request," and FHIR's `entered-in-error` ("should not have been part of the record") is a closer semantic match to S17 than reusing S15's `cancelled`.
- `app/mappings/oru.py`: **not** trigger-polymorphic — `BaseOruMapper` handles R01/R30/R31/R32/R40 identically (they differ only in upstream ordering-workflow semantics this converter doesn't model — R30 places a new order, R31 searches for an existing one, R32 assumes one was already pre-ordered, R40 places none — none of which this stateless converter models), so the five concrete classes are just `trigger_event = "R01"/"R30"/"R31"/"R32"/"R40"` with no overrides. This is the degenerate case of the same pattern, not a different one.
- `app/mappings/mdm.py`: same degenerate, non-trigger-polymorphic pattern as `oru.py` — `BaseMdmMapper` handles T02/T04/T06/T08/T10/T11 identically, since the official v2-to-FHIR IG ships exactly one MDM ConceptMap (`MDM_T02` → `Bundle`) and treats the TXA segment map as trigger-agnostic. The six concrete classes are just `trigger_event = "T02"/"T04"/"T06"/"T08"/"T10"/"T11"` with no overrides. T10 (replace) and T11 (cancel) look like they should warrant trigger-specific `DocumentReference.status` handling the way A11/A13/A38 deliberately override ADT's cancel-trigger status — but here the decision went the other way: the segment-txa-to-documentreference ConceptMap has no trigger-event-specific guidance at all, and TXA-13 (the field that would carry T10's "replaces" pointer) has no mapped FHIR target in it, so — per an explicit instruction to treat the IG as the authoritative source when weighing this exact tradeoff — status stays 100% TXA-19-driven and TXA-13 stays unmapped for every trigger, same as T02/T04/T06. See the module docstring for the full reasoning.

When adding a new trigger event for an already-supported message type, add a subclass in that type's module rather than a new file — only add a new file for a genuinely different HL7 message type.

**Extension point for repeating leader/member segment groups:** `app/hl7/parser.py::group_segments_by_leader(message, leader_name, member_names)` walks a message's segments in order and associates each run of member segments with the leader segment that immediately precedes it (e.g. ORU's `OBR` leading its `OBX` results). Use this - not the flat `optional_segments()` - whenever which-segment-belongs-to-which matters for correctness, not just presence/absence. It's deliberately generic (any leader/member name pair), so a future message type with a similar repeating-group shape (e.g. an order message's `ORC` leading `OBR`) can reuse it directly rather than re-deriving the same positional-walk logic.

**Synthetic message generation (`app/generators/`)** is the mirror image of `app/mappings/` - HL7 text out instead of in. `app/generators/registry.py::generate(message_type, trigger_event, seed=None) -> str` looks up a plain generator function per `(message_type, trigger_event)` (not a class hierarchy like the mappers - generation doesn't need per-trigger polymorphism, just per-trigger field-population rules) and threads a `random.Random(seed)` instance through it; same seed always reproduces the same message byte-for-byte, `seed=None` gives real randomness for the UI's "fresh one" use case. `("CDA", "CCD"): (app.cda.generator.generate_ccd, "CDA^CCD - Continuity of Care Document")` is registered in this exact same flat dict (see the C-CDA subsection below) - "CCD" stands in for `trigger_event` even though C-CDA has no real trigger-event concept, chosen because it costs zero UI/route changes versus a genuine third registry axis for one entry. `app/generators/base.py` holds shared primitives every generator uses: `maybe(rng, p=0.6) -> bool` (the one place "should this optional field be included" is decided), small pools of obviously-synthetic sample data (names/addresses/reason codes/lab tests/etc.), `generate_msh_segment()`, `generate_pid_segment()` (used by all three message types' generators), `build_minimal_pv1_fields(rng, patient_class) -> dict` (used by `adt.py`, `oru.py`, and `mdm.py` - the message types whose generators build a PV1; this exists because ADT and ORU independently duplicated the same PV1-1/2/7/19 field logic once, and had already silently diverged - ORU's copy was missing the physician field ADT's had - before being unified here), and shape-level helpers (`random_location_field()` for PL-shaped fields, `random_physician_xcn()` for XCN-shaped fields) reused across `adt.py`/`siu.py`/`oru.py`/`mdm.py` as needed.

**Generated data must be valid in meaning, not just in format.** Three defects were reported from real generated output and fixed together: `random_datetime_near_now`/`random_time_range` defaulted to `max_days=30`, so admissions, onsets, documents and claims were routinely dated in the future - the default is now past-only (`min_days=-30, max_days=0`), and the two places a future date is correct (a scheduled SIU appointment, a C-CDA Planned Observation in Plan of Treatment) pass a positive `max_days` explicitly so the intent reads at the call site. The helper also *added* a random time-of-day on top of the day offset, so even an explicit `max_days=0` landed up to 23h59m ahead - which is why every format still produced tomorrow's date; it now clamps, and `random_time_range` shifts the whole range rather than shortening it so a caller's duration rules (discharge strictly after admit) still hold. Separately, ORU drew `OBX-5` from 70-130% of its own `OBX-7` reference range and drew `OBX-8`'s abnormal flag independently of the value, so a sample could state a haemoglobin of 18.4 against a stated 12.0-16.0 range, or flag an in-range result `"H"`; the value is now drawn within the range and the flag agrees with it.

Required/optional policy (the field-level randomization rule the generators implement): a field is treated as required - always populated - when either the HL7 standard calls it required, *or* this app's own mapper needs it to avoid raising `MappingError`/`MissingSegmentError` for that specific trigger (e.g. `PV1-6` prior location is required only for A02; `PV1-44`/`PV1-45` are both required only for A03, since `AdtA03Mapper` raises without a discharge time; SIU S12/S13/S14 always get resolvable timing - via a randomized ~75/25 split between `TQ1` and legacy `SCH-11`, deliberately exercising both of `resolve_appointment_timing()`'s paths - while S15 leaves timing fully optional). Every other field is optional at a fixed ~50-60% inclusion rate via `maybe()`. The one deliberate exception is A08's discharge time, held at exactly 50% *by design* (not just "the general optional rate") specifically to exercise `AdtA08Mapper`'s finished-vs-in-progress status inference in both directions. This policy guarantees every generated message converts successfully through the existing pipeline - the generator's test suite leans on this heavily (see Testing below). **When both admit and discharge are generated, they must come from the same `random_time_range()` call**, not two independent ones - `generate_adt_a03`/`generate_adt_a13` already did this correctly, but `generate_adt_a08` originally drew them from two separate calls, which could (and, once `app/validation/` existed to notice, did) produce a discharge time before the admit time. Fixed once `app/validation/adt.py`'s `discharge-before-admit` rule caught it in the generator's own fuzz output - not a validation-rule bug, a real pre-existing generator gap the validator was the first thing to actually notice.

**Extension point for validation rules (`app/validation/`)** is a third, independent pillar alongside `app/mappings/` (HL7→FHIR) and `app/generators/` (FHIR-shaped→HL7 text) - it takes a parsed message and returns a `ValidationReport` (`app/validation/models.py`: `ValidationFinding{severity: error|warning|info, rule_id, segment, field, message}` + `ValidationReport{message_type, trigger_event, is_valid, findings}`), never a Bundle, and never raises for anything a real HL7 sender could plausibly produce. Same per-message-type module shape as mappings/generators, but two deliberate structural differences:
- `app/validation/registry.py` is keyed by **message type only** (`{message_type: validate_fn}`), not `(message_type, trigger_event)` like `app/mappings/registry.py` - rule *sets* are type-scoped; trigger-specific nuance (e.g. `adt.py`'s A02-only prior-location rule) lives inside the relevant rule function via an `if trigger_event == "...":` check, not via a second registry dimension.
- Each module (`generic.py` for every message type, `adt.py`/`siu.py`/`oru.py`/`mdm.py` per type) exposes one `validate(message, trigger_event) -> list[ValidationFinding]`, but internally composes small private `_rule_*` functions by concatenation rather than one large function - each rule is independently testable and a future contributor knows exactly where to add the next one, mirroring `build_encounter_core`'s small-named-step style more than a single do-everything function.

`app/validation/engine.py::validate_message()` runs `generic.validate()`, then the type-specific validator if one is registered, then `_check_convertibility()` - the load-bearing design decision here: rather than re-deriving a second, parallel "which segments does trigger X require" table (a real duplication-and-drift risk this project has already been bitten by once, see `build_minimal_pv1_fields`/`build_minimal_encounter` above), it actually calls `get_mapper(...).to_bundle(message)` and turns whatever it raises into a finding, so structural completeness can never drift from what the mappers actually enforce. Two gotchas that shipped wrong on the first pass and were caught by manual smoke-testing before the test suite was even written, not by code review this time:
- **`MappingError` is raised from two different places with two different meanings**, and both must be handled, not just one: `get_mapper(message_type, trigger_event)` raises it when the type/trigger isn't registered at all (→ an `info` finding, "no converter registered" - not a defect in the message), but several mappers (`AdtA03Mapper`, `SiuS12Mapper`/`S13`/`S14`/`S26`) *also* raise `MappingError` directly from inside `to_bundle()` for business-rule violations (missing discharge time, unresolvable appointment timing) once a mapper instance was already successfully obtained. The first version of `_check_convertibility()` only caught `(MissingSegmentError, ValidationError)` around the `to_bundle()` call, so every one of those legitimate, well-documented mapper errors fell through into the bare `except Exception` safety net and got logged as an "unexpected internal error" - technically not a crash, but a misleading severity/message for a completely ordinary outcome. Fixed by adding `MappingError` to that inner except tuple too.
- **PID-7 (date of birth) is very commonly date-only** (`YYYYMMDD`, 8 digits, no time component) - `app/fhir_models/builders.py::parse_hl7_datetime` requires 12+ digits (it's built for timestamps, not bare dates) and returns `None` for an 8-digit value, which `app/validation/common.py::parse_comparable_datetime` originally treated as "unparseable," flagging every ordinary date-only birth date as a warning. Fixed by falling back to `parse_hl7_date` (exactly 8 digits) and treating a date-only result as UTC midnight - this is also why `parse_comparable_datetime` exists as its own helper rather than every rule calling `parse_hl7_datetime` directly, so this fallback only had to be written once.

A follow-up multi-agent code review (adversarially verified - findings had to be reproduced by actually running code, not just argued for) caught four more, real ones the manual smoke-testing above missed:
- **The date-only-as-midnight fallback above has its own downstream consequence**: comparing a date-only value against a same-calendar-day *timestamped* value (e.g. `PV1-44` admit `...T08:30:00Z` vs. a date-only `PV1-45` discharge on the same day) made the synthetic midnight sort before the real time-of-day, producing a false `adt.discharge-before-admit`/`adt.admit-before-birth` **error** on a perfectly plausible message. `app/validation/common.py::has_time_precision()` now exposes whether a value actually carried a time component; `app/validation/adt.py::_is_before()` falls back to date-granularity comparison (`.date() <`) whenever either side lacks it, and only compares exact datetimes when both have full precision.
- **`parse_hl7_date`/`parse_hl7_datetime` only validate digit *shape*, not calendar validity** - a numerically well-formed but nonsensical date like `20260231` (Feb 31) made `datetime.fromisoformat()` raise an uncaught `ValueError` from inside `parse_comparable_datetime`'s date-only fallback branch (its sibling `_parse_fhir_datetime`, for the timestamp path, already guarded this correctly). Since this ran during the *generic rules* phase - before `_check_convertibility()`'s own `except Exception` safety net ever executes - it wasn't caught anywhere, and turned `/api/validate` into a raw HTTP 500 on exactly the kind of fat-fingered input a validator exists to catch. Fixed by wrapping that branch in the same `try/except ValueError` its sibling already had.
- **A registered *type* with an unmapped *trigger*** (e.g. `ADT^A38`, one of the "remaining trigger events" this file lists) already runs its type-specific rules before `_check_convertibility()` ever discovers the trigger isn't mapped - so the resulting `engine.unsupported-message-type` finding's message text (`"...only generic checks were run"`) was flatly false whenever type-specific findings sat right next to it in the same report. `_check_convertibility()` now takes a `ran_type_specific_checks` flag from `validate_message()` and adjusts the wording accordingly.
- **Two other, lower-stakes issues in the same pass**: `app/validation/oru.py::_resolve_reference_range` accepted a syntactically valid but semantically transposed range like `"100-70"` (low > high) as if it were normal, producing an inverted (wrong) in/out-of-range determination instead of being skipped like every other malformed `OBX-7` shape - fixed by rejecting `low > high` the same way. `app/validation/adt.py::_rule_a02_missing_prior_location` compared `trigger_event` with a bare `== "A02"`, unlike every other type/trigger dispatch point in this codebase (`get_mapper`, `get_type_validator`), which normalize with `.strip().upper()` first - a lowercase `ADT^a02` silently skipped the check. Both `adt.py` and `mdm.py`'s "is this in the future" rules were also independently hand-rolling the exact logic `common.py::not_in_future()` already existed to centralize (dead code, not a behavior bug) - now both call it.

`app/validation/adt.py`'s `_is_before(earlier_raw, earlier_dt, later_raw, later_dt)` (the date-only-aware ordering comparison behind `discharge-before-admit`/`admit-before-birth`) was promoted to `app/validation/common.py::is_before()` (public) once `app/cda/validation.py` needed the exact same logic for its own encounter/problem-ordering rules - the same "extract once a second real consumer exists" pattern already used for `build_minimal_pv1_fields`/`build_minimal_encounter` above, not a speculative abstraction.

Route/API shape (`app/routes/convert.py`): `_run_validation()`/`ValidationResult` mirror `_run_conversion()`/`ConvertResult` exactly, but `/api/validate` **always returns HTTP 200** for a report, even when `is_valid: false` - a report concluding "this message has an error" is itself a *successful* analysis, not an API-level failure. Only the two exceptions `validate_hl7()` can still raise (`Hl7ParseError`, and `MissingSegmentError` specifically for MSH itself being absent - there's nothing left to validate without it) map to a 4xx, via a narrower `VALIDATION_ERROR_STATUS` table that deliberately excludes `MappingError`/`pydantic.ValidationError` (validate_hl7 never raises either).

**Error taxonomy:** three custom exceptions in `app/hl7/errors.py` — `Hl7ParseError` (unparseable text), `MissingSegmentError` (required segment absent), `MappingError` (no mapper registered for a message type) — plus `pydantic.ValidationError` from FHIR resource construction. `app/cda/errors.py` adds exactly one more, `CdaParseError` (unparseable XML, or a well-formed document whose root isn't `ClinicalDocument`) - `MissingSegmentError`/`MappingError` are reused as-is for C-CDA rather than duplicated, since their meaning ("a required structural piece is absent" / "no builder registered for this recognized-but-unhandled type") is format-agnostic. `app/edi/errors.py` adds exactly one more the same way, `EdiParseError` (a genuinely broken envelope - missing/malformed ISA, unmatched ST/SE/GS/GE boundary segments - see the EDI subsection's parser notes for exactly which shapes raise it versus which become validation findings instead), again reusing `MissingSegmentError`/`MappingError` as-is. `app/routes/errors.py` holds the shared `ERROR_STATUS`/`VALIDATION_ERROR_STATUS` exception-to-(category, http_status) tables and the `resolve_raw_text()` file-upload helper - extracted from `app/routes/convert.py` once `app/routes/data_specification.py` became a second real consumer of both, the same "extract on second real consumer" discipline this project applies everywhere (`app/routes/dropdowns.py::grouped_supported_types()` was extracted the identical way, for the sample-type `<optgroup>` dropdown both pages now share). Every route translates its own outcome to an HTTP status + category label via one of these shared tables, and both the JSON API and the server-rendered form path for each feature route through the same `_run_*()` helper so error handling only lives in one place per feature. The UI never shows a raw traceback.

**Static asset cache-busting (`app/routes/static_assets.py::static_url`)** - a real, reproduced bug, not a hypothetical one: `app/main.py` mounts `/static` via a bare `StaticFiles(...)` with no cache-control configuration, and every template referenced `style.css`/`app.js` by a plain, unversioned path - a browser's own default HTTP caching heuristics can keep serving a stale copy across ordinary page reloads with no visible sign anything is wrong (every server response is correct; only the already-cached JS/CSS is stale). This shipped once and was reported directly: a UI fix verified correct in a fresh Puppeteer browser context (no prior cache) was reported broken in the user's own already-open browser session - the exact symptom a stale-cached crosswalk script produces (the crosswalk table rendering with the *previous* version's column layout). Fixed with a Jinja2 global, `static_url(filename) -> f"/static/{filename}?v={mtime}"`, registered on both of this app's independent `Jinja2Templates` instances (`app/routes/convert.py` and `app/routes/data_specification.py` each construct their own rather than sharing one) since `base.html`'s own `<link>` tag is rendered through whichever one served the current page. No build step or manual version bump needed - the query string is recomputed from the file's real on-disk mtime on every render, so any future edit to a static asset is automatically cache-busted the moment it's saved.

- **Data Specification** (`app/provenance/`) - README's own sixth named pillar, and a genuinely new kind of capability: real, dynamic field-level provenance from an actual conversion instance (not static reference documentation, and not FHIR's own resource-level `Provenance`, which this app has never built anywhere and which has no established field-granular extension pattern to lean on - a bespoke `CrosswalkReport` instead, the same "this pillar gets its own report shape" precedent `app/validation/models.py::ValidationReport` already set). Reachable via the crosswalk on the app's single page and `/api/data-specification`. **It shipped as its own separate page (`/data-specification`, behind a `<nav>` in `base.html`) and was later merged back onto `index.html`** - converting a message and seeing which source field produced each FHIR field turned out to be one action, not two, so the crosswalk simply *is* this app's Message → FHIR view now (the old URL survives as a 308 redirect for existing links; `POST /convert` was retired with it, since the no-JS "Convert to FHIR" button posts to `/data-specification`). The two routers still render that one template, so `app/routes/page_context.py::default_page_context()` supplies the union of both their context keys - Jinja2 raises on iterating an undefined, so every key has to be present regardless of which router served the render. The separate-page era's own rationale (sidestepping the `hidden`-toggle bug class by not sharing a template) did not survive the merge, so the panes are back under that hazard and coordinate explicitly instead: `showValidationResult`/`showCrosswalk`/`showError` each hide the other two panes, and `showCrosswalk` toggles `useBundleBtn.hidden` itself rather than trusting the server-rendered initial state.
  - **Why field-level provenance has no shortcut, confirmed by research before writing any code**: the *read* side is a narrow, real choke point (all field/element access for every message type funnels through ~15-20 primitives across `app/hl7/parser.py`/`app/cda/parser.py`/`app/edi/parser.py`), but the *write* side has none at all - FHIR resources are built via a hybrid of constructor kwargs and ~220+ individually-scattered `if value: obj.attr = value` statements across every mapper function/class in `app/mappings/*.py`/`app/cda/*.py`/`app/edi/*.py`, with zero existing wrapper, and the FHIR JSON path a fact needs (e.g. `Bundle.entry[2].resource.name[0].family`) requires knowing both the field name and the resource's eventual `Bundle.entry[N]` index - neither recoverable via a generic interception, and post-hoc value-matching against a serialized Bundle is unreliable (duplicate values are common). Real provenance genuinely requires touching essentially every mapper file, mechanically uniform in shape but large in count - an explicitly **multi-slice undertaking**, the same "one thing per slice" discipline this app's every other pillar was built with (ADT^A01 before A02-A38; CCD's Problems-only slice before Medications/Allergies/etc.; 270/271 before 276/277/278/835/837). The first phase shipped the full architecture plus the first, fully-proven slice - the whole ADT trigger family (all 9 triggers, not just A01 - confirmed cheap to extend once `build_encounter_core` is instrumented, since every ADT trigger already shares it); SIU (all 6 triggers) followed as the second slice, the first proof the architecture generalizes past `Encounter` to a genuinely different resource shape with materialized participant resources (see its own bullet below) - with every other message type/format left as a disclosed, honestly-reported follow-up, not silently missing.
  - **`app/provenance/decisions.py`** — the reviewable **mapping-decision register**: every value a conversion *inferred*, and every source component it *dropped*, listed above the crosswalk table so a reviewer can accept or reject each one. **Computed, never hand-declared** - inferred decisions come straight from `derivation="inferred"` entries; dropped ones from diffing the components the raw message populates against the components the recorder saw. Review state lives in the browser and applies only to the conversion at hand (nothing is stored server-side; the browser replays `rejected_decision_ids` on each request). Rejecting an inferred value re-runs the conversion without it, expressed conformantly per `REJECTION_STRATEGY` - either a null-flavour code the field's own value set already has, or dropping the value and carrying FHIR's `data-absent-reason` extension on the primitive. Every row of that table was checked against the published R4 value set by hand, since `fhir.resources` does not validate value sets (it accepts `intent="null"`). Rejecting a *drop* is recorded but cannot be applied - it flags a mapper gap, not something conversion can supply.
    - **Coverage is per format, and the split is structural.** The *inferred* half works for all three, straight off `derivation="inferred"`. The *dropped* half needs the raw source to diff against, so it is written per format: HL7v2 splits on `|`/`~`/`^`, X12 on the ISA's own self-declared delimiters. **C-CDA has no positional coordinate at all**, so its scan works differently: a recorded XML location is a relative path composed while walking, with no inverse, so the scan enumerates every leaf value (each attribute, and the text of each childless element) and matches them against the source *spans* `cda_locator.py` already resolves for the correlated-highlighting view. Matching by span rather than value is load-bearing - most values are transformed on the way through (a date reformatted, an OID rewritten as `urn:oid:`, `use="HP"` mapped to `home`), and an initial value-comparison build reported 46 of a 96-leaf CCD as lost. Value comparison survives only as a fallback for the few locations the locator cannot resolve (a bare `<id>`). Three classes of noise are excluded, each stated: XML/RIM structure (`classCode`/`moodCode`/`typeCode`/`xmlns`/`xsi:type`/`nullFlavor`), elements whose whole subtree is plumbing (`templateId`, `typeId`, `realmCode`), and the conditional qualifiers above; a section's narrative reports once per section rather than per paragraph, since C-CDA requires it to restate the entries. **Drops now cite what the IG actually says, via `app/provenance/cda_ig_verdicts.py`.** The IG's published per-resource tables (`mappings/CF/*.csv`) carry an `Approach` column that is the authoritative signal: `source value`/`transform` define a FHIR target, `not supported by target` states there is none. An element with *no row at all* is a real answer too, per the IG's own mappingGuidance - "If you have data in an input artifact that is defined in the source specification and for which no map is specified here, that means that this team did not find a target for which we could build consensus." That single quote settles the whole document header: the IG publishes no header or Encounter table at all (confirmed by reading `CF-notes.md` and `mappingGuidance.md` directly, and by listing the CSV directory), so `ClinicalDocument/code`/`title`/`languageCode`/`confidentialityCode` and a section's own `code`/`title` are "no map specified", not gaps. **Keys are matched longest-suffix-first because the same tag means different things at different depths** - a Problem Concern Act's own `id` is "not supported by target" while the Problem Observation's `id` inside it maps to `Condition.identifier` as a source value, which this app does not build: a **real gap**, surfaced with a `GAP:` summary prefix so a reviewer sees the one verdict they have to act on. A shape whose table has not been read yet keeps the honest `DROP_NOT_YET_CHECKED` citation, so "unchecked" means unchecked. **Two GAP verdicts were later withdrawn, and the reason is the durable lesson**: an element *shape* cannot always identify which section it came from, and a key that over-reaches is worse than no key. `entryRelationship/observation/id` was giving an Allergy Reaction Observation the Problem Observation's verdict (the IG maps a reaction's own id to `.reaction.id`), and `entryRelationship/act/code` was giving a Comment Activity the Instruction act's - both nested at the same depth as the element each verdict was written for. Telling them apart needs the templateId, which the drop register does not carry, so neither asserts a verdict now. This is precisely what the confirm-against-the-Bundle rule exists to catch; it slipped past because the check was applied to the verdict *source* rather than to each *match*. Reading the narrative pages (CF-encounters/CF-vitals/CF-results, which publish no CSV) also found that both Vitals and Results map `/id` to `.identifier` for organizer and observation alike - now built, matching what every other section already did. **All three formats now report zero gaps and nothing unchecked.** **Six of the original gaps were closed by implementing them**: the IG maps each entry's own `<id>` as a source value to that resource's `.identifier`, and `Procedure` built it while `Condition`, `MedicationRequest`, `Immunization` and `AllergyIntolerance` did not - a parity gap *within* C-CDA that the register found. All four now reuse `build_identifiers` the way `procedures.py` already did. **A GAP verdict is only assigned after confirming the target really is absent from the converted Bundle** - the IG saying a target exists is not evidence this app fails to build it. That check immediately paid for itself: `Procedure/id` looked like a textbook gap (the IG maps it as a source value to `Procedure.identifier`) but `Procedure.identifier` is in fact built, and the drop was an artifact of `cda_locator` not resolving a childless `<id>` element. The Problem Observation's own `id` failed the same check for real - `Condition.identifier` is genuinely `None` - so that one is a true gap. The locator artifact was fixed at the source by recording an identifier against the attribute its value came from (`id[0]/@extension`) rather than the bare element. **Rows are grouped, because 31-37 per document is not readable.** Two mechanical passes, no judgement involved: a *wholly* unmapped element reports once naming the element and listing what it carried, rather than one row per attribute (`<code>` was three rows saying one thing - the same rule the HL7v2 half already applies to an untouched field); then identical shapes within a document collapse into one row carrying the occurrence count and the distinct values (a Vital Signs organizer with six readings dropped six identical `statusCode` facts). A partially-read element still reports its specific unread parts, and a single occurrence keeps its exact indexed path, so precision is only given up where there was nothing to distinguish. Measured effect: it more than halved the rows across the C-CDA fixtures. **Its accuracy is bounded by how completely the CDA mappers record, not by its own tables** - a field read but never recorded looks dropped. Two more such gaps the scan then exposed, both fixed in the mappers rather than excluded: several call sites recorded `.coding[0].code` without `.coding[0].display`, and `build_quantity_from_pq` reads `@unit` that several callers never recorded, so `@displayName` and `@unit` reported as lost while the mapper had carried them through. `app/cda/common.py::record_coding`/`record_quantity` now record each pair together so they cannot drift apart again (used by `vitals.py`'s interpretation/method/bodySite, `procedures.py`'s targetSiteCode, `medications.py`'s routeCode/doseQuantity); verified by diffing the whole drop set: exactly the four targeted false positives gone, nothing newly reported. A follow-up pass extended the same two helpers to `results.py` (interpretation/method/bodySite, specimen collection quantity), `immunizations.py` (routeCode/doseQuantity), `family_history.py` (the relative's own sex display) and `vitals.py`'s BP/pulse-ox components, with nothing newly reported at any step. **Scope was decided by measurement, not by the obvious refactor**: `build_codeable_concept_from_cd` has 45 call sites and threading recorder params through all of them looked like the tidy fix, but checking each dropped `@displayName`/`@unit` against the actual Bundle showed only ~14 were false positives - the other 94 are genuine, since a document/section/template code's display really is not carried anywhere. Most of that refactor would have been churn. **Two cautions the pass produced**: the Bundle-substring check is itself a heuristic and mis-flagged the patient's own `administrativeGenderCode/@displayName` by matching a *family member's* identical `"Male"`, so each candidate needs confirming against the real path before being called a bug; and recording that family member's display then suppressed the patient's genuine drop through the value fallback, two leaves with identical text being indistinguishable to it - the disclosed weakness failing in its intended under-reporting direction. Still reported and still genuine-looking: an IVL_PQ range's own low/high `@unit`, the specimen collection procedure's targetSiteCode display, Social History's `value/@displayName`, and a family history age `@unit`. Building it surfaced exactly that: `build_identifiers` set `Identifier.system` from `@root` but recorded only `.value`, so every CDA `id/@root` reported as lost; fixed in `app/cda/common.py` (the same gap `PID-3.4` had on the HL7v2 side) rather than papered over with an exclusion. `compute_decisions(report, raw_text)` owns that branch so no caller re-derives it.
    - **Both formats repeat whole segments, and the scan is keyed per occurrence for both.** HL7v2 repeats segments as freely as X12 does - an ORU carries one OBX per result, a SIU one AIP per participant, any segment can be trailed by several NTEs - and the HL7v2 scan originally missed this, keying only `(segment, field, repetition)` so the third OBX overwrote the first two and a component dropped by an earlier segment was invisible. Both scans now key by `(segment, occurrence, ...)`, and a location carries the occurrence before the dash (`OBX[2]-3.3`, `N4[1]-2`) distinct from a *field* repetition after the field number (`PID-3[1].5`). **Consumption, though, is aggregated by element in both formats, and that is a disclosed trade:** neither `hl7_location()` nor `edi_location()` records which occurrence a fact came from, so `"OBX-5"` cannot say which OBX and `"NM1-3"` cannot be attributed to one of an 837P's six NM1 loops. An element read on any occurrence counts as read on all of them. Attributing per occurrence would need `highlighting.py`'s occurrence-claiming resolver, and getting it wrong reports five of six NM1 loops as wholly dropped - so this errs toward under-reporting rather than crying wolf. What it can miss: element E read on one occurrence and populated-but-unmapped on another.
    - **Every exclusion is conditional, because "this is never data" was wrong.** A qualifier (`CLM05-2`, `SV1-01.1`, `NM1-08`, `TOO-01`, `OBX-2`) is consumed only when the element it qualifies was itself mapped - 837I never reads `CLM05`, so its `CLM05-2` qualifies nothing, yet it was suppressed while the `CLM05-1` it supposedly qualified was reported dropped. `JOINED_FIELDS` is conditional the same way: it exists so a mapper that collapses several components into one value does not look like it dropped the rest, so where *nothing* read the field it collapsed nothing (MDM never reads `PV1-3`, and suppressing its point-of-care and room hid real drops). Only pure structure stays unconditional - Set IDs, `EVN-1`, the `HL` tree, `BHT` structure codes, `LX01`, and the `NM1`/`N1` entity codes that say which loop a segment is. C-CDA's own scan follows the same rule - `CDA_QUALIFIER_OF` makes `@codeSystem` consumed only when the `@code` beside it was read.
    - Three hand-maintained tables, each kept tiny because nothing in the recorded data can derive them: `JOINED_FIELDS` (a mapper that collapses several components into one recorded value, so the diff doesn't report the others as lost), `UNMAPPABLE_FIELDS` (Set IDs, `EVN-1`, and control fields like `OBX-2` that are fully consumed selecting where another field's value goes - noise, not loss), and `REJECTION_STRATEGY` above. X12 splits the same way: `UNMAPPABLE_EDI_ELEMENTS` for pure structure and `EDI_QUALIFIER_OF` for the conditional qualifiers, mapping each to the element it qualifies (`HI`'s diagnosis qualifier repeats across `HI01`/`HI02`/... so its key uses `None` for "any element"). `HL7_QUALIFIER_OF` is the HL7v2 mirror. Envelope segments (ISA/GS/ST/SE/GE/IEA) are skipped wholesale. Anything not listed is reported, so the default stays "disclose it".
    - **Three defects a code review found and fixed, all reproduced before being believed.** A repeating field collapsed every repetition onto one location and so one decision id (`PID-3[0].5` and `PID-3[1].5` both became `dropped:PID-3.5`) - `apply_rejections` keys a dict by id, so one decision became unreachable and the UI shared one review state across both rows; ids now carry a `[n]` suffix from the second repetition on. `scan_populated_components` skipped any field with no `^` as "nothing to drop", which made every *non-composite* field structurally invisible no matter what it carried (`PV1-10` Hospital Service was absent from both the Bundle and the register) - fatal to a register whose whole value is completeness. And a value joined from several segments is recorded against a disclosed marker (`"OBX-5 (xN segments)"` for an MDM document body) that `parse_hl7_location` cannot parse, so the register reported the document text itself as dropped - `_mapped_components` now falls back to the leading `SEG-N`.

  - **`app/provenance/models.py`** — `ProvenanceEntry` (`source_format`, `fhir_path`, `derivation: "direct"|"inferred"`, `source_location`, `reason`, `source_value`, `value`) and `CrosswalkReport` (echoes back `message_type`/`trigger_event`/`source_format`, a flat `entries` list, plus `unsupported`/`unsupported_reason` for honest partial coverage - mirrors `ValidationReport`'s own top-level shape). `derivation="inferred"` entries (no single source field to point at - a trigger-event-driven literal, a value inferred from another field's mere *presence* rather than its content, internal UUID wiring) are shown with a `reason` explaining where the value actually came from **instead of being silently omitted** - a deliberate choice, confirmed with the user directly, so the crosswalk stays a complete picture of the resource rather than only showing the directly-copied parts.
  - **`app/provenance/recorder.py`** — `ProvenanceRecorder`: accumulates facts keyed by `(resource_id, relative_path)`, **last-write-wins, not append-only** - a fact represents "the current true source of the field's current value," not a full write history. This matters for real, existing mapper logic that writes a field once and then conditionally corrects it: `app/mappings/adt.py::_drop_evn2_period_start_fallback` (called by the three cancel-trigger ADT mappers, A11/A13/A38) resets `Encounter.period.start` after `build_encounter_core` already recorded it from a PV1-44-or-EVN-2 fallback, since EVN-2 means something different on a cancel-trigger message (see that function's own long-established docstring) - re-recording at the same path overwrites the stale fact instead of leaving two contradictory entries in the crosswalk for one real field. `forget()`/`forget_prefix()` are the same idea for a field a later correction removes entirely (the same function also clears `Encounter.period` altogether when nothing is left in it after the correction). Every instrumented mapper function takes `recorder: ProvenanceRecorder | None = None` as a trailing parameter, defaulting to `None` everywhere - every existing caller (normal conversion, `validate_message`'s own convertibility check, generation, transform) is completely unaffected unless it explicitly opts in, verified by a dedicated regression test (see Testing below).
  - **`app/provenance/resolver.py`** — `resolve_bundle_paths(bundle, recorder)`: the second half of the two-phase design. A resource's eventual `Bundle.entry[N]` index isn't known until the Bundle is fully assembled, so facts are recorded resource-id-relative during mapping and resolved into fully Bundle-qualified paths in one pass afterward. **Bundle-level fields need a real special case, not an afterthought**: `app/mappings/common.py::assemble_bundle` sets `bundle.identifier`/`bundle.timestamp` from MSH-10/MSH-7 on the Bundle resource itself, not on any entry - a fact recorded against `resource_id == bundle.id` resolves to `f"Bundle.{relative_path}"` with no `entry[N].resource.` prefix, since the Bundle isn't one of its own entries. A fact recorded against a `resource_id` that isn't found in the Bundle is skipped, never crashes - defensive, not currently reachable, but cheap insurance for a future mapper shape.
  - **`app/provenance/location.py`** — `hl7_location(segment_id, field, *, repetition=None, component=None) -> str` (e.g. `hl7_location("PID", 5, repetition=0, component=1)` → `"PID-5[0].1"`), the one source-location formatter that exists yet. **C-CDA's own equivalent needs real design work, not yet done, flagged so it isn't rediscovered as a surprise**: stdlib `xml.etree.ElementTree` (this app's own parsing choice) has no parent pointers, so an XPath-shaped location has to be composed *forward* while walking the document tree - each `find_child`/`find_all` call extending a path string it's handed - not reconstructed backward from a bare `Element` after the fact, confirmed during this phase's own planning research.
  - **`app/provenance/dispatch.py`** — `convert_with_provenance(raw_text) -> tuple[Bundle, CrosswalkReport]`, the pillar's own top-level entry point. **Deliberately does not thread a `recorder` parameter through `app/pipeline.py`/`app/cda/pipeline.py`/`app/edi/pipeline.py`** - that would add a dead, no-op parameter to two format pipelines whose own builder ABCs don't do anything with it yet this phase, widening the diff into files that don't need to change. Instead reuses `app.pipeline`'s own `is_x12`/`is_xml` sniffing directly (the real, stable functions, not re-derived) and, for HL7v2, does the same tiny parse-then-dispatch `app/hl7/pipeline.py::convert_hl7_to_bundle` already does, with a `ProvenanceRecorder` threaded into the one call that needs it. EDI/CDA input converts normally (identical output to `convert_to_bundle`) but always reports `unsupported=True` - neither format has any field-level instrumentation yet. **A real bug caught before shipping, not assumed away**: whether an HL7v2 message type is "supported" is checked against an explicit `_INSTRUMENTED_MESSAGE_TYPES` set (now `{"ADT", "SIU"}`), **not** inferred from "did the recorder produce any facts at all" - `OruR01Mapper`/`MdmT02Mapper`/etc.'s own `to_bundle()` already accept a `recorder` and thread it into the `build_patient`/`assemble_bundle` calls they share with ADT/SIU (mechanical signature-only changes, so `mapper.to_bundle(message, recorder=recorder)` never raises `TypeError` for a not-yet-instrumented type), so an ORU/MDM message genuinely does produce a few real PID/MSH facts despite having zero instrumentation for its own type-specific resource (DiagnosticReport/DocumentReference) - an empty-entries heuristic reported these types as fully supported until caught by manually exercising the actual API response, not by any automated test. `tests/test_data_specification_routes.py::test_api_data_specification_non_instrumented_hl7_type_converts_but_is_unsupported` is the regression test.
  - **Mapper-layer instrumentation (`app/mappings/common.py`, `app/mappings/adt.py`, `app/fhir_models/builders.py`)**: `build_patient`/`assemble_bundle` (shared by every message type) and `build_encounter_core`/every ADT trigger subclass's own `build_encounter` override all gained an optional trailing `recorder` parameter and `.record()`/`.record_inferred()` calls placed immediately after each existing write, with **zero changes to any actual business logic** - confirmed by a dedicated regression test (see Testing below), not just asserted. Real complications this slice surfaced, not anticipated up front:
    - **Constructor kwargs have no variable to record against until after construction returns**: `build_encounter_core` originally built `id`/`class_fhir` inline inside the `Encounter(...)` call - hoisted `encounter_id`/`encounter_class` onto their own lines *before* the constructor so there's something to record against, the general rule for every future constructor-kwarg site in every other mapper too, not just this one.
    - **A field's array index can desync from its own source component when some components are absent**: `build_human_names`'s own `given` list (PID-5 components 2/3, family middle name) filters out empty components before assigning - `given[0]` isn't always component 2 if component 2 happened to be empty and component 3 wasn't. Fixed by tracking `(component, value)` pairs through the filter, not just values, so the recorded `source_location` always points at the real originating component. `build_addresses` (PID-11 street lines) has the identical shape and got the identical fix; `build_addresses` also gained the `enumerate()` its own address-repetition loop didn't previously have (harmless on its own, needed to record `address[{i}]`-scoped facts at all).
    - **`AdtA02Mapper`'s own prior-location insertion shifts an already-recorded index**: inserting the prior location (PV1-6) at `location[0]` pushes the current location (already recorded by `build_encounter_core` at `location[0]`, from PV1-3) to `location[1]` - both facts are explicitly re-recorded at their final, post-insert indices rather than leaving a stale `location[0]` fact pointing at the wrong source field.
    - **`AdtA03Mapper`'s own discharge-time override needs to know which of two sources actually won**: `discharge_datetime(pv1, evn)` prefers PV1-45, falling back to EVN-2, but doesn't expose which branch fired to its own caller - `AdtA03Mapper.build_encounter` re-derives the same truthy check purely for provenance purposes (no change to `discharge_datetime`'s own behavior) so the corrected `period.end` fact points at the real source, not always PV1-45.
    - **Status is `derivation="inferred"` for all nine ADT triggers, never `"direct"`**: no ADT trigger reads `Encounter.status` from a field's own *value* - even A08's "finished if PV1-45 present" check is about the field's presence, not its content - so `build_encounter_core` takes an explicit `status_reason` string parameter (supplied by each trigger subclass alongside `status` itself) rather than trying to infer a reason generically.
  - **UI/route wiring**: `app/routes/data_specification.py` (`GET`/`POST /data-specification`, `POST /api/data-specification`) mirrors `app/routes/convert.py`'s exact established shape (`_run_crosswalk()` returning a `CrosswalkResult` with `error_category`/`error_message`/`status_code`, both a JSON API and a no-JS form-post fallback). `app/templates/index.html` is a genuinely separate page (not a pane on `index.html`) with its own self-contained paste-or-generate form (reusing `grouped_supported_types()`) and a results table (Source Location | FHIR Path | Source Value | FHIR Value, `derivation="inferred"` rows shown in italic with `reason` in place of a source location) built client-side by `app/static/app.js` from the JSON API response - the no-JS fallback shows the raw `CrosswalkReport` JSON instead, matching every other feature's own no-JS precedent exactly. Being a genuinely separate page is what let this pillar **sidestep, not just avoid repeating, the documented `hidden`-toggle bug class** every earlier feature had to work around (the "forgot to toggle this pane too" bug the FHIR → Message section's own "Use Bundle above" button shipped once, disclosed above) - this page's own panes never need to be added to `index.html`'s `show*()` chains or vice versa, since they're on different pages entirely. Verified end-to-end with a real browser (Puppeteer), not just route-level tests, per this project's own established discipline for anything UI-facing - light/dark mode, the unsupported-format banner, the inferred-row styling, and the horizontal-scroll-within-its-own-container behavior of the results table (confirmed the *page* itself never scrolls horizontally, only the table's own wrapper does) were all checked visually, not just asserted.
  - **Side-by-side correlated crosswalk UI** - a follow-up redesign of the page itself, after every backend section/EDI-family slice above had already shipped, prompted directly by real usability friction: the original single-column page (paste → table of Source Location/FHIR Path/Source Value/FHIR Value rows) made a user manually cross-reference table rows against the raw text and a separately-viewable, unhighlighted FHIR JSON to see how any one field actually mapped. The redesign puts the source pane and the resulting FHIR Bundle pane **side-by-side** with a left-to-right arrow between them, relabels the submit button **"Generate Crosswalk"**, and - the real payoff - **color-codes matching spans** in both panes (a source span and its FHIR-side counterpart share a `--match-{0..9}` background color) with a **hover tooltip** showing the fact's own detail and simultaneously outlining every element sharing that match, in both panes at once. The existing table stays below, unchanged, as the accessible/copyable fallback (screen readers, and the only place an inferred entry's full `reason` text is readable).
    - **Architecture: every highlighting span is computed server-side in Python**, never parsed client-side - the frontend only wraps pre-computed `[start, end]` character offsets in `<mark>` tags. This is a parsing problem (turning a `source_location`/`fhir_path` string into an exact character span in two different texts, for three structurally different source formats), not a rendering one, so it belongs next to the rest of this pillar's own format-specific logic, not in `app/static/app.js`.
    - **`app/provenance/json_locator.py`** - the one format-agnostic half: a hand-rolled recursive-descent JSON parser (not the stdlib `json` module, which exposes no token positions) walking `bundle.model_dump_json(indent=2, exclude_none=True)` once, building every leaf value's own `fhir_path` (the identical dotted/`[i]`-indexed convention `resolve_bundle_paths` already produces) → its exact `JsonSpan(start, end, token_type)`, `token_type` matching `app.js::highlightJson`'s own `json-string`/`json-number`/`json-literal` CSS classes so the FHIR pane's existing syntax coloring and the new match coloring can share one `<mark>`. A real recursive-descent walk, not a line/regex scan, is required for correctness - a FHIR value can legitimately contain `{`, `[`, or an escaped quote (a clinical note, a URI, a free-text SIG). Never raises - a parse failure (defensive, not currently reachable against this app's own well-formed output) degrades to an empty dict, not a crashed page.
    - **Three format-specific source locators, one shared hard problem**: a bare `source_location` string (`"OBX-5"`, `"SV1-1.2"`, `"act/entryRelationship[SUBJ]/observation/value/@code"`) doesn't say *which* physical occurrence of a repeating segment/element it came from when a message legitimately has more than one (several OBX groups across several DiagnosticReports, several LX/SV1 line items in one Claim, several Concern Act entries in a Problems section) - harmless while the crosswalk was just a table of strings, but now needs to resolve to one specific span. `app/provenance/highlighting.py`'s **occurrence-claiming algorithm** solves this once, generically, for all three formats: a claim key of `(root_key, scope_hint, scope_id, item_index)` (`root_key` = segment id for HL7v2/EDI or first path segment for CDA; `scope_hint` disambiguates same-named C-CDA sections, see below; `item_index` = a `.item[N]` index when present - deliberately *not* every bracketed integer in `fhir_path`, since an ordinary array field like `identifier[0]` is still sourced from the same physical segment as every other field on that resource, confirmed as a real bug during development: an EDI Organization's `identifier[0].value` and `.name` facts were wrongly claiming two different physical NM1 segments purely because one path had an extra bracket the other didn't). Entries are processed in `CrosswalkReport.entries`'s own already-guaranteed document order (confirmed by reading `recorder.py`/`resolver.py` directly); the first entry with a given claim key claims the next unclaimed physical occurrence of `root_key`, every later entry sharing that key reuses it.
      - **`app/provenance/hl7_locator.py`** (`Hl7Locator`) - reuses `app/hl7/parser.py`'s own `normalize_segment_separators`/`truncate_to_first_message` directly (both promoted from module-private to public for this - a second real consumer beyond `parse_message` itself), so offsets are relative to the *exact* text the real parser also sees, then independently re-splits by `\r`/`|`/`~`/`^` tracking offsets. Replicates the MSH field-numbering quirk exactly (MSH-1 is the field-separator character itself, not a `|`-split token; every MSH field N≥2 is `|`-split token N-1, one off from every other segment's own field N = token N) - confirmed by direct construction against the `hl7` library's own indexing, not assumed. `\r`→`\n` substituted for the *displayed* text only (a 1-for-1 character replacement, offsets unaffected), matching the FHIR→Message page's own established display-only substitution precedent.
      - **`app/provenance/edi_locator.py`** (`EdiLocator`) - reuses `app/edi/parser.py::strip_bom_and_whitespace` (promoted public the same way) and the interchange's own discovered `Delimiters`. **A genuine same-segment-id collision found and fixed**: `"DTP"` alone doesn't distinguish 837I's claim-level `DTP*434` (statement period, never mapped) from its own per-line `DTP*472` (service date) - the unfiltered locator counted the unrelated `DTP*434` as occurrence 0, shifting both real service-date occurrences off by one and resolving to an obviously-wrong concatenated span (`'19960911-19960911'` instead of `'19960911'`), reproduced directly against the real `edi_837i_basic.x12` fixture. Fixed via a qualifier filter (`DTP_SERVICE_DATE = "472"`, imported from `app.edi.common`) applied specifically to `"DTP"` lookups.
      - **`app/provenance/cda_locator.py`** (`CdaLocator`) - the hardest one: re-parses the raw XML a second time via `xml.parsers.expat` directly (not `ElementTree`, which exposes no position info), confirmed by direct testing with non-ASCII content (`José García`) that `parser.CurrentByteIndex` - despite its name - is a Python **character** offset, not a UTF-8 byte offset, into a lightweight position-aware tree built from custom start/end/char-data handlers. Walks a location string against a small grammar confirmed by direct inspection of every real `xpath_location(...)` call site in `app/cda/*.py`, plus one shape testing surfaced that inspection alone missed: `TAG` (0-indexed child), `TAG[N]`/`TAG[LABEL]`/`TAG[LABEL][N]` (positional/typeCode-filtered/both), `@attr` (must be last), `text()` (must be last), and a **bare trailing tag** (no `@`/`text()`) meaning "descend into this child, take its own text content" - used throughout for `family`/`given[N]`/`city`/`state`/`postalCode`/`country`/the free-text-SIG `<text>`. Fixed a real bug in the first draft: the fallback branch for a bare trailing tag returned the child's *start-tag* span (`"<family>"`) instead of its *text* span (`"Gierson"`) - caught immediately by comparing a resolved span's own substring against the fact's known `source_value`, not by a later review. **Cross-section root-tag collisions, confirmed real and fixed via a `scope_hint`**: Vitals and Results both wrap their entries in `<organizer>`; Problems and Hospital Discharge Diagnosis both wrap theirs in `<act>` - the bare tag name alone can't tell which section's own occurrence-count a given fact should count against. `highlighting.py`'s `_CdaScopeResolver` resolves each fact's own resource to a `scope_hint` string (`"vitals"`/`"results"`/`"problems"`/`"hospital_discharge_diagnosis"`/etc. - Observation needing an extra check, since a plain `Observation` alone doesn't say whether it's a Vitals panel member or a Results panel member; resolved via the Bundle's own `hasMember`/`result` reference graph, precomputed once per Bundle), and `CdaLocator._SCOPE_TEMPLATE_IDS` filters candidate elements by their own child `<templateId>` match against that section's real, already-public template ID constant (`app.cda.allergies`/`hospital_discharge_diagnosis`/`immunizations`/`medications`/`problems`/`procedures`/`results`/`vitals`) - verified by constructing real combined test documents that exercise the actual collision, not just asserting the fix works in isolation.
    - **The shared-physical-segment problem, and its second-order variant** - the one hazard genuinely specific to `highlighting.py`'s own orchestration, not any single locator: some fields belong to a *different* resource than the one whose segment they're physically embedded in - ORU's OBX-16 (Responsible Observer) materializes a separate `Practitioner`, but OBX-16 lives inside the identical physical `OBX` segment the referencing `Observation`'s own fields do.
