Left alone, both resources would claim *different* "OBX" occurrences under the scheme above - reproduced directly against the real `oru_r01_basic.hl7` fixture: the Practitioner's own OBX-16 claim consumed an occurrence slot, silently shifting every *later* Observation's own claim off by one and resolving it against the wrong physical segment entirely, not just an imprecise highlight on the Practitioner itself. Fixed via `_build_reference_map()` (walking every resource's own `Reference` fields the same generic, any-depth way `app/dedup.py::_rewrite_references` already does, reused here for the identical reason) plus a **borrowing** rule in `_resolve_source_span`/`_borrow_occurrence`: before claiming a fresh occurrence, a resource first checks whether some *other* resource directly connected to it by a Reference already holds a claim for the same `(root_key, scope_hint)` - if so, it borrows that occurrence instead. **A second-order bug this same mechanism then introduced, caught and fixed before the first draft shipped**: a Practitioner shared by *two* different Observations' own OBX-16 (the identical dedup shape `app/dedup.py`'s own performer-caching precedent already establishes) correctly borrowed the *first* Observation's occurrence, but then the *second*, independent, later Observation wrongly re-borrowed that *same* occurrence too, relayed through the shared Practitioner as an unintended bridge between two otherwise-unrelated resources - reproduced against the real `oru_r01_shared_performer.hl7` fixture (`entry[4].valueQuantity.value` resolved to `'7.2'` instead of the correct `'13.5'`). Fixed by restricting borrowing to only *original* (counter-assigned, never-itself-borrowed) claims, tracked via `resource_claims: dict[str, dict[tuple, tuple[int, bool]]]` - a shared "follower" resource can be borrowed *from* once, but can't relay a borrowed occurrence on to a second referencer, which then correctly falls through to claiming its own fresh one.
    - **A third occurrence-claiming bug, found by measuring span-resolution coverage rather than by inspection**: several resources routinely come from *one* source element - a C-CDA Vital Signs organizer builds a panel plus one `Observation` per reading, 5 resources from 1 `<organizer>` - but each distinct resource claimed a *fresh* occurrence, so every resource past the first claimed an index off the end and resolved to nothing. Vitals resolved 13/45 of its own facts (28.9%), Results 21/51, Procedures 25/53: the correlated-highlighting view was mostly blank for exactly the three sections whose own BP/pulse-ox/specimen/performer slice increased how many resources one element produces. Fixed by letting `_borrow_occurrence` relay a *borrowed* claim (`allow_relayed`) - but **only once every physical occurrence is already claimed**, which is precisely what distinguishes this from the ORU bug the original restriction exists to prevent: that one depended on a fresh occurrence still being available for the second referencer to take. Verified as strictly additive by diffing every resolved span before and after across every fixture - **153 facts newly resolve, 0 stopped resolving, 0 changed which text they point at** - so no existing highlight moved. Coverage: Vitals 28.9% -> 68.9%, Results 41.2% -> 84.3%, Procedures 47.2% -> 81.1%; the C-CDA drop register got more accurate for free (Results reports fewer drops, since a fact that now resolves is no longer mistaken for one that was never read).
    - **Bundle-level facts need `bundle.id`, not their own `fhir_path`, as their scope**: `Bundle.identifier` (from MSH-10/BHT03/TRN02 depending on format) and `Bundle.timestamp` (from MSH-7/BHT04+05) have no `entry[N]` resource to key off - scoping each by its own distinct `fhir_path` (the naive choice) meant the *second* one processed wrongly claimed a fresh occurrence instead of reusing the *one* real header segment both actually came from, reproduced directly (MSH-10 claimed "MSH" occurrence 0, then MSH-7 wrongly claimed occurrence 1). Fixed by using `bundle.id` itself as the scope for any fact with no resolvable resource.
    - **Two real, pre-existing provenance bugs found via this feature's own cross-checking, unrelated to the UI work itself**: `app/edi/eligibility_270.py`/`eligibility_271.py`'s own `CoverageEligibilityRequestItem.category`/`...ResponseInsuranceItem.category` facts were recorded with a `relative_path` missing the trailing `.category` segment, producing a `fhir_path` that doesn't exist in the real Bundle JSON at all - invisible to every existing test (none of which cross-check a recorded `fhir_path` against the real serialized Bundle), but immediately surfaced by `json_locator`'s own span lookup coming back empty. Fixed in both modules (plus the two `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py` assertions that had encoded the same bug).
    - **A genuine CSS specificity bug, caught only by looking at the actual rendered page, not by any of the above**: `button { display: inline-flex; ... }` (an ordinary author-stylesheet rule, `app/static/style.css`) has equal specificity to the browser's own UA-default `[hidden] { display: none }` rule; since author-origin rules always beat user-agent-origin rules regardless of specificity or source order, the `hidden` attribute was silently inert on *any* `<button>` in this app - including this new page's own "← Edit source" toggle link (confirmed visibly still rendered in a Puppeteer screenshot after `editSourceBtn.hidden = true` had genuinely been set) and, on closer inspection, the pre-existing FHIR→Message page's own "Use Bundle above" button (`app/templates/index.html`), which had silently rendered on every page load regardless of `result`'s own truthiness since the day it shipped, despite its own CLAUDE.md bullet's already-disclosed JS-side fix for a *different* bug in the same feature. Fixed with one shared rule, `button[hidden] { display: none; }` (specificity `0-1-1`, beats the bare `button` rule regardless of origin now that it's also author-authored and more specific) - confirmed via Puppeteer that both buttons now correctly toggle `display: none`/`inline-flex`/`flex`, not just that the `hidden` attribute itself gets set.
    - **`app/routes/data_specification.py`** - `_run_crosswalk` additionally calls the new `build_highlighting_payload(bundle, report, raw_text, report.source_format)` and serializes it onto `CrosswalkResult.highlighting_json`; `/api/data-specification`'s JSON response gains a `highlighting` key alongside the existing `bundle`/`report`, purely additive - every existing response key keeps its exact prior shape.
    - **Frontend (`app/templates/index.html`, `app/static/app.js`)** - `.crosswalk-panes` (CSS grid, `1fr auto 1fr`) holds the left pane (the existing `<textarea>`, an initially-hidden read-only `<pre>` for the post-generate highlighted view, and the "← Edit source" toggle), a center SVG arrow, and the right pane (an initially-shown placeholder, an initially-hidden highlighted `<pre>` for the FHIR JSON). `renderWithMarks(text, spans, {syntaxHighlightGaps})` is the one shared rendering primitive: interleaves escaped plain text with `<mark class="xwalk-mark" data-match="{i}" style="--match-color: var(--match-{colorIndex})">`-wrapped spans, sorted by start offset - the FHIR pane passes `syntaxHighlightGaps: true` so unmatched JSON stretches (punctuation, keys) still get `highlightJsonFragment`'s own token coloring (a fragment-safe adaptation of `app.js::highlightJson`, tolerant of a gap boundary that happens to split a token) while matched spans carry *both* their JSON-syntax class and their match color on the same `<mark>`. **Hover correlation**: event delegation (`mouseover`/`mouseout`/`mousemove`) on both panes keyed on `data-match` - on hover, every element sharing that `data-match` value *in both panes* gets `.is-active` (an accent-colored outline), and a floating `<dl>` tooltip (positioned via `getBoundingClientRect()`, flipping to stay on-screen) shows Source Location-or-Inferred-reason/Source Field Name/Source Value/FHIR Path/FHIR Value (in that order - a follow-up user-requested reorder from an earlier FHIR-Path-first version, matching the crosswalk table's own left-to-right column order) pulled from `report.entries` by array position (the same order `highlighting.matches` was built in). **Click to locate**: hover only correlates spans that happen to be on screen together, and only while the pointer stays put - clicking a mark instead *pins* the match (`.is-pinned`, a solid ring distinct from hover's `.is-active`, which can sit on the same element at once), scrolls the **other** pane to its counterpart, and writes both addresses into the two caret readouts. Clicking the same mark again, clicking pane background, or Esc releases it. Two deliberate scope calls: the scroll adjusts the pane's own `scrollTop` rather than calling `scrollIntoView`, which would also move the page and drag the just-clicked element out from under the pointer; and the pinned crosswalk row is marked but *not* scrolled to, since it sits below the panes. **Keyboard access lives on the table rows, not the marks** - each row carries `data-match` (the row index IS the match id, both being `report.entries` order), `tabindex="0"` and Enter/Space activation, giving the same pin-and-locate without making a document's several hundred inline `<mark>`s each a tab stop.
    - **Verification**: 62 new direct unit tests across `tests/test_json_locator.py`/`test_hl7_locator.py`/`test_edi_locator.py`/`test_cda_locator.py`/`test_highlighting.py`, each asserting a resolved span's own substring against a known expected value from a real fixture (`adt_a01_basic.hl7`, `edi_837i_basic.x12`, `ccd_allergies_basic.xml`, ...), not just "some span was returned" - plus a comprehensive sweep script (not a standing test, the same "run manually before shipping" precedent `app/dedup.py`'s own full-registry sweep already established) cross-checking every resolved `source_span`'s substring against `entry.source_value` across every real fixture in `tests/fixtures/`, run repeatedly through each bug-fix cycle above until it reached **zero mismatches, zero crashes across 1464 direct entries**. `tests/test_data_specification_routes.py` gained a dedicated assertion that the JSON API's `highlighting` key has the right shape, including that `exclude_none=True` correctly *omits* `color_index`/`source_span` for an inferred entry rather than serializing `null`. Verified end-to-end with a real browser (Puppeteer) across all three input formats (HL7v2/C-CDA/EDI 270, plus a dedicated EDI 835 pass - the one family with no `HL`/`BHT` structure at all) and both themes: side-by-side layout, color correlation between panes, the hover tooltip *and* its paired cross-pane outline firing together, and the "Edit source" round trip - this pass is what caught both the `button[hidden]` CSS bug and confirmed the fix, exactly the kind of defect this project's own established "test the feature in a browser, don't just review the code" discipline exists to catch.
  - **HL7v2 drops cite the v2-to-FHIR IG too (`app/provenance/hl7_ig_verdicts.py`)**, closing the parity gap that remained once C-CDA had verdicts: HL7v2 has a ballot-published IG with per-segment and per-datatype ConceptMaps, yet every one of its drops still said "not yet checked". Reading those tables found two real gaps and closed them, which between them accounted for most of the HL7v2 drops outstanding at the time: **CX.5 -> `Identifier.type.coding.code`** (bound to HL7 table 0203) was the single largest class of dropped HL7v2 data in this app at 59 rows, and **XCN.7 -> `qualification.code`** (table 0360) meant every XCN-derived Practitioner discarded its degree. A follow-up pass took it further, to **no unchecked drops and no gaps at all**: the SCH/OBR/AIG segment tables supplied the rest of the verdicts, and **OBR-2/OBR-3 -> `DiagnosticReport.identifier[1]`/`[2]`** (with the IG's fixed PLAC/FILL type codes) and **PV1-7 -> `participant.individual(Practitioner)`** were both implemented. That last one is why `PV1-7.7` is no longer a gap: ADT built only a display string for PV1-7, so XCN's id and degree had nowhere to go - it now materialises a real `Practitioner`, the same shape SIU's AIP and ORU's OBX-16 already used, and ORU drops to zero. A fourth verdict kind, **superseded**, covers `SCH-9`/`SCH-11`: the IG defines a target, this app implements it as a fallback, and in that message TQ1 simply supplied the value first - a fallback that was not needed rather than a capability that is missing. Of what remains, `EVN-2` maps to `Provenance.recorded` - a real target, on a resource this app deliberately never builds, which is the same permanent scope decision taken for C-CDA `<author>` and now cites it as such rather than as an unchecked drop. `PV1-7.7` stays a **GAP**: XCN.7 has a target, but ADT materialises no Practitioner for PV1-7 at all, only a display string, so there is nothing to carry the degree on. Unlike C-CDA, this IG publishes no "no map specified means no consensus target" statement, so a blank row is recorded as "the IG names no target" rather than borrowing language it never wrote.
  - **Every drop in all three formats now carries a checked verdict** - none saying "not yet checked", and none a gap. `test_every_drop_carries_a_checked_verdict` and `test_no_drop_is_an_unclosed_gap` keep it that way, parametrized across every fixture in all three formats. **EDI satisfies both structurally rather than being verified by them** - `_dropped_edi_decisions` cites the absent X12 crosswalk unconditionally and can produce neither an unchecked drop nor a gap, so those arms would pass even if its citations were wrong; `test_every_edi_drop_cites_the_absent_crosswalk` is the assertion actually holding EDI's half up. Getting there took two more rounds of the same discipline: check whether the value reached the Bundle before calling anything a gap, and read the IG's own table rather than the shape's resemblance to one already answered. Real gaps closed on the way: `FamilyMemberHistory.bornDate` from the relative's `birthTime`, and the deceased date, which was recorded as *inferred* while its own reason text said it had been read from `sdtc:deceasedTime` - so the attribute it came from was simultaneously reported as dropped.
  - **A leaf whose relative path something recorded counts as read.** Recorded C-CDA locations are relative and say nothing about which repeat they came from - `procedure/performer[0]/assignedEntity/id/@root` is what the performer of *every* procedure records - so a document with two procedures records one string twice and the span resolver can only attribute it to one. Treating the unattributed twin as dropped accused the mapper of losing data it had carried. This is the same aggregation, and the same disclosed trade, the X12 half already makes: it errs toward under-reporting, the direction this register has to fail in. Comparison also normalises away `[0]`, since the scanner omits a first repeat's index while recorders write it out - the two spelled the same element differently and never matched.
  - **`xmlns` declarations are XML plumbing, not lost clinical data.** The position-aware parser runs with namespace processing off (it must, to report real character offsets), so `xmlns:sdtc="urn:hl7-org:sdtc"` arrives as an attribute named `sdtc` carrying the namespace URI, and was reported as a dropped value.
  - **C-CDA verdicts are keyed by templateId, not by element shape alone.** A shape like `entryRelationship/observation/id` is ambiguous - an Allergy Reaction Observation and a Problem Observation sit at identical depths under identically-named tags, and the IG gives them different answers. Two verdicts had already been withdrawn for exactly that misattribution. `scan_populated_cda_values` now carries every templateId root on a leaf's ancestor chain, drops group by chain as well as shape, and a verdict key may name the template it read (`"<root>|<shape>"`, scoped keys beating unscoped ones). Total drops rise slightly because two sections that previously collapsed into one finding are now correctly two - the IG tables they answer to are different.
  - **Two required header elements were invisible, because nothing in the corpus carried them.** `ClinicalDocument/author` is `1..*` and `custodian` is **1..1** per the CDA StructureDefinition, so every real document has both - yet no fixture and no generated document emitted either, which is why the verdict-coverage tests passed without ever reaching them. The generator now emits both (its own contract is to produce valid output, and omitting a required element broke that), two fixtures carry them, and each reports as **one grouped row** rather than one per name part and id - the same rule `<entry>` already follows, one level up, via `HEADER_PARTICIPATION_TAGS`. The verdict is `NO_MAP`, and precisely: the IG publishes no header table, *and* the FHIR home would be `Composition.author`/`.custodian`, which this app deliberately never builds (it emits `Bundle(type="collection")`). **Two authors are two findings, not one shape repeated twice** - the shape collapse exists because seven Problems entries dropping the same `id` say nothing seven times, which is not what a header carries: this one holds a person and a software system. Header participations are marked `distinct` and key off their own indexed location, so each keeps its own "Carried ..." detail, headed by a name or device model rather than by its `<time>`. **That exposed a pre-existing id collision, fixed here**: rows group by templateId chain as well as shape but the id carried only the shape, so two sections dropping the same-looking element produced two findings sharing one id - and `apply_rejections` keys a dict by id, making one unreachable and having the UI share one review state across both rows. Ids now take a `[n]` suffix on collision, the same rule the HL7v2 half already used for a repeating field, guarded by `test_decision_ids_are_unique` across every fixture in all three formats. **The verdict-coverage tests now run over generated samples too**, not only fixtures - `test_generated_samples_carry_only_checked_verdicts`, parametrized across every registered type. The fixtures are a couple of dozen hand-written documents; the generators reach far more shape combinations, and measuring against them found **27 unchecked shapes while the fixture tests were green**. Checking each against "should this drop at all?" before writing any verdict turned six into bugs: namespace declarations excluded by *prefix name* when the prefix is arbitrary (`ns2` after an ElementTree round trip); reference-range and one-sided IVL_PQ bounds recording their value but not their `@unit`, so a carried unit read as lost; `rateQuantity` the same; `is_mapped` consulting the value fallback *before* honouring `allow_fallbacks=False`, which let a wholly skipped entry borrow a sibling read and scatter into leaf rows instead of one "entry not converted" finding; **PV1-7** mapped for ADT and ignored by ORU/MDM's minimal Encounter; and **SCH-25**, which the v2-to-FHIR SCH map routes to `Appointment.status` through a published ConceptMap while this app derived status from the trigger event alone - so a waitlisted appointment came out `booked`. The remaining shapes carry real verdicts, including three new kinds the existing set could not express honestly: `UNRECOGNIZED` (the element was read; its code has no row in the published ConceptMap, so the mapping fell back to its default - the C-CDA analogue of HL7v2's `UNPARSEABLE`), `FIXED_TARGET` (the spec or IG fixes the FHIR value, so the source element is never consulted), and `FOLDED` (the IG names a target on a resource this element did not produce, because it was folded into a panel). **A second standing check covers what the verdict tests structurally cannot**: recording one half of a pair makes the other look dropped, but the register's value fallback can mask that by matching the same text elsewhere in the document - so an unrecorded half does not reliably surface as an unchecked drop. `test_no_built_value_has_an_unrecorded_other_half` compares the recorded facts against the built Bundle directly instead, for every coding's `display` and every quantity's `unit`. Four instances had already been found by hand (reference ranges, one-sided IVL_PQ bounds, `rateQuantity`, and `Composition.section` codes), which is three more than should depend on somebody noticing.
  - **An entry nothing was read from is one finding, not six.** This app skips some entries by design (a negated observation, a relationship whose typeCode is not what the template calls for), and every element beneath a skipped entry then drops as collateral. Reporting each leaf separately made one decision look like six unrelated ones and buried the thing worth reviewing: a whole clinical statement was discarded. `_unconverted_entry_paths` finds `<entry>` elements with no mapped leaf anywhere beneath and reports them once, citing "Source entry not converted".
  - **Nine drops were the register lying, not real data loss** - each value was confirmed present in the converted Bundle before anything was called a gap, which is the rule that caught them. `ContactPoint.use` on all three Procedure telecom sites, `CodeableConcept.text` from `<originalText>` (fixed once in the shared `record_coding`, so it covers every section), IVL_PQ bound `@unit`s, the Specimen collection body-site display, and the Smoking Status value display were all carried into FHIR and simply never recorded. A crosswalk that accuses the mapper of losing data it carried is worse than one that says "unchecked".
  - **Four header findings were real, and one was a bug rather than a gap.** `HumanName.use` was decided by position - first name official, rest "old" - which labelled a `<name use="P">` pseudonym a *former name*, stating something about the patient the document never said. The source's own use code now decides where it maps, with position as the fallback. `Address.use` was not mapped at all and now is; `ContactPoint.use` was mapped but unrecorded; and `Encounter.class.display` now carries the source `displayName`, but only for a code we actually recognised - a display belonging to some other code would misdescribe the default class we fell back to. The name and address use maps are read off the HL7 v3 code systems directly (the IG maps both as whole datatypes and publishes no datatype table for either) and are deliberately partial: v3's vacation-home, artist-name and indigenous-name codes have no unambiguous FHIR counterpart, so they stay unmapped and keep being reported, rather than being given a plausible-looking mismap.
  - **HL7v2's drops are now fully accounted for: none unchecked, none a gap.** Reading the last seven against the IG's own segment and datatype tables turned up two real gaps, one scanner bug and two categories that were never IG questions:
    - **`TXA-19` is conditional, and only half of it was implemented.** The segment map assigns `status = "current"` when TXA-19 is `"AV"`, and for `"CA"`/`"OB"`/`"UN"` places the code on `status` itself as an alternate-codes extension - a URL the IG assigns verbatim. We honoured the first branch and dropped the second, so a cancelled or obsolete document was indistinguishable from an available one. `status` stays inferred `"current"` for those codes (the IG assigns no status there, and `DocumentReference.status` is required), while the sender's own code now rides along on `status__ext`. Its value is `ID[CodeableConcept]`, which maps `ID.1` to `coding.code` and assigns no system, so none is invented.
    - **`AIG-3.3` had a target too.** AIG-3 maps through `CWE[Identifier]`, where CWE.3 (Name of Coding System) is `Identifier.system`. A local placeholder was hard-coded and CWE.3 ignored, so a sender naming its own coding system had that name dropped - the same complaint already raised about CE coding systems, one field over. The raw name is carried as-is: the IG's own comment ("some mapping of the CWE.3 value to an actual URI") leaves the resolution open, so guessing a URI would be inventing one.
    - **A literal `^` in free text was being read as a component separator.** The mappers already know better - that is what `raw_field_str` exists for - but the register's scanner split every field alike, so `"Grade II^ tear noted on exam"` was reported as having dropped a component `" tear noted on exam"` nobody ever sent. The register was accusing the mapper of losing data it had carried whole. The scanner now mirrors the mappers' own set exactly: NTE-3 and TXA-25 always, OBX-5 when OBX-2 says so.
    - **Two drops were never IG questions.** `PID-7` maps to `Patient.birthDate` and this app builds it whenever the value parses - so a PID-7 that reaches the register carried a date the parser could not read. That is a malformed message, not a missing capability, and it gets a citation that says so rather than "unchecked". `TXA-10.7` (degree) is recorded whenever TXA-10 builds its own Practitioner; it only ever drops when TXA-10 names the same person as TXA-9 and reuses that Practitioner - superseded, not lost. `TXA-9`/`TXA-10` also joined `JOINED_FIELDS`, being `person_display` fields exactly like `PV1-7`.
  - **The review workflow reaches C-CDA and X12 EDI resources too.** `REJECTION_STRATEGY` only knew HL7v2's resources, so rejecting an inferred `Coverage.status` or `Task.intent` reported "no verified conformant representation" and did nothing. Sixteen rows were added, each read off the **published R4 CodeSystem** rather than the ValueSet page or memory - `fm-status` (`active|cancelled|draft|entered-in-error`) has no null flavour, so `Coverage`/`Claim`/`ClaimResponse`/`CoverageEligibility*`/`PaymentReconciliation` statuses all take the data-absent-reason route, while `request-status` does carry `unknown` so `CarePlan.status` takes the code route. **`Task.intent` is the one that would have been got wrong either way**: its own CodeSystem contains *only* `"unknown"` (the request-intent codes arrive through the ValueSet), so `unknown` really is valid there. `history-status` carries `health-unknown`, but that states the *relative's health* is unknown rather than the record's status - a different concept, so it is not usable as a null flavour and `FamilyMemberHistory.status` takes the absent route. Nested paths (`category[0].coding[0].code`) still report "not applied", which is honest: each would need its own value-set check.
  - **`PV1-7/8/9/17` all map now, and all repeat.** The v2-to-FHIR PV1[Encounter] map gives each its own ParticipationType code - Attending `ATND`, Referring `REF`, Consulting `CON`, Admitting `ADM` - and each a cardinality of **0..-1**. Only the attending doctor was read, and only its first repetition, so a referring or consulting doctor and any second attending were silently discarded. `build_encounter_participants` in `common.py` builds all four for `build_encounter_core` and `build_minimal_encounter` alike, since the IG's map does not care which message type carried the PV1. **Three other repeating fields had the same defect**, each found by diffing the IG's own cardinality column against what the code reads rather than by inspection: `PID-13` (home phone), `OBX-16` (responsible observer) and `TXA-9` (originator) all read only repetition 0. `PID-3/5/11` were already correct, which is why the bug was easy to miss. The generator now emits all four doctor fields with an occasional second repetition - without that, nothing exercised the repeating half of the mapping. **`PV1-3` is now mapped for a minimal encounter too.** ORU's and MDM's context Encounter read only PV1-2 and PV1-19, so the location the PV1 actually carried was dropped - "minimal" was meant to mean "no lifecycle to infer", not "ignore the location". `build_minimal_encounter` now builds the same PL Location chain ADT does. `location[].status` stays unset: the IG maps PV1-3 to it, but nothing in a result or document context says whether the patient is still there.
  - **Mapping decisions shown beside the conversion they produced.** The register listed every decision in one block above the table, so seeing which conversion a decision belonged to meant cross-referencing two lists on an already-long page. Inferred decisions now render as a **Mapping Decision** column in the table (renamed from "Field detail" to **Specification Crosswalk**), carrying the badge, the citation and the Reject control - which had to move with them, or rejecting an inferred value would no longer be reachable. Dropped decisions then moved into the table too, as their own rows with the FHIR columns left empty - source data that went nowhere is still part of the specification, and the emptiness *is* the finding. That left the register block with nothing to show, so it is gone entirely and the crosswalk is the single place every decision appears. The CSV export gained the same rows, or the export and the table would disagree about what the conversion did. Direct entries get a `Direct copy`/`Transformed` badge, computed from whether `source_value` differs from `value`. **Two real bugs surfaced while building it**: `renderDecisions` ran *after* `showCrosswalk`, so the lookup was empty when the table rendered and every inferred row drew a blank cell; and, pre-existing, `build_highlighting_payload` was built from the model *before* `apply_rejections` mutated the serialized dict - so rejecting a value changed the returned bundle but not the pane the reviewer was looking at, which read as doing nothing. The payload now takes an optional `fhir_json_text` and is rebuilt from the post-rejection JSON when anything was rejected (only then - the source-side spans it feeds to `compute_decisions` are offsets into the raw message, unaffected either way).
  - **Dedup-aware provenance** - the last disclosed gap this pillar had left, closed as a follow-up once the correlated crosswalk UI above made it directly visible: `app/dedup.py`'s own opt-in Bundle deduplication pass (merging duplicate Patient/Practitioner/Organization/Location resources, the real 837P/837I billing-vs-rendering-provider case that module was built for) had never been wired into the Data Specification pillar at all - generating a crosswalk for a Bundle with duplicates always showed *every* duplicate's own facts, with no way to see what the crosswalk looks like once they're merged. **Turned out to need zero changes to `app/provenance/{recorder,resolver,highlighting}.py` themselves, confirmed rather than assumed**: `ProvenanceRecorder`'s own facts are keyed by `(resource_id, relative_path)`, and `deduplicate_bundle` never rewrites a *surviving* resource's own fields - it only drops the removed duplicate's own `Bundle.entry` and rewrites *other* resources' `Reference`s to point at the kept one - so running `deduplicate_bundle()` *before* `resolve_bundle_paths()` (not after) means a fact recorded against a resource that dedup goes on to remove simply hits `resolve_bundle_paths`'s own pre-existing "a `resource_id` not found in `bundle.entry` is skipped, not raised" branch, a mechanism that already existed for a different, hypothetical reason. `app/provenance/dispatch.py::convert_with_provenance` gained a `deduplicate: bool = False` parameter and a small `_deduplicate_if_requested()` helper called in all three format branches, between `build_bundle()`/`to_bundle()` and `resolve_bundle_paths()`; the function's own return type grew from `tuple[Bundle, CrosswalkReport]` to `tuple[Bundle, CrosswalkReport, DedupResult | None]` so the route layer can report what merged, mirroring `app/routes/convert.py::_run_conversion`'s own `dedup_summary` shape and `deduplication` JSON key exactly (`app/routes/data_specification.py::_run_crosswalk` gained the identical `deduplicate` parameter and `dedup_summary` dict-building logic, kept as its own small copy rather than a shared extraction - CLAUDE.md's own "extract on second real consumer" discipline correctly applied in the other direction here, since a two-line dict comprehension isn't yet worth a cross-module helper). The UI gained a "Deduplicate resources" checkbox (`app/templates/index.html`, reusing the exact `.checkbox-field`/`.dedup-summary` CSS classes and wording style the Convert page's own identical checkbox already established) and a `#crosswalk-dedup-summary` line, wired in `app/static/app.js`'s `showCrosswalk()` the same way `app.js::showResult()` already drives `#dedup-summary`. The correlated-highlighting payload itself (`build_highlighting_payload`) needed no dedup-awareness of its own either - it's always called with whatever `bundle`/`report` `_run_crosswalk` already has in hand, which is the *post*-dedup pair when requested, so a merged-away duplicate's own source segment simply shows no highlight at all in the correlated view (the honest result: that segment's own data didn't produce a *separate* surviving resource for a highlight to point at). Verified against the real motivating case (`edi_837p_basic.x12`, the identical fixture `tests/test_dedup.py`'s own direct-module test already uses) both at the module level (`tests/test_highlighting.py`: `dedup_result.merged_count == 1`, the removed Practitioner's own facts gone from `report.entries`, the kept one's facts still resolve to real, correctly-reindexed spans) and the route level (`tests/test_data_specification_routes.py`, mirroring `tests/test_routes.py`'s own three dedup-checkbox tests for `/api/convert` almost line-for-line) and confirmed visually via Puppeteer, screenshotting the same fixture with the checkbox off and on.
    - **A real, previously-undetected highlighting bug found and fixed while verifying the above in an actual browser, unrelated to dedup itself**: the 837P screenshot showed the Billing Provider's own recorded name highlighting the wrong physical text entirely (`"PREMIER BILLING SERVICE"`, the 1000A Submitter's own name, not `"KILDARE"`) - reproduced directly, confirmed present *before* any of this session's dedup work too (not a regression), and invisible to the correlated-crosswalk feature's own original 1464-entry verification sweep only because that sweep cross-checked against `entry.source_value`, which every EDI-family fact leaves `None` (EDI's own `.record()` calls only ever pass `value`) - re-running the sweep with `entry.value` as the fallback comparison immediately surfaced it. **Root cause**: `app/provenance/highlighting.py`'s original occurrence-claiming scheme assigned strictly sequential occurrence numbers (0, 1, 2, ...) to each newly-seen `(root_key, scope_hint)` claim, in the order facts were *recorded* during mapping, trusting that the Nth distinct fact-bearing resource really was the Nth *physical* segment occurrence in the raw text - an assumption that silently breaks for 837P/837I/837D specifically, since all three carry two leading, untracked `NM1` loops (1000A Submitter, 1000B Receiver, neither ever mapped to a FHIR field) *and* build their own tracked resources in an order that doesn't match physical document order (`Edi837pBuilder.build_bundle()` builds Billing, Payer, Subscriber, Rendering Provider, but the raw text has Billing, Subscriber, Payer, Rendering) - every single `NM1`-rooted fact in the whole family resolved to a different, wrong physical segment as a result, not an approximation. **Fixed via content-verified occurrence claiming**: `_claim_fresh_occurrence()` now tries the fact's own already-recorded `source_value` (falling back to `value`) against every not-yet-claimed physical occurrence's own field-scoped resolved text first, only falling back to the original sequential scheme when no exact match is found (a transformed/derived value - `person_display`/`location_display`'s own space-joined strings, a parsed date, a mapped code - never matches any literal physical text, so this correctly degrades to the already-proven-correct original behavior for HL7v2/CDA with zero regression risk, confirmed by re-running the same 1464-entry sweep with the fix in place). Occurrence tracking itself had to change shape to support this: a bare monotonic counter (`next_occurrence: dict[count_key, int]`, correct only when occurrences are claimed in ascending order) became `used_occurrences: dict[count_key, set[int]]`, since content matching can legitimately jump straight to occurrence 4 before occurrence 1 is ever claimed. For the genuine duplicate-value case this app's own dedup feature exists to collapse (837P's own solo-practitioner shape, `"KILDARE"`/`"BEN"`/the same NPI legitimately repeated at both the Billing and Rendering Provider's own physical `NM1` loops) content matching still resolves each fact to its *own* physical occurrence, not a shared one - the first resource processed claims the first matching occurrence, and the second resource's own search then correctly excludes it, finding the *other* one instead.
    - **A fifth, reported from the running app: a Platelets row pointing at Leukocytes.** Three separate causes, all pre-existing, found by sweeping every direct fact in every fixture and generated document and asking whether its resolved span really contains its own value. (1) `_claim_fresh_occurrence` **skips already-claimed occurrences** when content-matching. That is right when each resource is a *different* occurrence (an 835's claims) and wrong when several legitimately *share* one: the second member of a Results organizer skipped its own organizer because a sibling had claimed it, then resolved its `component[N]` against the *next* organizer - a Hemoglobin row showing Leukocytes' text. It now falls back to matching among the claimed occurrences before resorting to the next free index. (2) **Borrowing ran before content matching.** `Composition.section[].entry` references every narrative `DocumentReference`, so the Composition borrowed one's `<code>` and reported the *document's* type against a *section's* code. Borrowing is a heuristic about relationships and the text is direct evidence, so where the borrowed occurrence demonstrably does not carry the fact's value and another does, the text now wins. (3) `problems.py` **recorded both of a Concern Act's `entryRelationship[SUBJ]` children at the identical location** - no index, unlike `allergies.py`'s own `entryRelationship[MFST][{index}]` - so the second Condition could not resolve to its own observation. Measured across every C-CDA fixture plus 120 generated documents: **563 misattributed facts fixed, 0 introduced**; register still 0 unchecked/0 gaps over 360 documents, and reverse-transform resource counts unchanged.
  - **A fourth: three C-CDA sections build a `MedicationRequest` from a `<substanceAdministration>`, and the scope resolver returned `"medications"` for all of them.** Discharge Medications and Immunizations resources therefore claimed a *plain Medications* entry, and that entry's own values then reported as dropped - the register accusing the mapper of losing data, from a document where the real cause was a misattributed span. Both halves needed different signals, and both are real data rather than invented markers: Discharge Medications carries the `.category` marker the reverse builder already splits on, but its `<substanceAdministration>` bears the **identical** Medication Activity templateId as a plain one (unlike Problems/Hospital Discharge Diagnosis, whose acts differ), so `_SCOPE_ANCESTORS` filters on the Discharge Medication Act *one level up* instead. An INT-mood Immunization is CVX-coded, which no medication is. Anything else stays `"medications"`, so the fallback is exactly the old behaviour. Verified additive by the same span diff: **11 newly resolve, 0 lost, 0 moved.**
  - **Fixing that surfaced two genuine mapper gaps the misattribution had been masking**, which is the point of the register: **CVX was absent from `OID_TO_FHIR_SYSTEM` entirely**, so every vaccine code landed on a `urn:oid:` fallback rather than `http://hl7.org/fhir/sid/cvx` (confirmed from HL7's own terminology server: `CodeSystem-v2-0292` carries that url and `identifier: urn:oid:2.16.840.1.113883.12.292`); and a **planned (INT-mood) immunization's `effectiveTime` was never read at all**, though the IG's MedicationRequest table maps it and `app/cda/medications.py` already implements exactly that row - it now lands on `dosageInstruction[0].timing.repeat.boundsPeriod`, sharing `timing.repeat` with `repeatNumber` rather than replacing it. The register's own fuzz sweep went from 8 unchecked drops in 360 documents to **0 in 450**.
  - **A third occurrence-claiming bug, surfaced by the 835 ClaimResponse work**: several resources routinely come from *one* source element (a C-CDA Vital Signs organizer), so once every physical occurrence is claimed a further resource shares one instead of claiming past the end - but it shared `min(used)` unconditionally. An 835 builds a ClaimResponse per `CLP`, and each is a *different* claim, so every claim past the first highlighted the **first** `CLP`'s text - reproduced directly against `edi_835_multi_claim.x12` (the second claim's `300.00` charge resolving to the first claim's). Fixed by content-matching among the already-claimed occurrences before falling back to `min(used)`, which stays the fallback for a value transformed on the way through and so matching no physical text - the C-CDA panel case this branch was written for. Verified strictly additive by the same before/after span diff across every fixture the earlier fixes used, keyed on `(resourceType, relative path)` rather than the Bundle-qualified path so the 835's own new entry indices don't read as lost spans: **20 newly resolve, 0 stopped resolving, 0 changed which text they point at**.
  - **A second, independent bug found via the same re-run sweep, a different shape entirely**: `PaymentReconciliation.detail[]` (one per 835 `CLP` claim) shares the identical `"CLP-1"`/`"CLP-2"`/`"CLP-4"` location string across every claim, on the *one* `PaymentReconciliation` resource - the exact same "no other disambiguator baked into the string itself" shape `Claim.item[]`/`CoverageEligibilityRequest.item[]`/`ClaimResponse.item[]` already needed `_item_index()`'s help for, but `.detail[N]` was never added to that function's own regex - reproduced directly against `edi_835_multi_claim.x12`, whose own second claim's facts silently reused the first claim's already-claimed physical `CLP` segment instead of claiming its own. Fixed by broadening `_ITEM_INDEX_RE` (renamed `_REPEATING_FIELD_INDEX_RE`) to `r"\.(?:item|detail)\[(\d+)\]"` - a grep across every `relative_path=f"..."` call site in `app/edi/*.py`/`app/mappings/*.py`/`app/cda/*.py` for this same shape (an f-string array-index bracket with no other disambiguator) turned up two more candidates, `SIU.serviceType[N]` and CDA's `AllergyIntolerance.reaction[N]` - both confirmed **not** to need the same fix: `reaction[N]`'s own physical disambiguation already lives inside the location string itself (`entryRelationship[MFST][{index}]`, C-CDA's own bracket grammar), and `serviceType[N]`'s underlying gap is real but currently unreachable (confirmed by reading `app/generators/siu.py`: the generator only ever emits one `AIS` segment, and no real fixture has more than one either) - left disclosed rather than "fixed" against a case nothing can currently exercise or verify.
    - Both fixes verified together against every real fixture in `tests/fixtures/` (the same sweep methodology this pillar's own original verification pass established, re-run to **zero mismatches beyond documented, deliberate value transformations** - display-string formatting, date reformatting, status-code ConceptMap mappings, the disclosed CDA bare-`<id>` whole-start-tag simplification, EDI's own disclosed comma-joined `diagnosisSequence`/formatted-disposition-text fields - none a resolution bug), four new dedicated regression tests in `tests/test_highlighting.py` (837P/837I/837D's own `NM1` facts each resolve to their own physical segment; content matching still disambiguates the genuine Billing-vs-Rendering duplicate case correctly; content matching still falls back to the original sequential scheme for a `person_display`-built value; both 835 `CLP` claims resolve independently), and a fresh Puppeteer pass re-confirming the 837P screenshot now highlights the *correct* physical text on both the with- and without-dedup views.
  - **"Source Field Name" column + capped inferred-row width** - a follow-up prompted directly by real user feedback on the crosswalk table: (1) an inferred entry's `reason` text (shown in the Source Location column in place of a real location) could run to a full sentence and visibly stretch the whole table wide; (2) a reader unfamiliar with HL7v2's own field numbering had no way to know what `"PID-3[0].1"` or `"PID-3[0].4"` actually *mean* without looking them up separately. **`app/provenance/hl7_field_names.py`** is a new, disclosed-scope lookup - `SEGMENT_FIELD_NAMES` (whole-field names, e.g. `PID-5` -> "Patient Name") plus a `_FIELD_DATATYPES`/`_COMPONENT_NAMES` pair for the composite HL7v2 datatypes (CX/XPN/XAD/XCN/CWE/TQ) this app's own mappers actually decompose into components - scoped to exactly the segments/fields/datatypes a grep of every real `hl7_location(...)` call site across `app/mappings/`/`app/fhir_models/` turned up, not a standalone HL7v2 encyclopedia, the same "map what's actually used" discipline this app's CDA/EDI field-mapping modules already establish. `resolve_hl7_field_label(parsed)` prefers the more specific component-level name when one resolves (`"PID-3[0].4"` -> "Assigning Authority", not "Patient Identifier List") - CX component 1 is deliberately shortened to "ID" (not the standard's own fuller "ID Number") to keep a narrow table column readable. **`ProvenanceEntry` gained a `field_label: str | None` field**, populated in the one real choke point that already builds every entry - `app/provenance/resolver.py::resolve_bundle_paths` - by reusing `app/provenance/hl7_locator.py::parse_hl7_location` (the same parser the correlated-highlighting feature already built and battle-tested) rather than writing a second location-string parser; gated to `source_format == "HL7v2"` explicitly (not left to accidental regex-mismatch/empty-lookup safety) since CDA's `xpath_location()`/EDI's `edi_location()` produce structurally different location shapes this lookup was never scoped to handle - both degrade to `None` (excluded from JSON via the route's existing `exclude_none=True`), never a guessed label. Read in both `app/static/app.js::renderCrosswalkTable` (a new "Source Field Name" table column, inserted between Source Location and FHIR Path) and `showTooltip` (a new row, shown only for direct entries - an inferred entry has no `source_location` and thus no label either). **A real, confirmed-in-browser CSS bug caught while verifying the width fix, not assumed to just work from reading the CSS**: an initial `max-width`+`overflow-wrap` fix applied directly to `#crosswalk-table td:nth-child(1)` silently did nothing - `getComputedStyle` showed `white-space: nowrap` still winning over the intended `white-space: normal` override, because `#crosswalk-table td { white-space: nowrap; }`'s ID-selector specificity beats `tr.crosswalk-inferred td:nth-child(1)`'s own class-plus-pseudo-class specificity regardless of how many classes the latter has (specificity compares ID count before class count) - the reason text was overflowing straight into the FHIR Path column instead of wrapping, confirmed visually via Puppeteer before the fix and invisible from reading the CSS rules alone. Fixed two ways together: the overriding selector itself gained the same `#crosswalk-table` ID prefix (`#crosswalk-table tr.crosswalk-inferred td:nth-child(1)`) to actually win the cascade, and the `max-width`/`overflow-wrap` themselves target a `display: inline-block` wrapper (the existing `<em>` the JS already wraps reason text in), not the `<td>` directly - `max-width` on a table cell in the table's own default auto layout is only a soft hint to the column-width algorithm, not a hard constraint, confirmed by the identical overflow persisting even after the specificity fix alone. Verified via direct unit tests in `tests/test_hl7_field_names.py` (component-vs-whole-field precedence, unrecognized segment/field/component fallback behavior) and `tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`/`_cda.py` (a real ADT fixture's own `PID-3[0].1` resolving to `"ID"` end-to-end through `resolve_bundle_paths`, a CDA fixture's every entry confirmed to carry no `field_label` at all), plus a full Puppeteer pass against a real generated ADT^A08 message (the one that originally prompted this feedback) confirming the table header/column order, the wrapped-not-overflowing inferred cell (measured via `getBoundingClientRect`, ~319px vs. a ~1249px table rather than stretching it), and the tooltip's own new "Source Field Name: ID" row for a `PID-3[0].1` hover - in both light and dark themes. **A real stale-static-asset bug found immediately after shipping this, unrelated to the feature's own logic**: the user reported both fixes above "not working," which turned out to be a browser serving its own previously-cached copy of the crosswalk script/`style.css` from before the fix - confirmed by reproducing the SAME page fresh via Puppeteer (no prior cache) and seeing the correct behavior, while the user's own already-open session did not; see `app/routes/static_assets.py`'s own bullet below for the durable fix (cache-busted static URLs), not just a one-off "hard refresh" workaround. **Two direct follow-up UX requests, both shipped immediately after**: the hover tooltip's own row order was reordered to match the table's left-to-right column order (Source Location, Source Field Name, Source Value, FHIR Path, FHIR Value - previously FHIR Path came first, a carryover from before Source Field Name existed at all). Separately, **"Source Value" was inconsistently blank for any field a mapper copies without transforming** - `ProvenanceRecorder.record()`'s own `source_value` parameter is only ever passed explicitly when a mapper actually transforms a value (a date reformatted, a code mapped - see `app/mappings/common.py`'s own `birthDate`/`gender` calls), never for a plain copy (e.g. `PID-3[0].1`'s raw identifier text, `PID-5[0].1`'s raw family name) since it would just duplicate `value` verbatim - a deliberate, disclosed choice on the recording side, but one that made "Source Value" look randomly, inconsistently absent depending on whether that specific field happened to need reformatting, not a real semantic gap. Fixed at the display layer rather than touching the ~100+ `recorder.record()` call sites across every mapper: a new shared `displayedSourceValue(entry)` helper in `app/static/app.js` falls back to `entry.value` whenever `entry.source_value` is absent, for any *direct* entry (an inferred entry genuinely has no source field to fall back to, so it correctly still shows nothing) - used by both `renderCrosswalkTable`'s own Source Value column and `showTooltip`'s own row, so the table and tooltip can't drift apart on this again. Verified in a real browser: `PID-5[0].1` (a plain copy) now shows `"Doe"` in both places where it previously showed nothing, while `PID-7` (birthDate, a real transformation) still correctly shows the untransformed `"19620305"` distinct from the reformatted FHIR value `"1962-03-05"`, and an inferred entry's tooltip still omits the row entirely.
  - **"Source Field Name" extended to CDA and EDI** - a follow-up closing the disclosed HL7v2-only gap the bullet above left open, prompted directly by the user's own suggestion of "the most natural next step." Two new sibling lookup modules, each mirroring `hl7_field_names.py`'s exact "scoped to what's actually used, unrecognized returns `None`, never a guess" shape but adapted to that format's own genuinely different location-string grammar - confirmed against every real `edi_location(...)`/`xpath_location(...)` call site in `app/edi/*.py`/`app/cda/*.py` (including tracing every `_LOCATION`/`_base`/`*_location` constant forward to its own `recorder.record(...)` call, not just its definition line, since several are intermediate base strings further extended with `/@code`/`/@displayName`/`/@value` before a fact is ever actually recorded) rather than guessed from field-name similarity to the HL7v2 table. **`app/provenance/edi_field_names.py`** reuses `edi_locator.py`'s own already-existing `parse_edi_location()`/`ParsedEdiLocation` directly (no new parser needed, unlike CDA below) and mirrors `hl7_field_names.py`'s whole-field/component split - `SEGMENT_ELEMENT_NAMES` (e.g. `EQ-1` -> "Service Type Code") plus, since X12 handles a *repeating* composite by repeating the whole element position rather than a component within one fixed element the way HL7v2 never needs to here, **two** component tables instead of one: `_ELEMENT_COMPONENT_NAMES` for a composite pinned to one specific element number (`STC-1.1`/`STC-1.2` mean different things at the identical element number; `CLM-5.1`/`SV1-1.2`/`SV2-2.2`/`SV3-1.2` each their own single case) and `_ANY_ELEMENT_COMPONENT_NAMES` for one whose component meaning doesn't depend on which element carried it (`HI`'s own diagnosis-code sub-element, recurring at `HI01`/`HI02`/.../`HI0N` - one composite per diagnosis, per `app/edi/common.py::build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts`'s own `position` loop - `HI-1.2`/`HI-5.2`/`HI[1]-1.2` must all resolve to the identical "Diagnosis Code" label regardless of element number or `segment_repetition`). A handful of labels are deliberately shortened from the generic, dozens-of-segments-reused X12 element name to something legible in a narrow column (`EB-12`'s own generic "Yes/No Condition or Response Code" -> "In-Network Indicator"; `DTP-3`'s generic "Date Time Period" -> "Service Date", confirmed accurate across every real call site by checking `EdiLocator`'s own `DTP_SERVICE_DATE`-qualifier filter) - the same shortening precedent CX.1's "ID" (not "ID Number") already set. **`app/provenance/cda_field_names.py`** has no whole-path parser to reuse - `cda_locator.py`'s own docstring already discloses why a CDA location can't be parsed backward into a fixed grammar the way HL7v2/EDI's field-numbering shapes can (composed forward, one relative segment at a time, with no equivalent to `SEG-N.C`) - so `resolve_cda_field_label()` takes the raw string directly, splitting it itself and reusing only `cda_locator.py::parse_segment()` (already public) to strip `[N]`/`[LABEL]` brackets off the final piece. Since a bare `"@code"`/`"@value"` is hopelessly ambiguous alone (dozens of unrelated coded/timestamp fields across every section share the identical trailing attribute name), both are resolved by *(parent tag, attribute)* pair - `_CODE_ATTR_NAMES`/`_VALUE_ATTR_NAMES`, e.g. `value/@code` -> "Coded Value" but `administrativeGenderCode/@code` -> "Administrative Gender", `low/@value`/`high/@value` -> "Start Date/Time"/"End Date/Time" (the two `IVL_TS` bound tags `effective_time_location()`'s own three-shape resolution produces) - while `@displayName`/`@negationInd`/`@moodCode` are genuinely universal regardless of parent, confirmed by checking every real occurrence means the identical thing everywhere it appears in this app's own mappers. A bare trailing tag (`family`/`given`/`city`/`text`/`title`/`id`/`lotNumberText`/...) needs no parent context, the same "descend into this child, take its own text" shape `cda_locator.py`'s own docstring already documents. `resolver.py::_resolve_field_label` was rewritten from its previous hard `if source_format != "HL7v2": return None` gate into a proper three-way dispatch (HL7v2/EDI both parse-then-lookup via their own locator's parser, CDA resolves directly from the string) - `ProvenanceEntry.field_label`'s own docstring updated to match, dropping the now-stale "None for CDA/EDI" disclosure. `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`'s own former blanket `assert all(e.field_label is None for e in entries)` (now factually wrong - most CDA facts in that fixture DO resolve a label) was replaced with targeted per-field assertions instead. Verified via two new direct unit-test files (`tests/test_edi_field_names.py`/`test_cda_field_names.py`, mirroring `test_hl7_field_names.py`'s own component-wins-over-whole-field/unrecognized-returns-None coverage, plus dedicated cases for EDI's any-element-vs-pinned-element distinction and CDA's parent-tag-disambiguated `@code`/`@value` lookups) and dedicated content-correctness assertions added to the real fixture-driven tests in `test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`/`test_provenance_recorder_edi.py` (spot-checking `SV1-1.2` -> "Procedure Code", `STC-1.1`/`STC-1.2` -> their own distinct labels, a compound `"BHT-4+BHT-5"` location correctly resolving to no label since it isn't a real `edi_location()` shape, and CDA's own `low/@value`/`high/@value`/`value/@code`/`value/@displayName` cases against `ccd_basic.xml`'s real values) - plus a live Puppeteer pass against the real running app posting both `ccd_basic.xml` and `edi_270_basic.x12` to the Data Specification page, confirming every CDA row (25/25) and the expected majority of EDI rows (16/21, the remaining 5 being genuinely inferred/compound-location entries with no real label to show) render a populated "Source Field Name" column end-to-end, not just at the unit-test level.
  - **SIU instrumentation (`app/mappings/siu.py`, plus `build_practitioner_from_xcn`/`build_location_from_pl` in `app/mappings/common.py` and `build_codeable_concept_from_cwe` in `app/fhir_models/builders.py`)** - the second slice, and the first proof this architecture generalizes past ADT's own `Encounter`-only shape to a genuinely different resource (`Appointment`) with materialized participant resources (`Practitioner`/`Location`/`Device`). Every SIU trigger instrumented in one pass (all 6: S12/S13/S14/S15/S17/S26), the same "shared code, cheap to cover the whole family" situation ADT's own breadth already established. **`build_codeable_concept_from_cwe` gained its own optional `resource_id`/`relative_path`/`recorder` params and now records internally** (`.coding[0].code`/`.coding[0].display`) - centralized once, in the one shared builder, rather than requiring every caller (SCH-7/SCH-8/AIS-3 this slice; other CWE-shaped fields in future slices) to re-extract the same two components purely to record them. **A real, deliberately-chosen complication, not an oversight**: unlike ADT, this slice explicitly records the "one source field, two FHIR destinations" case the forward mapper's own architecture creates - AIP-3 (and AIL-3/AIG-3) is read once to materialize a real `Practitioner`/`Location`/`Device` resource *and* again (via `person_display`/`location_display`) to build the `Appointment.participant[].actor.display` string on a *different* resource - both facts are recorded, confirmed via `test_siu_s12_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values`'s own dedicated assertion that both entries exist, share the same source field, and land on two different `Bundle.entry[N]` resources. This required hoisting `appointment_id = str(uuid.uuid4())` in `BaseSiuMapper.to_bundle` *before* `_build_participants` runs (rather than letting `build_appointment_core` generate its own id, as it did before) - `_build_participants` needs a real Appointment id to record `participant[i].actor.display` against, before the Appointment itself exists yet, the same "hoist the id early enough to have something to record against" pattern ADT's own constructor-kwarg hoist already established, just one level higher up the call stack this time. **Two more dual-source-resolution cases, mirroring ADT's own PV1-44-or-EVN-2 pattern exactly**: `_timing_source_location`/`_tq1_duration_used` (new, private helpers in `siu.py`) each re-derive - purely for provenance, no change to the real resolution functions' own behavior - which of TQ1 or SCH-11/SCH-9 actually supplied `start`/`end`/`minutesDuration`, confirmed via `test_siu_s12_sch11_fallback_timing_points_at_sch_not_tq1` (no TQ1 segment present at all) and `test_siu_s13_duration_prefers_tq1_over_stale_sch9` (both present, TQ1 must win). **`Appointment.status` is `derivation="inferred"` for all 6 triggers**, the same reasoning as every ADT trigger's own status - a `status_reason` class attribute (mirroring `status`'s own existing class-attribute pattern on `_BookedSiuMapper`/`_UntimedSiuMapper`/`SiuS26Mapper`/`SiuS17Mapper`) supplies each trigger's own specific wording. `Appointment.comment` (the NTE-3 join) has no per-segment location concept the way a repeating *field* does - disclosed via a literal `"NTE-3 (×N segments)"` location string when more than one note was folded in, rather than fabricating a fake per-segment breakdown. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression test discipline ADT established (parametrized across 13 real SIU fixtures spanning all 6 triggers, including the edge-case fixtures for AIG-as-Location, AIP-id-only, multiple-NTE, partial-TQ1, and SCH-11 fallback) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`.
  - **ORU instrumentation (`app/mappings/oru.py`, plus `build_minimal_encounter` in `app/mappings/common.py`)** - the third slice, and mechanically the simplest so far: `BaseOruMapper` is non-trigger-polymorphic (see this file's own ORU extension-point notes above - all 5 triggers share identical logic with zero per-trigger overrides), so there's no `status_reason`-per-subclass pattern to write at all, just one shared code path covering R01/R30/R31/R32/R40 in a single pass. `build_minimal_encounter` (shared with MDM's own slice, see below) was instrumented the same "hoist constructor kwargs, record after" way `build_encounter_core` established - `status` is always `derivation="inferred"` (an ORU/MDM Encounter's own PV1 gives result/document context, never a real admit-discharge lifecycle signal to read a status from), `class.code`/`identifier[0].value` are direct from PV1-2/PV1-19, instrumenting MDM's own future Encounter "for free" the same way `build_patient`/`assemble_bundle` already instrument every type's Patient/Bundle fields for free. **A genuinely new grouping-relationship case, distinct from SIU's own single-flat-list AIP/AIL/AIG participants**: `DiagnosticReport`/`Observation` is this app's first *panel* shape in the crosswalk - one `OBR` group's worth of `Observation`s, built inside a `for obr, obx_segments in group_segments_by_leader(...)` loop, each independently recorded against its own freshly-hoisted `observation_id`/`report_id` (mirroring ADT's/SIU's own "hoist id, record after construction" rule at the per-loop-iteration level, not just once per mapper call) - confirmed via `test_oru_r01_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values`'s own dedicated per-report assertions (the CBC report's own status/code/effectiveDateTime/issued facts, and the GLU report's own separately-resolved status) that a two-report, three-observation fixture never cross-contaminates which fact belongs to which resource. **`_build_observation_value`'s four branches (ST/FT/TX -> `valueString`, NM -> `valueQuantity.value`+`.unit`, CE/CWE/CNE/IS -> `valueCodeableConcept` via the already-instrumented CWE builder, DT/DTM -> `valueDateTime`) each record against whichever key they actually return**, rather than a single generic "value" fact - since `Observation.value[x]` is a real FHIR choice type, the crosswalk's own `fhir_path` needs to name the specific chosen field (`valueQuantity.value`, not a vaguer `value`) to stay meaningful. The free-text branch's own already-established `raw_field_str`-not-`field_str` gotcha (a literal `^` in clinical/pathology free text must not be mistaken for a component separator) carries through unchanged into what the crosswalk records too - confirmed via a dedicated `test_oru_r01_free_text_value_with_caret_is_not_truncated_in_crosswalk` reusing the existing `oru_r01_ft_with_caret.hl7` fixture. **OBX-16's performer dedup (`_resolve_performer`/`performer_cache`) needed one small, deliberate provenance-specific rule**: `recorder` is threaded into `build_practitioner_from_xcn` only on a cache *miss* (a newly-built Practitioner) - a cache *hit* must not re-record, since the shared Practitioner's own fields were already correctly recorded the first time it was built, and doing it again on every subsequent hit would be harmless-but-wasteful at best and risk a spurious re-record of an unrelated OBX's own (identical) source location at worst; confirmed via `test_oru_r01_shared_performer_recorded_once_not_once_per_observation` that a message with two OBX segments sharing one OBX-16 physician produces exactly one Practitioner's worth of `name[0].family` facts, not two. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (parametrized across all 8 real ORU fixtures spanning all 5 triggers, including the shared-performer and caret-in-free-text edge cases) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`.
  - **MDM instrumentation (`app/mappings/mdm.py`)** - the fourth slice, and the one that completes full HL7v2 breadth for this pillar (all four message types this app converts - ADT/SIU/ORU/MDM - are now fully instrumented). `DocumentReference` is this app's first resource whose crosswalk needs to disclose a genuinely *hardcoded* (not merely inferred-from-absence, like ADT's status) field: `_resolve_status(txa)` never reads TXA-19's own value at all - it unconditionally returns `"current"` regardless of what's there (see the module's own docstring for why a fuller crosswalk isn't verified) - so `status` is recorded via `record_inferred` even in the one case (`"AV"`) where the hardcoded result happens to coincide with a real, verified mapping; the reason string says so explicitly rather than implying a value was read and happened to match. **A second "one source field, two FHIR destinations" case, the same shape SIU's own AIP-3 already established but now with a real complication SIU's own AIP never had**: TXA-9/TXA-10 (originator/authenticator) each independently produce both a materialized `Practitioner`'s own name (recorded inside the already-instrumented `build_practitioner_from_xcn`) and this `DocumentReference`'s own `author[0].display`/`authenticator.display` string (recorded here, in `build_document_reference`, against the `DocumentReference`'s own id) - confirmed via `test_mdm_t02_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values`'s own dedicated assertions that both facts exist, point at the same TXA field, and land on two different resources. **The real complication**: when TXA-10 identifies the same person as TXA-9 (the existing `originator_id == authenticator_id` dedup branch, reusing the single materialized Practitioner rather than building a second one), `authenticator.display` must still be recorded with `source_location = TXA-10` even though the underlying Practitioner resource itself was built from TXA-9 - getting this wrong (recording it against TXA-9, since that's the field that actually built the referenced Practitioner) would misattribute the display string's own real source, since `person_display(txa, 10)` - not 9 - is what actually built that particular string; confirmed via a dedicated `test_mdm_t02_same_author_authenticator_records_authenticator_display_from_txa10` against the real `mdm_t02_same_author_authenticator.hl7` fixture. **`Binary.data` (the document body, built by joining every TX/FT-typed OBX-5 line) reuses the identical "disclose the segment count, don't fabricate a per-segment breakdown" precedent SIU's own `Appointment.comment`/NTE join already established** - a literal `"OBX-5 (×N segments)"` location string when more than one line was folded in, a plain `hl7_location("OBX", 5)` when there's exactly one. The recorded `value` is the joined plaintext body itself, not the base64-encoded/raw-bytes form `Binary.data` actually stores it in (see this file's own `fhir.resources` notes on that gotcha) - a human reading the crosswalk wants the readable text, not an encoding artifact. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (parametrized across all 4 real MDM fixtures, one per remaining trigger T04/T06/T08/T10/T11 reusing the T02 basic fixture the same way ORU's own R30-R40 breadth coverage reuses R01's shared logic) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`, including a dedicated free-text-caret-preservation test mirroring ORU's own.
  - **C-CDA instrumentation (`app/provenance/location.py::xpath_location`, `app/cda/common.py`, `app/cda/problems.py`)** - the fifth slice, and the first proof this architecture generalizes to a genuinely different input format: XML via stdlib `ElementTree`, not HL7v2's pipe-delimited segments. `xpath_location(*segments) -> str` (`xpath_location("recordTarget", "patientRole", "patient", "name[0]", "family")` -> `"recordTarget/patientRole/patient/name[0]/family"`) is the real design work `location.py`'s own Phase-0-era docstring flagged as deferred: `ElementTree` has no parent pointers, so an XPath-shaped location can't be reconstructed backward from a bare `Element` the way `resolve_bundle_paths` reconstructs a FHIR path forward from a resource's own `Bundle.entry[N]` index - it has to be composed *forward*, at each call site, from the same relative-path segments that call site is already walking via `find_child`/`find_all`. **A deliberate, disclosed design choice this slice made, not a partial implementation of the Phase-0 docstring's original idea**: `find_child`/`find_all` themselves were NOT modified to thread a path parameter through automatically - every currently-shipped CDA section builder (Medications, Allergies, Immunizations, Vitals, Results, Procedures, Discharge Medications) would have needed touching just to keep accepting an unused parameter, for zero benefit this slice (only Problems is instrumented). Each instrumented function instead composes its own path strings directly from its own known-static traversal structure, exactly mirroring how `app/mappings/adt.py` already hardcodes `hl7_location("PID", 5, ...)` at each of its own call sites rather than deriving it from a generic tree-walking substrate - `xpath_location` standardizes the *formatting* (segment joining, `"[i]"` 0-based repetition indices at any depth, `"@attr"` for attribute-derived values), not the path-derivation mechanism itself. `app/cda/common.py::build_patient_from_header`/`build_encounter_from_header`/`assemble_bundle`/`build_sectioned_bundle` and `app/cda/problems.py::build_condition`/`build_conditions` are the real instrumented functions; `build_identifiers`/`_build_patient_names`/`_build_patient_addresses`/`_build_patient_telecoms` each track `(source_index, value)` pairs through their own list-building filters (mirroring HL7v2's own established "avoid array-index desync when some components are empty" discipline) so a kept FHIR array index never desyncs from the XML repetition it actually came from. A new shared helper, `app/cda/common.py::effective_time_location(base_path, element, bound)`, re-derives - purely for provenance, no change to `ivl_ts_bounds`' own resolution - which of `IVL_TS`'s three legal shapes (`bare @value`, `<low>`/`<high>` children, `nullFlavor`) a given `effectiveTime` element actually used, the CDA-format mirror of `app/mappings/adt.py`'s PV1-44-vs-EVN-2 dual-source-resolution pattern; used by both `build_encounter_from_header`'s own `period.start`/`.end` and `build_condition`'s own `onsetDateTime`/`abatementDateTime`, its second real consumer justifying the "extract into common.py, not module-private" placement from the start rather than waiting for a later duplication to force it. `_resolve_clinical_status` was changed to return `(CodeableConcept | None, str | None)` - the resolved status alongside which of its own two checked vocabularies (a nested Status Observation, or the Concern Act's own `statusCode`) actually produced it - the CDA mirror of ADT's own `_drop_evn2_period_start_fallback` "report which branch really fired" discipline. **`SECTION_BUILDERS`' own generic dispatch loop in `build_sectioned_bundle` required every registered section-builder function across 8 sibling files (Medications/Allergies/Immunizations/Vitals/Results/Procedures/Hospital Discharge Diagnosis/Discharge Medications) to accept a `recorder=None` parameter uniformly, even though only Problems' own is acted on this slice** - the identical "every message type's own `to_bundle()` accepts a recorder even before its own slice ships" precedent Phase 0 already established for SIU/ORU/MDM, just one level lower (per-section rather than per-message-type) since C-CDA's registry dispatches sections generically through one shared loop rather than one `to_bundle()` per type. **A real, deliberate "for free" extension found while wiring this dispatch, not assumed**: `app/cda/hospital_discharge_diagnosis.py::build_hospital_discharge_diagnoses` threads `recorder` straight into the *reused* `problems.py::build_condition` (rather than accepting-and-ignoring it like the other 7 section builders), since that section's own entry shape is byte-for-byte identical to Problems' (see that module's own docstring) and the recorded location strings (`"act/entryRelationship[...]/..."`) stay accurate regardless of which outer Act template wraps the shared inner shape - confirmed directly against the real `discharge_summary_basic.xml` fixture, whose own Hospital Discharge Diagnosis Condition (`category="encounter-diagnosis"`) gets real `code`/`clinicalStatus` facts recorded exactly like its sibling plain-Problems Condition in the same document, with no extra code needed. `app/cda/base.py::CdaDocumentBuilder.build_bundle` (the ABC) and all three document builders (`CcdBuilder`/`DischargeSummaryBuilder`/`HistoryAndPhysicalBuilder`) gained a `recorder=None` parameter threaded straight into `build_sectioned_bundle`, mirroring `MessageMapper.to_bundle`'s own Phase-0 signature change. `app/cda/validation.py::_resolve_trigger_event` was promoted to public `resolve_trigger_event` once `app/provenance/dispatch.py` became its second real consumer, needing the identical `"CCD"`/`"DISCHARGESUMMARY"`/`"HISTORYANDPHYSICAL"` resolution `ValidationReport.trigger_event` already used. **`app/provenance/dispatch.py`'s own CDA branch is a genuinely different shape from its HL7v2 sibling, not a copy**: since C-CDA's own "fully instrumented" bar is per-*section* (Problems), not per-*document-type* (a document type also carries Medications/Allergies/Immunizations/Vitals/Results/Procedures, none of which are instrumented yet), no CDA document type is ever added to an `_INSTRUMENTED_...` set the way an HL7v2 `message_type` is - `unsupported` stays unconditionally `True` for every CDA document this phase, even though `entries` is now genuinely non-empty for a document with a header and/or a Problems section, the identical "some real facts, still reported unsupported" shape a not-yet-instrumented HL7v2 message type already exhibited before ORU/MDM's own slices shipped. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (parametrized across 16 real CDA fixtures spanning all three document types, including several whose own section - Medications/Allergies/Vitals/Results/Procedures/Immunizations - is deliberately NOT instrumented yet, proving the no-op `recorder=None` additions to those 8 sibling files don't alter their own output either) plus content-correctness tests in the new `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`.
  - **C-CDA Medications instrumentation (`app/cda/medications.py`, `app/cda/discharge_medications.py`)** - a follow-up slice after every EDI family reached full breadth, and the first proof the C-CDA branch of this pillar generalizes past a single section (Problems). Instruments `_build_dosage`/`build_medication_request`/`build_medication_requests` field-for-field: `medicationCodeableConcept.coding[0].code`/`.display` (from the nested `consumable/manufacturedProduct/manufacturedMaterial/code`, the identical "extract attrs directly from the element handle already in scope" style `build_condition`'s own `value` extraction already established, rather than adding recorder params to the shared `build_codeable_concept_from_cd`), `status`/`intent` (each direct when `statusCode`/`moodCode` resolves to a recognized `STATUS_MAP`/`_MOOD_TO_INTENT` entry, inferred with the disclosed fallback reason otherwise - the same "direct when read, inferred when the same table's own fallback fired" distinction `build_encounter_from_header`'s own class-code recording already established), and `_build_dosage`'s own four branches (`route`, `doseAndRate[0].doseQuantity`/`.rateQuantity`, `timing.repeat.boundsPeriod.start`/`.end` via the already-public `effective_time_location`, and `patientInstruction` from the free-text-SIG nested `substanceAdministration`) - confirmed via `test_ccd_medications_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values`'s own dedicated assertions that the real fixture's two entries (one structured-dosing, one free-text-SIG) each record their own branch and no others. **A real, disclosed location-string simplification found while wiring this, not assumed away**: unlike Problems' own `build_condition` (reused by Hospital Discharge Diagnosis, where both callers genuinely have an outer `<act>` wrapper, just different templateIds, so a hardcoded `"act/..."` base is accurate for both), Medications' own plain section has *no* wrapping Act at all (`entry/substanceAdministration` directly) while Discharge Medications wraps the identical `substanceAdministration` one level deeper inside its own Act - so no single hardcoded base prefix is literally accurate for both of `build_medication_request`'s own two callers. Resolved via `_ENTRY_BASE = "substanceAdministration"`, accurate for the primary/first consumer (plain Medications) and close-enough-to-be-useful for the reused Discharge Medications case, rather than threading a third parameter through the whole dosage-building chain to disambiguate two callers for a cosmetic difference in the recorded location string - a deliberate, disclosed tradeoff, not an oversight. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the fixture parametrization to `ccd_medications_basic.xml`/`ccd_medications_negated.xml`) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`, including a dedicated proof that `build_discharge_medication_requests` (via `discharge_summary_basic.xml`'s own real Discharge Medications entry) gets the identical recorded facts a plain-Medications-sourced entry would, and that a negated entry (which produces no `MedicationRequest` at all) leaves zero `medicationCodeableConcept`/`dosageInstruction`-scoped facts anywhere in the resolved crosswalk.
  - **C-CDA Allergies instrumentation (`app/cda/allergies.py`)** - a second follow-up slice after Medications, and the deepest C-CDA section instrumented so far - it needs to reconstruct three levels of nested Status/Criticality/Reaction(+Severity) Observations, not just one flat entry shape.
