Instruments `_resolve_clinical_status`/`_build_reaction`/`_build_reactions`/`_build_allergy_intolerance` field-for-field: the allergen itself (`code.coding[0].code`/`.display`, read directly off the already-constructed `CodeableConcept` object rather than re-parsing the XML element a second time, a minor implementation simplification safe here since `build_codeable_concept_from_cd` never transforms code/display values, only wraps them), `clinicalStatus` (changed `_resolve_clinical_status`'s own return type to `tuple[CodeableConcept, str | None]`, the identical "report which branch really fired" discipline `problems.py`'s own two-vocabulary resolution and `adt.py`'s PV1-44-vs-EVN-2 resolution already established - direct when a real Status Observation resolves, inferred with the IG's own disclosed fixed `"active"` default otherwise), `type`/`category[0]` (the same "one source field, two FHIR destinations" case SIU's AIP-3/MDM's TXA-9/CCD Medications' own status-and-intent pair already established, both sourced from the identical `<value>` element), `criticality`, `onsetDateTime`, `recordedDate`, and each reaction's own `manifestation[0].coding[0].code`/`.display`/`.onset`/`.severity`. **A genuinely new, deliberately negation-aware inferred field, distinct from every earlier slice's own**: a negated entry's `code.text` (the IG's own disclosed "No known allergy to X"/"No known allergies" text pattern, see `_resolve_allergen_code`'s own docstring) is recorded via `record_inferred`, not `record`, even for the still-resolvable-allergen case that carries a real display name inside its constructed text - the value genuinely isn't copied from one source field, it's built from IG-disclosed prose. **A real, proactively-applied fix for a collision risk no existing fixture happens to exercise, the identical discipline 837D's own diagnosis-loop fix already established for EDI**: an allergy entry can carry more than one Reaction Observation, and a bare, non-indexed reaction location string would record the *identical* location for every reaction's own facts (the same shape 837I's own multi-`HI`-segment diagnoses collision already proved is a real hazard, not a hypothetical one) - `_reaction_base(index)` disambiguates via a `entryRelationship[MFST][{index}]` bracket-index segment, applied even though neither real Allergies fixture currently carries more than one reaction to actually trip over it. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the fixture parametrization to `ccd_allergies_basic.xml`'s existing entry plus newly-added `ccd_allergies_no_known.xml`) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`, including a dedicated proof that the negated fixture's own fully-unresolvable-allergen case records both `code.text` and `clinicalStatus` as inferred with no coded-allergen fact at all, while the basic fixture's own real Status Observation and reaction both record as direct facts at their own genuinely deeper, distinct source locations.
  - **C-CDA Immunizations instrumentation (`app/cda/immunizations.py`)** - a third follow-up slice after Medications/Allergies, structurally simpler than Allergies (no wrapping Act, no nested Status/Criticality Observations - the identical bare-`substanceAdministration` shape Medications already established) but with its own genuinely different status-resolution shape. Instruments `_build_immunization` field-for-field: `vaccineCode.coding[0].code`/`.display`, `status`, `occurrenceDateTime`/`occurrenceString`, `lotNumber`, `route.coding[0].code`, `doseQuantity.value`. **`status`'s own negation branch is recorded as `direct`, not `inferred`, a deliberate distinction from ADT's own A08 status precedent**: unlike A08 (where only a field's mere *presence*, not its value, drives the inferred status), `negationInd`'s own real attribute *value* (`"true"`) is what's read and directly drives `"not-done"` - a genuine source field genuinely read, so it gets `record(..., xpath_location(_ENTRY_BASE, "@negationInd"), ...)` rather than `record_inferred`, distinguishing this from the non-negated branch's own real inferred case (`statusCode` absent/unrecognized, falling back to the disclosed `"completed"` default). `occurrenceString`'s own `"Unknown"` fallback is `record_inferred` with no source location at all, the module's own disclosed "regenerate the identical fallback rather than fabricate a fake timestamp" precedent made real in the crosswalk. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (the existing `ccd_immunizations_basic.xml` fixture was already in the parametrization) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`, including a dedicated proof that the fixture's third, INT-mood planned entry produces zero `vaccineCode` facts anywhere in the resolved crosswalk (only two, matching the two real `Immunization` resources actually built).
  - **C-CDA Vital Signs instrumentation (`app/cda/vitals.py`)** - a fourth follow-up slice, and the second real proof (after HL7v2 ORU's own `DiagnosticReport`/`Observation` grouping) that this pillar can reconstruct a genuine panel/member grouping relationship, not just a flat entry list - here, an `Observation` panel whose own `.hasMember` references separately-recorded member `Observation`s. Instruments `_build_vital_sign_observation`/`build_vital_signs` field-for-field: each member's `code.coding[0].code`/`.display`, `effectiveDateTime`, `valueQuantity.value`/`.unit`, `interpretation[0].coding[0].code`, `method.coding[0].code`, `bodySite.coding[0].code`, plus the panel's own `effectiveDateTime` (direct, from the organizer's own `effectiveTime`, via `effective_time_location`). **The first section where `status`/`category` are recorded as fixed/inferred for *every* Observation this section builds, panel and member alike** - centralized in one small `_record_fixed_status_and_category` helper rather than four independently-drifting `record_inferred` call sites, since C-CDA fixes both per the spec itself (not just this app's own disclosed default), the one CCD section reversed so far with literally no status-mapping ambiguity at all. The panel's own `code.coding[0].code="85353-1"` is likewise recorded inferred - fixed per the IG's own table, never read from the organizer's own narrative-only `/code`. **A real, proactively-applied disambiguation for a genuine multi-member collision, the same class of fix Allergies'/837I's own multi-entry location strings already needed**: an organizer commonly wraps more than one member `Observation`, so `_member_base(index)` threads the raw `<component>` position (via `enumerate()` in `build_vital_signs`'s own loop, over every component found, not just the successfully-built ones - preserving the real XML position even if an earlier sibling were skipped) into every member fact's own location (`organizer/component[{i}]/observation/...`), rather than letting two different members' facts collide on an identical, ambiguous location string - confirmed via a dedicated test that the real fixture's two members (Heart Rate at `component[0]`, Body Temperature at `component[1]`) resolve to genuinely distinct source locations. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (the existing `ccd_vitals_basic.xml` fixture was already in the parametrization) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`.
  - **C-CDA Results instrumentation (`app/cda/results.py`)** - a fifth follow-up slice, the same panel/member grouping shape Vital Signs already proved, wrapped in `DiagnosticReport` instead of a panel `Observation` - and, unlike Vitals' own fixed status, a genuinely variable one shared by both the report and each member. Instruments `_build_result_observation`/`_build_observation_value`/`_build_reference_range`/`build_diagnostic_reports` field-for-field: `status` (via a new shared `_record_status` helper - direct when a recognized `STATUS_MAP` code drove it, inferred with the disclosed `"unknown"` fallback otherwise, reused identically for both the report's own `organizer/statusCode` and each member's own `.../observation/statusCode`), `code.coding[0].code`/`.display` for both report and member (the report's own code falls back to `record_inferred`'s disclosed `"Unspecified result panel"` placeholder text when the organizer's own `/code` doesn't resolve, mirroring 837I's own revenue-code-text-fallback precedent), `effectiveDateTime` for both, and each of `_build_observation_value`'s own four recordable branches (`valueQuantity.value`/`.unit` for `PQ`/`REAL`, `valueCodeableConcept.coding[0].code` for the `CD`-family types, `valueInteger`, `valueString`) - recorded against the real chosen field, not a vaguer generic `"value"`, the identical discipline ORU's own OBX-5 value-type dispatch already established on the HL7v2 side. **`referenceRange[]` is this app's first repeating *nested* array within a member Observation to need its own index disambiguation on top of the member's own** (`referenceRange[{i}].low.value`/`.high.value`, each independently indexed via `enumerate()` over the source XML's own repeating `referenceRange` elements) - a two-level indexing scheme (`component[{member_index}]` for which member, `referenceRange[{range_index}]` for which range within that member) neither Vitals nor any earlier section needed at once. Reuses Vitals' own `_member_base(index)` disambiguation shape directly (re-derived locally, not imported, matching this app's own per-module `_ENTRY_BASE`-style precedent for small location-string helpers that don't yet have a second cross-module consumer). Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (the existing `ccd_results_basic.xml` fixture was already in the parametrization) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`, including a dedicated proof that the fixture's own `PQ`-valued member (with a `referenceRange`) and free-text `ST`-valued member both record their own real value-choice fact and no others, at genuinely distinct source locations.

Instruments `_build_procedure`/`build_procedures` field-for-field: `status` (via a new `_record_status` helper mirroring Immunizations' own negation-vs-table distinction exactly - `negationInd`'s own real value drives `"not-done"` directly, a recognized `statusCode` drives its mapped value directly, anything else falls back to the disclosed `"unknown"` default, inferred), `code.coding[0].code`/`.display` (only recorded when present at all, since `Procedure.code` is genuinely optional per FHIR R4 - confirmed via `model_fields`, matching this module's own pre-existing "still build the resource without one" precedent), `identifier[i].value` (reusing `build_identifiers`' own already-public `resource_id`/`location_prefix`/`recorder` parameters directly - added during the Data Specification header slice for patient/encounter ids, this is its first section-level real consumer, needing zero changes to cover it), and `performedDateTime` **or** `performedPeriod.start`/`.end` (the mutually-exclusive branches the IG itself draws, mirrored via a plain `xpath_location` fact for the bare-`@value` point-in-time branch and `effective_time_location` for the low/high period branch - the identical "two genuinely different source shapes for one target concept" pattern every earlier section's own `effectiveTime` handling already established), plus `bodySite[0].coding[0].code`. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (the existing `ccd_procedures_basic.xml` fixture was already in the parametrization) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`, including a dedicated proof that the fixture's own two entries - a completed point-in-time Appendectomy with a root+extension identifier, and a negated period-bounded Colonoscopy with a root-only identifier - each record the correct branch of every mutually-exclusive field and none of the other. **A real design decision made and disclosed, not silently assumed, once every registered section builder was instrumented**: `app/provenance/dispatch.py`'s own `unsupported` flag deliberately stays unconditionally `True` for every CDA document even now, rather than graduating to a per-document-type `_INSTRUMENTED_...`-style set the way HL7v2/EDI's own binary bar works - see `_CDA_UNSUPPORTED_REASON`'s own updated comment for the full reasoning. **This disclosure itself needed revisiting once `app/cda/narrative_sections.py` shipped**: Discharge Summary's Hospital Course/Plan of Treatment and History and Physical's own nine required narrative sections are no longer sections this app's forward-conversion pillar fails to map at all - they now convert to a `DocumentReference`+`Binary` pair each - but `build_narrative_document_reference` doesn't record anything with the `recorder` it already accepts (a disclosed, deliberate follow-up of its own, not attempted alongside the section itself), so the crosswalk still can't show where any of these sections' own facts came from. The `unsupported=True` disclosure stays correct for a narrower, updated reason: unlike an HL7v2 message type or EDI transaction set (where every field the standard defines for that type is either read or a narrow, disclosed per-field gap), a CDA document type's own provenance coverage is keyed at the *section* level, and these ten sections (Hospital Course/Plan of Treatment/the eight remaining H&P-specific ones) are now converted-but-not-yet-instrumented, the identical shape ORU/MDM's own HL7v2 message types were in before their own provenance slices shipped - graduating any document type to "fully supported" here would still overclaim relative to what this pillar's own coverage actually guarantees.
  - **EDI 270/271 instrumentation (`app/provenance/location.py::edi_location`, `app/edi/common.py`, `app/edi/eligibility_270.py`, `app/edi/eligibility_271.py`)** - the sixth slice, and the first proof this architecture generalizes to X12's own delimited-text shape with self-describing (per-file) delimiters - a third genuinely different parsing substrate after HL7v2's pipe-delimited fields and C-CDA's XML tree. `edi_location(segment_id, element_num, *, component=None) -> str` (`edi_location("NM1", 9)` -> `"NM1-9"`) is deliberately the *same* `SEGMENT-element[.component]` shape as `hl7_location()`, not a new convention - X12's own element numbering (`NM109`) is already positional exactly like HL7v2's fields, the one structural difference being that X12 handles repetition via repeated *segments* (multiple `NM1`s, one per loop) rather than a repeating element within one segment, so there's no `repetition` parameter the way `hl7_location()` has one. Every NM1/DMG-shaped builder shared across the whole EDI package - `build_organization_from_nm1`/`build_practitioner_from_nm1`/`build_patient_from_nm1_dmg`/`_build_nm1_identifier`/`build_coverage`/`build_service_type_category`/`assemble_bundle` in `app/edi/common.py` - gained real instrumentation in this one pass, since 270 and 271 (unlike C-CDA's per-section sprawl) share almost their entire loop-walk/NM1/DMG/header machinery, the same "shared code, cheap to cover both" situation SIU's own single-pass 6-trigger breadth already established. **A genuinely new "hardcoded, not merely inferred-from-absence" case, distinct from every earlier slice's own inferred fields**: `Coverage.status` is unconditionally `"active"` for every Coverage this app has ever built (270/271 today; 278/837P once their own slices ship) - `build_coverage` was instrumented once, in the one shared function, benefiting every present and future caller identically, with `record_inferred`'s own reason making explicit that no X12 field in *any* family this converter reads carries a real policy-status value at all. **`build_service_type_category` needed a different centralization shape than CDA's `build_codeable_concept_from_cd`/HL7's `build_codeable_concept_from_cwe`**: those two receive the raw segment and extract code/display themselves, but this function is only ever handed the already-extracted code string (270's `EQ-1` and 271's `EB-3` are two different segment/element pairs for the identical target field) - so `source_location` is a caller-supplied parameter here instead of composed internally, a deliberate, disclosed departure from the two established precedents, not an inconsistency. **`_build_items_and_serviced_date`/`_build_insurance_items` both needed the request/response resource's own id hoisted *before* they run** (mirroring SIU's own `appointment_id` hoist, ADT's own encounter-id hoist) - each EQ/EB group becomes its own `item[i]` fact scoped to that not-yet-constructed resource. 271's own `insurance[0].inforce` (aggregated - true if *any* EB01="1" across every EB group for the patient, not a single segment's value) is the first boolean fact this pillar has recorded - confirmed via `ProvenanceEntry.value`'s own `str | None` type that pydantic stringifies it (`"True"`/`"False"`), not a bug, the same "value is always a display string" contract every other recorded fact already follows; when no EB01="1" is found, it's `derivation="inferred"` (nothing drove the value), and when one is, it's `derivation="direct"` pointing at that segment's own `EB-1` — the same "some real facts, still not fully instrumented" shape ORU/MDM's own recorder exhibited before their own slices shipped, but never quite as a boolean before. 271's own `.outcome`/`.disposition` mirror the identical "inferred common-case default vs. direct when a real AAA rejection drove it" pattern the module's own docstring already discloses in prose - now made real in the crosswalk too. **Every other registered EDI builder (276/277, 278, 835, 837P/837I/837D) gained a no-op `recorder=None` parameter**, the identical "accept it now, act on it later" precedent CDA's 8 sibling `SECTION_BUILDERS` files and Phase 0's own SIU/ORU/MDM `to_bundle()` signatures already established - **six of those seven thread `recorder` straight into their own shared `assemble_bundle()` call, giving every one of them free `Bundle.identifier`/`.timestamp` facts from their own BHT segment**, mirroring `build_patient`/`assemble_bundle`'s own "free" PID/MSH-fact precedent exactly; **835 is the one deliberate exception** - it has no `BHT` segment at all (a genuine structural fact about that family, already disclosed elsewhere in this file) and uses its own separate, still-uninstrumented `_assemble_835_bundle`, so it gets zero facts, not even free ones, confirmed directly via `test_edi_835_non_instrumented_family_with_no_bht_gets_zero_facts`. `app/provenance/dispatch.py`'s own EDI branch was rebuilt from a bare `convert_edi_to_bundle(raw_text)` passthrough into the identical tiny parse-then-dispatch shape its HL7v2/CDA siblings already use (`parse_interchange` -> `first_transaction_set` -> `get_transaction_builder` -> thread `recorder` -> `resolve_bundle_paths`), gated by a new `_INSTRUMENTED_TRANSACTION_SETS = {"270", "271"}` set checked against the transaction set's own `st01` - the EDI-format mirror of `_INSTRUMENTED_MESSAGE_TYPES`, at transaction-set-family granularity rather than CDA's own finer per-section one, since (unlike a C-CDA document type's independently-registered sections) a 270 or 271 transaction set is one self-contained resource shape that genuinely can become "fully instrumented" in a single pass, the same way each HL7v2 message type already did. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (parametrized across all 4 real 270/271 fixtures plus 4 fixtures from other, still-uninstrumented EDI families - proving both the no-op `recorder=None` additions AND the "free" `assemble_bundle` facts don't alter any of their own output) plus content-correctness tests in the new `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`.
  - **EDI 276/277 instrumentation (`app/edi/claim_status.py`)** - the seventh slice, reusing every NM1/DMG/BHT builder 270/271 already instrumented in `app/edi/common.py` directly (payer/receiver/provider/subscriber/dependent all resolve through the identical shared functions) - only this family's own `Task`-specific fields needed new work: `_build_status_concept`/`_resolve_task_status` (STC01's `category:status` composite, split via `component()` the same way 270/271 never needed to), and `_build_tasks_for_patient_loop`'s own per-`TRN`-group id-hoisting (mirroring 270's own per-`EQ`-group `item[i]` hoist, one level further down - a `Task` per claim-status group, not just an `item[]` entry within one resource). **`Task.status`'s own reversal is the interesting part, genuinely different from 270/271's own "always active" `Coverage.status`**: for 276 (request), `status` stays the constructor's own literal `"requested"` - `record_inferred`, since 276 carries no `STC` at all (confirmed directly, not assumed, from the module's own docstring: STC is response-only); for 277 (response), `_resolve_task_status` always overwrites it, either `derivation="direct"` from `STC-1.1`'s own category-prefix lookup when an `STC` segment is present, or `derivation="inferred"` (defaulting to `"completed"`) on the one occurrence within a nominally-STC-bearing family where a specific claim-status group still lacks its own `STC` - the identical "direct when read, inferred when the same table's own fallback fired with nothing to read" distinction `build_encounter_from_header`'s own class-code recording already established for CDA. **Two more hardcoded-and-disclosed fields, following `Coverage.status`'s own precedent from the 270/271 slice**: `Task.intent="order"` and `Task.code.text="Claim Status"` are both fixed literals with no X12 source field at all, each `record_inferred` once per `Task` with its own plain-English reason rather than left silently unexplained. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the same 270/271 fixture parametrization to `edi_276_basic.x12`/`_no_dependent.x12` and `edi_277_basic.x12`/`_error_status.x12`) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`, including a dedicated proof that `edi_277_error_status.x12`'s own `"E1"` category resolves directly to `Task.status="failed"` via `STC_CATEGORY_PREFIX_TO_TASK_STATUS`'s own `"E"` entry, not the completed/inferred default.
  - **EDI 278 instrumentation (`app/edi/prior_auth.py`, plus `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` in `app/edi/common.py`)** - the eighth slice, reusing 270/271's own shared NM1/DMG/BHT/`Coverage` builders directly (per this family's own module docstring, its 2000A-2000D loop shape is genuinely, not coincidentally, identical to 270/271's) - the real new work is `Claim`/`ClaimResponse`'s own fields. `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` (shared with `claim_837p.py`, not yet instrumented) gained `resource_id`/`relative_path_prefix`/`recorder` params that hardcode the `.diagnosisCodeableConcept.coding[0].code` wrapper shape internally, a deliberate, disclosed exception to this pillar's usual "caller composes its own relative path" convention - safe here since both of this function's real consumers (278 today, 837P once its own slice ships) target the identical `Claim.diagnosis[]` field. **The largest single count of hardcoded/inferred fields of any slice so far**: `Claim.status`/`.type.coding[0].code`/`.use`/`.priority.text` and `ClaimResponse.status`/`.use` are all fixed literals with no X12 source field at all (matching `UM01`'s own disclosed "no clean FHIR target" gap and `Coverage.status`'s own precedent), each `record_inferred` with its own specific reason - `Claim.item[0].productOrService` is the one field with a genuine two-way split, direct from `UM03` via the already-instrumented `build_service_type_category` when a `UM` segment is present, inferred (`"Unspecified service"` placeholder text) when it's absent, confirmed via dedicated tests against both the UM-less basic request fixture and the UM-bearing certified-response fixture. **A real formatting bug caught and fixed before commit, not shipped and found later**: `ClaimResponse.created` reuses `claim.created` directly rather than re-parsing `BHT04`/`05` a second time (a deliberate efficiency this module's own docstring already discloses) - but `claim.created`, read back off the resource *after* construction, is a `datetime.datetime` object pydantic normalized the original FHIR-formatted string into, not the string itself; recording it via `str()` (Python's own default `datetime` rendering, space-separated: `"2026-08-12 11:02:00+00:00"`) would have silently mismatched the identical fact's own differently-formatted sibling on the `Claim` (`"2026-08-12T11:02:00Z"`) - caught by directly inspecting the resolved crosswalk output before writing the test suite, not by the tests themselves; fixed via `created.isoformat().replace("+00:00", "Z")`, verified as a general, correct reconstruction of `parse_hl7_datetime`'s own output format (not just a common-case patch) since Python's `isoformat()` on an aware `datetime` always emits a `+HH:MM`/`-HH:MM` suffix, never `Z`, so this substitution recovers the exact original string whether the source offset was UTC or not. `ClaimResponse.item[0].adjudication[]`'s own two independently-indexed entries (`HCR01` action code, `HCR03` reason code) mirror `claim_status.py`'s own multi-coding `businessStatus` pattern from the 276/277 slice exactly. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the fixture parametrization to all 4 real 278 fixtures - basic request, with-dependent request, certified response, denied response) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`, including a dedicated regression proof that `Claim.created` and `ClaimResponse.created` resolve to the identical displayed value.
  - **EDI 835 instrumentation (`app/edi/remittance_835.py`)** - the ninth slice, and the first EDI provenance slice for a family with **no `HL` hierarchy and no `BHT` segment at all** - a flat `BPR`/`TRN`/`N1`(payer)/`N1`(payee)/`CLP`... shape, genuinely simpler to instrument than every HL-hierarchy-based family this app has instrumented so far, needing no loop-resolution helper at all. Instruments `_build_organization_from_n1`/`_build_n1_identifier`/`_build_detail`/`_assemble_835_bundle`/`Edi835Builder.build_bundle` field-for-field: `N1`'s own simpler shape (no first/last name split, `N102`/`N103`/`N104` recorded directly rather than reusing `edi_common.py`'s `NM1`-scoped helpers, matching this module's own pre-existing "not a reuse of `build_organization_from_nm1`" precedent), `BPR02`/`BPR16` (payment amount/date), `TRN02` (trace number, `Bundle.identifier`'s own source here since there's no `BHT` to derive it from), and one `detail[i]` fact set per `PaymentReconciliationDetail`. **`BPR16` produces two independent facts on the same resource**, the same "one source field, two FHIR destinations" case SIU's AIP-3/MDM's TXA-9 already established, just landing on two fields of one resource this time (`PaymentReconciliation.created` and `.paymentDate`) rather than two different resources. **Money fields are recorded from the raw source text, not the constructed `Money.value` Decimal** - `paymentAmount.value`/`detail[i].amount.value` both record `element(bpr, 2)`/`element(clp, 4)` directly rather than re-stringifying the parsed `Decimal`, avoiding any risk of the exact trailing-zero-loss class of bug `app/transform/`'s own adversarial review already caught once for this app's reverse-direction money formatting (see that section's own disclosed fix). `PaymentReconciliation.status="active"` is the same category of hardcoded/inferred field `Coverage.status`/278's own `Claim.status` already established - no X12 field in this family carries a real transaction-status value either. **`.outcome`, `.paymentIssuer`, `.requestor` are deliberately never recorded**: `.outcome` is never set at all (disclosed in the module's own docstring - 835 has no source field for it), and the two reference fields are internal UUID wiring to the already-recorded payer/payee Organizations, the same "don't record reference wiring itself" precedent every earlier slice already established. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the fixture parametrization to both real 835 fixtures - a single-claim basic fixture and a two-claim fixture with one paid and one `$0.00` claim, confirming a zero-but-present amount is still recorded, not skipped) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`, including a dedicated assertion that `Bundle.timestamp` is genuinely absent (835's own disclosed permanent gap, not an oversight).
  - **EDI 837P instrumentation (`app/edi/claim_837p.py`)** - the tenth slice, and the first EDI provenance slice for the deepest, most cross-referential loop shape of any EDI phase this app has instrumented so far: a claim-level `HI` diagnosis list cross-referenced by position from each service line's own diagnosis-pointer composite, plus a rendering provider (`Claim.careTeam[]`) distinct from the billing provider (`Claim.provider`). **Payer/billing-provider/rendering-provider resolution reuses the identical `build_organization_from_nm1`/`build_practitioner_from_nm1`/`build_patient_from_nm1_dmg`/`build_coverage` builders 270/271/276/277/278 already instrumented** - only this family's own `Claim.item[]`/diagnosis-pointer/care-team fields needed new work. **The first slice to record a list-valued fact as a joined string**: `item[i].diagnosisSequence` (`SV1-07`'s own up-to-4 1-based pointers into `Claim.diagnosis[]`) is recorded as a comma-joined string (`"1,2"`) against its own single `SV1-7` source location, rather than one fact per pointer - a deliberate, disclosed choice mirroring `Binary.data`'s own "one fact for a value built from several source pieces" precedent, since `ProvenanceEntry.value` is a single display string and a resolved pointer list has no natural per-position FHIR sub-path the way a repeating array element does. **`item[i].locationCodeableConcept.coding[0].code` is a genuinely new "one claim-wide source, N per-item destinations" case**: `CLM05-1` (Place of Service) is read once per claim but applied identically to every service line, so every `item[i]` gets its own recorded fact pointing at the identical `CLM-5.1` source location - confirmed via `test_edi_837p_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values`'s own dedicated assertion that both of that fixture's two items record this fact independently. `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts`'s own `resource_id`/`relative_path_prefix`/`recorder` params (added during 278's own slice, once `claim_837p.py` was already identified as its second real consumer) needed no changes at all to cover this family - direct proof that promotion was correctly scoped the first time. `Claim.careTeam[0].role.coding[0].code="primary"` is the same category of hardcoded/inferred field every earlier slice's own fixed-literal fields already established - recorded only when a rendering provider actually exists (confirmed via a dedicated test that the with-dependent fixture, which has no rendering provider, produces no `careTeam` fact at all). Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the fixture parametrization to both real 837P fixtures, plus one fixture each from the two still-uninstrumented siblings, 837I/837D) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`. **A real design problem this slice's own dispatch surfaced, not a mapper-layer one**: 837P/837I/837D all share the literal `ST01="837"` (see `app/edi/registry.py`'s own dispatch docstring), so a single `"837"` entry in `_INSTRUMENTED_TRANSACTION_SETS` would have incorrectly marked all three variants supported the moment any one of them shipped - `app/provenance/dispatch.py`'s own EDI branch now resolves the real variant via the shared `resolve_837_variant()` (the same resolver `registry.py`'s/`validation.py`'s own 837-family dispatch already uses) and reports `trigger_event="837P"`/`"837I"`/`"837D"` accordingly, tracked as three independent entries in the instrumented set - the identical finer-than-the-obvious-key granularity problem C-CDA's own per-section (not per-document-type) instrumentation already had to solve, recurring here one level differently.
  - **EDI 837I instrumentation (`app/edi/claim_837i.py`)** - the eleventh slice, and the second real proof (after 837I's own forward direction) that 837P's own 3-level HL chain genuinely, not coincidentally, generalizes to a sibling variant, for provenance too. Reuses the identical `build_organization_from_nm1`/`build_practitioner_from_nm1`/`build_patient_from_nm1_dmg`/`build_coverage` builders every earlier EDI family already instrumented. Instruments `_build_procedure_concept`/`_build_service_line_item`/`_build_discharge_status_supporting_info`/`Edi837iBuilder.build_bundle` field-for-field: `SV2-2.2` (procedure, when present) or a direct fact built from the real `SV2-1` revenue code as fallback text (confirmed via a dedicated test that this fallback is `derivation="direct"`, not `"inferred"`, since the text genuinely reflects a real source value, just reformatted - a third branch this family's own `productOrService` resolution needed that 837P's simpler two-branch version didn't), `SV2-1` (revenue code, its own `.revenue` field - a genuinely new field 837P's own `SV1`-derived items never populate), `CL1-3` (discharge status, via `Claim.supportingInfo`, alongside the same hardcoded/inferred `category="discharge"` literal every earlier slice's own fixed-vocabulary fields already established). **A real, previously-undetected location-string ambiguity this slice's own multi-`HI`-segment shape surfaced, fixed before it could ship silently wrong**: institutional claims split diagnoses across *several* physical `HI` segments (Principal in one, Other in another - confirmed directly against the real fixture), and `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` is called once per segment - recording each diagnosis's location as bare `"HI-{position}.2"` meant two diagnoses from *different* segments, both at intra-segment position 1, recorded the *identical*, misleadingly-ambiguous location string (`"HI-1.2"` for both), even though they came from physically different segments in the raw X12 text. Fixed by adding a `segment_repetition` parameter to both `edi_location()` (producing `"HI[1]-1.2"` when given, mirroring `hl7_location()`'s own `repetition` parameter but at the *segment* level rather than the field level, since X12 handles this kind of repetition via repeated segments, not a repeating field within one) and `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts()` itself (threaded through from `claim_837i.py`'s own `enumerate()` over `iter_diagnosis_hi_segments()`) - both parameters default to `None`/absent, so 278's and 837P's own single-`HI`-segment call sites (which never pass it) produce byte-identical location strings to before, confirmed directly by re-running their own fixtures after the fix. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the fixture parametrization to both real 837I fixtures, plus 837I's own fixture folded into 837P's zero-behavior sweep too) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`, including a dedicated regression proof that the basic fixture's own three diagnoses (one from the Principal `HI` segment, two from the Other `HI` segment) resolve to three genuinely distinct source locations, not two identical ones.
  - **EDI 837D instrumentation (`app/edi/claim_837d.py`)** - the twelfth slice, and the one that completes Data Specification coverage for the entire "big five" HIPAA EDI suite - every EDI family this app's forward direction supports is now instrumented, the identical milestone MDM's own slice already reached for HL7v2 breadth. Reuses the identical `build_organization_from_nm1`/`build_practitioner_from_nm1`/`build_patient_from_nm1_dmg`/`build_coverage` builders every earlier EDI family already instrumented, and reuses 837I's own `enumerate()`+`index_offset`+`segment_repetition` diagnosis-loop shape verbatim for structural consistency, even though neither real 837D fixture currently carries more than one `HI` segment to actually exercise the disambiguation (confirmed directly via fixture inspection before writing this bullet) - the same "apply the fix everywhere the identical hazard structurally exists, not just where a fixture happens to trip over it" discipline this app's own `NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM`/`sanitize_x12_text` fixes already established on the forward-mapping side. Instruments `_build_procedure_concept`/`_build_tooth_body_site`/`_build_tooth_sub_sites`/`_build_service_line_item`/`Edi837dBuilder.build_bundle` field-for-field: `SV3-1.2` (CDT procedure code), `SV3-2`/`SV3-6` (unit price/quantity, two fields neither 837P's `SV1` nor 837I's `SV2` instrumentation needed to record as their own independent facts), `TOO-2`/`TOO-3` (tooth number/surfaces - a segment with no 837P/837I equivalent at all, `subSite`'s own repeating shape recorded one fact per surface the same "array index, not one combined fact" discipline `diagnosis[]`/`item[]` themselves already use), and the claim-wide `CLM-5.1` place-of-service default recorded independently against every `item[i].locationCodeableConcept`, the identical "one claim-wide source, N per-item destinations" case 837P's own slice already established. **A genuinely new dual-source-resolution case, distinct from every earlier EDI slice's own**: `servicedDate` can come from either a per-line `DTP*472` or the one claim-level `DTP*472` default (see `resolve_line_dtp_raw_date`'s own module docstring for why dental claims commonly need the fallback) - `_build_service_line_item` re-derives, purely for provenance with no change to `resolve_line_dtp_raw_date`'s own real behavior, which candidate actually fired, recording the claim-level fallback's own source location with a `segment_repetition=0` marker (reusing `edi_location`'s existing parameter, not a new one) to distinguish it from a per-line `DTP` at the identical element position - the same "report which branch really fired" discipline `app/mappings/adt.py`'s own PV1-44-vs-EVN-2 resolution and 837I's own multi-`HI`-segment fix already established, now applied to disambiguate two different *segment instances* rather than two different *segments*. `productOrService` uses the same two-branch shape 837P's own slice established (direct from `SV3-1.2` via the now-instrumented `_build_procedure_concept`, or an inferred `"Unspecified procedure"` placeholder) rather than 837I's three-branch one, since 837D has no revenue-code-style fallback text to fall back to - confirmed directly from the forward mapper's own shape, not assumed by analogy. `Claim.careTeam[0].role.coding[0].code="primary"` is the same hardcoded/inferred field every earlier 837 slice's own rendering/attending-provider role already established, recorded only when a rendering provider actually exists. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier slice established (extending the fixture parametrization to both real 837D fixtures) plus content-correctness tests in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`, including a dedicated regression proof that the basic fixture's own two service lines (neither carrying its own `DTP`) both resolve `servicedDate` from the one claim-level default via the `segment_repetition=0` marker, while the no-dependent fixture's own single line (which does carry a per-line `DTP`) resolves without it.
  - **C-CDA narrative-section instrumentation (`app/cda/narrative_sections.py`)** - a follow-up slice once every EDI family reached full breadth, instrumenting all twelve templateIds this module registers (Discharge Summary's Hospital Course/Plan of Treatment, History and Physical's own nine) via the identical shared `build_narrative_document_reference`. Reads `.type.coding[0].code`/`.coding[0].display` from the section's own real `<code>` (`xpath_location("code", "@code")`/`xpath_location("code", "@displayName")`) and `.description` from `<title>` (`xpath_location("title")`) - both straightforward, single-location facts, the same shape every other section's own coded/title fields already record. **`Binary.data`'s own location needed the same "one real location when there genuinely is one, a disclosed marker otherwise" judgment call `app/mappings/mdm.py`'s own OBX-5 join and `app/mappings/siu.py`'s own NTE-3 join already established, confirmed rather than assumed to be necessary**: when `<text>` has no child elements at all (Hospital Course's own real shape - plain mixed text, no `<paragraph>` wrapper), the joined narrative *is* `<text>`'s own direct text content, so `xpath_location("text")` points at it exactly; every other real shape (one or more `<paragraph>`/`<list>`/`<table>` children - confirmed directly against the real `discharge_summary_basic.xml` fixture's own table-shaped Plan of Treatment section, which joins a header row plus two data rows into three lines) has no single element whose own text span covers the *joined* value `Binary.data` actually holds, so it gets the disclosed `f"text (×{count} blocks)"` marker instead - built directly rather than through `xpath_location()` itself, since that helper joins real path segments with `/` and a disclosed marker isn't one. `_extract_narrative_lines` was split out of `extract_narrative_text` (which is now a thin `"\n".join(...)` wrapper over it) specifically so the builder can get the block *count* needed for this decision without re-deriving it. Verified via the identical zero-behavior-change regression discipline every earlier C-CDA slice established (both real fixtures already in `test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`'s own parametrization, plus a 200-seed manual fuzz sweep of `generate_discharge_summary()`/`generate_history_and_physical()` output run with/without a recorder, confirming byte-identical Bundle output and zero orphaned facts) plus content-correctness tests proving the precise-location branch (Hospital Course), the disclosed-marker branch (Plan of Treatment's own table), and that all nine of the real History and Physical fixture's own narrative sections independently produce their own `type`/`description`/`data` facts with no cross-contamination between them. **`app/provenance/dispatch.py::_CDA_UNSUPPORTED_REASON` needed a real rewrite, not just a comment update, since it's the literal string returned to API consumers via `unsupported_reason`**: its own previous wording ("some document types... carry their own additional IG-required sections this app's conversion never maps at all") became factually wrong the moment `narrative_sections.py` shipped on the forward-conversion side - every section either document type's own IG requires now converts to *something*. The reason narrows to what's still genuinely true: Plan of Treatment/Social History/Family History can each carry real structured clinical data (a Plan of Care Activity Observation, Social History/Smoking Status Observations, a Family History Organizer) this app's conversion still only represents as narrative text, not structured entries - so `unsupported=True` stays correct, for a narrower and now-accurate reason (**superseded again** once the structured-entry follow-up slice below shipped - see the "Known, disclosed scope for this phase" bullet for the current, accurate reason).
  - **Known, disclosed scope for this phase**: all four HL7v2 message types this app converts (ADT - all 9 triggers, SIU - all 6 triggers, ORU - all 5 triggers, MDM - all 6 triggers) are fully instrumented. **Every general-purpose C-CDA section this app recognizes is now instrumented too** - the document header plus all seven sections (Problems, Medications, Allergies, Immunizations, Vital Signs, Results, Procedures), across all three document types (CCD, Discharge Summary, History and Physical), with Discharge Summary's own Hospital Discharge Diagnosis and Discharge Medications sections both covered "for free" via their reuse of Problems'/Medications' own entry-level builders, and all twelve narrative-section templateIds (`app/cda/narrative_sections.py`, see its own bullet above) now instrumented too - the identical breadth the bidirectional-transformation pillar's own reverse direction has *not yet* reached for the narrative sections specifically (see that pillar's own disclosed gap above). **Every EDI family this app's forward direction supports is fully instrumented** - 270 (Eligibility Inquiry), 271 (Eligibility Response), 276 (Claim Status Request), 277 (Claim Status Response), 278 (Prior Authorization), 835 (Remittance Advice), 837P (Professional Claim), 837I (Institutional Claim), and 837D (Dental Claim) - the complete "big five" HIPAA EDI suite, including all three 837 variants. **Every C-CDA document type now reports `unsupported=False` too**, alongside HL7v2 and EDI - the last three items holding it back each resolved rather than lingering: Plan of Treatment/Social History/Family History's own structured entries (closed by `app/cda/social_history.py`/`family_history.py`/`plan_of_treatment.py`), Procedures' own Indication/Comment Activity cross-references (closed, see that module's own bullet), and `originalText` resolution (closed, see `app/cda/common.py::resolve_narrative_references`). **`author` maps two ways, and only one of them was a scope decision.** The IG's tables list `author` twice wherever it appears - once to a `Provenance` resource and once to a plain resource attribute - so the *person* is a `Practitioner` referenced from the resource itself, which is a real mapping this app implements wherever the IG names a target: `Procedure.recorder`, `MedicationRequest.requester` (Medication Activity and INT-mood Immunization), `Observation.performer` (Vital Signs), and `AllergyIntolerance.recordedDate` for the time. **Sections whose table names Provenance alone - Problems, Allergies, Results - deliberately build no author Practitioner**: doing so would invent a target the IG declines to name, and Problems marks the non-Provenance rows "not supported by target" explicitly. `build_author_participant` is the shared entry point and its docstring lists which sections may call it. **The author is not always a person, and treating it as one was a real bug**: CDA models `assignedAuthor` as a choice - `assignedPerson` *or* `assignedAuthoringDevice`, with `<id>` required either way - so an EHR that generated the entry is an ordinary author. The first version built a nameless `Practitioner` for one, asserting a software system is a person and dropping its `manufacturerModelName`/`softwareName`, which `Practitioner` has nowhere to put. A device author now becomes a real `Device`, and the caller says whether its own target can reference one: `MedicationRequest.requester` can (confirmed via `enum_reference_types`), `Observation.performer` and `Procedure.recorder` cannot, so those pass `allow_device=False` and leave the field unset rather than mismapping. **Three follow-on defects surfaced with it, none of which the round-trip suite caught**, because the generator emitted no author outside Procedures and so had nothing to lose: the reverse direction regenerated no `<author>` at all for Medications/Discharge Medications/Vitals, silently dropping the Practitioner (`_reverse_author_element` now covers both shapes); a Pulse Oximetry Panel read no author, even though its primary O2-saturation reading *is* the panel and keeps every other field of its own (the read moved into `_apply_common_observation_fields`, which both the flat and panel builders share); and the generator's random vital-sign pool contained the BP and pulse-ox codes it *also* adds deliberately, putting two systolics in one organizer - panel membership is first-match-wins on the LOINC code, so which reading became the panel could shift across a round trip and take its author with it. A Blood Pressure Panel's own sides still carry no author, which is IG-faithful rather than a gap: the row maps a Vital Sign Observation's author to the Observation built from it, and a folded side produces none (the same reason its id, status and effectiveTime are not carried either). **The separate `Provenance` *resource* is the actual scope decision** - a deliberate, permanent scope decision, not deferred work: `Provenance` models "who created/revised/signed this record, and when," which presumes a system that *stores* records over time, and this app is a stateless converter with no record lifecycle for such a resource to describe (its required `recorded` timestamp has no honest value here - conversion time is clinically meaningless). Where the source `<author>` has a real home on the resource itself, the plain attribute already carries it (`Procedure.recorder`, `Annotation.authorReference`), reaching a downstream consumer without fabricating an audit record for an event that never happened; the C-CDA on FHIR IG itself explicitly declines to give guidance here ("does not provide definitive CDA <-> FHIR guidance on when resource attributes... vs. dedicated Provenance resources should be used"). **Reclassifying it is what made the graduation correct, and the prior framing was inconsistent**: a deliberate decision NOT to map something has never counted against provenance coverage in this app - `app/mappings/mdm.py` leaves TXA-13/TXA-17 unmapped for well-documented reasons and MDM has always reported `unsupported=False` - so treating an out-of-scope decision as a coverage gap for C-CDA alone was a real inconsistency, not a stricter standard. `_INSTRUMENTED_CDA_DOCUMENT_TYPES` now keys the flag per document type (the CDA mirror of `_INSTRUMENTED_MESSAGE_TYPES`/`_INSTRUMENTED_TRANSACTION_SETS`), and `_CDA_UNSUPPORTED_REASON` survives for the one case that genuinely remains: a document type this app doesn't recognize at all.

### C-CDA support (`app/cda/`)

C-CDA is a genuinely different input *format* from HL7v2 (XML, not pipe-delimited), so it's a sibling package to `app/hl7/`/`app/mappings/`, not folded into either — the field-access primitives are fundamentally different even though the FHIR-building spirit is the same. Built entirely on stdlib `xml.etree.ElementTree` (verified sufficient for this scope: template-id-driven section/entry lookup at fixed nesting depths, no need for XPath 1.0 axes or XSD validation) — no new dependency.

- **`app/cda/parser.py`** — the `app/hl7/parser.py` equivalent: `parse_document(raw_xml) -> Element` (raises `CdaParseError`), `has_template_id(element, root_oid) -> bool`, `find_child`/`find_all` (path navigation), `xsi_type`, `coded_value`, `ts_value`, `ivl_ts_bounds`. Two real `ElementTree` gotchas these primitives are built to make structurally impossible to hit by accident: (1) a multi-segment relative path missing the namespace prefix on **any** segment fails **silently** (`[]`, not an error) — `find_all` auto-prefixes every `/`-separated segment itself, so a caller can't under-prefix a path; (2) every C-CDA templateId is emitted **twice** per element (a bare `<templateId root="...">` and a versioned sibling with `root`+`extension`), as sibling elements in no guaranteed order — `has_template_id` checks all direct children for a matching `@root`, never assumes `find()`'s first hit is the bare one, and (deliberately) only checks **direct** children, not descendants, so checking a section for its own templateId can't false-match a nested entry's templateId several levels down.
- **`app/cda/common.py`** — the `app/mappings/common.py` equivalent, shared across C-CDA document types (not CCD-specific, since header structure and coded-value shape are standardized across the whole C-CDA family): `build_codeable_concept_from_cd(element) -> CodeableConcept | None` (CD-shaped `<code>`/`<value xsi:type="CD">`, via a disclosed OID→FHIR-system table - SNOMED CT, LOINC, RxNorm, ICD-10-CM, falling back to `urn:oid:<oid>`), `parse_partial_ts(raw) -> str | None` (tries a full HL7 TS timestamp, falls back to date-only — CDA's `@value` digit shape is identical to HL7v2's TS fields, so `app/fhir_models/builders.py::parse_hl7_date`/`parse_hl7_datetime` are reused directly, not reimplemented), `build_patient_from_header(document) -> Patient` (raises `MissingSegmentError` if `recordTarget/patientRole/patient` is absent), `build_encounter_from_header(document, patient_id) -> Encounter | None` (from `componentOf/encompassingEncounter`, `None` when absent since a CCD is a summary document not necessarily bound to one encounter; status always `"unknown"` — same rationale as ORU/MDM's minimal PV1-derived Encounters, no reliable lifecycle signal to infer from), `assemble_bundle(document, patient, *resources) -> Bundle` (`Bundle.identifier`/`.timestamp` from `ClinicalDocument/id`/`effectiveTime`, `Bundle.type = "collection"` matching every existing HL7v2 pipeline's output shape — a proper FHIR-Document-shaped `Bundle(type="document")` + `Composition` is out of scope, since `fhir.resources.R4B.Composition` requires `author` (1..*), which this slice never materializes), and `build_sectioned_bundle(document) -> Bundle` (header + walk every `structuredBody` section through `SECTION_BUILDERS` - the full `build_bundle()` implementation for any document type whose conversion is exactly that shape; extracted here once `app/cda/discharge_summary.py` became a second real consumer of logic `ccd.py` had originally implemented inline, the same "extract once a second real consumer exists" pattern already used for `build_minimal_pv1_fields`/`build_minimal_encounter`, `is_before`, etc. — see that function's own docstring for why a document type with genuinely different structure should implement `build_bundle()` directly instead of being forced through it). CDA's `AdministrativeGender` codeSystem uses `F`/`M`/`UN`, a genuinely different vocabulary from HL7v2 PID-8's `M`/`F`/`O` — its own `_GENDER_MAP`, not a reuse of `app/fhir_models/builders.py`'s HL7v2 gender map. Three bugs caught by a follow-up multi-agent code review (adversarially verified by actually reproducing each with real code, not just argued for), all fixed before any fixture exercised them:
  - **`Bundle.timestamp` is FHIR `instant`, which has no date-only form** (unlike `Condition.onset(Date)Time`, a plain `dateTime` that does) - `assemble_bundle` originally fed it through `parse_partial_ts`, whose whole purpose is a date-only fallback. A `ClinicalDocument/effectiveTime` with no time component (a common, legitimate real-world shape) produced a value the `instant` regex rejects, crashing resource construction with `pydantic.ValidationError` on an otherwise perfectly convertible document - reproduced directly via `Bundle(timestamp="2026-08-12")`. Fixed by calling `parse_hl7_datetime` directly (returns `None` rather than a date-only string) instead of `parse_partial_ts`, matching `app/mappings/common.py::assemble_bundle`'s own deliberate no-date-only-fallback choice for this exact field.
  - **A root-only `<id>` (no `@extension`) was silently treated as "no identifier"** - `_build_identifier` returned `None` whenever `@extension` was absent, even though an II with only a `root` is still a complete identifier per the HL7 datatype spec (the root itself IS the identifier value). Reproduced: a root-only `patientRole/id` produced `Patient.identifier = None` instead of an identifier. Fixed by falling back to `Identifier(system="urn:ietf:rfc:3986", value=f"urn:oid:{root}")` when `@extension` is absent but `@root` is present, matching FHIR's own CDA-on-FHIR convention for representing a bare root.
  - **`build_encounter_from_header` only ever captured the first `<id>`** (`find_child`, not `find_all`), unlike `_build_patient_identifiers` which correctly collects every one - an `encompassingEncounter` with two ids (e.g. local + national encounter number) silently lost the second. Fixed by extracting a shared `_build_identifiers(id_elements, fallback_system) -> list[Identifier]` helper, used by both the patient and encounter paths now.
  - **`build_identifier`/`build_identifiers`** (promoted from `_build_identifier`/`_build_identifiers` - dropped the leading underscore - once `app/cda/procedures.py` became a second real consumer of the identical root/extension `<id>` resolution already used for patient/encounter/document ids) and **`build_quantity_from_pq(element) -> Quantity | None`** (a PQ-shaped element's `@value`/`@unit` → `Quantity`, value+unit only - no system/code, matching the IG's own worked examples for both Medications' `doseQuantity` and Vital Signs'/Results' own `value` - promoted here once `app/cda/medications.py`'s `_resolve_pq` and `app/cda/immunizations.py`'s `_resolve_dose_quantity` were found to be byte-for-byte identical, with `vitals.py`/`results.py` becoming a third and fourth real consumer of the same shape) are both now public, reused across four-plus section modules apiece.
- **The patient header covers the IG's whole Patient table, not just the obvious half.** `build_patient_from_header` also maps `maritalStatusCode` -> `.maritalStatus` (a system rewrite, not a code map - C-CDA's own codes are the ones FHIR's marital-status value set draws on), `languageCommunication` -> `.communication` (language coded against BCP-47, since `languageCode` carries a bare language tag with no codeSystem of its own, plus `preferenceInd` -> `.preferred`), `raceCode`/`ethnicGroupCode` -> the `us-core-race`/`us-core-ethnicity` extensions, `guardian` -> `.contact`, and `providerOrganization` -> `.managingOrganization`.
  - **Race and ethnicity are complex extensions with a slice decision to make.** Each coding goes under `ombCategory` or `detailed` depending on whether its code is one of the OMB categories that slice is bound to; `text` is 1..1, so it is built from the codings' own displays and recorded as inferred - there is no source element for it. The **second** race only reaches a document through the sdtc-namespaced repeat (`sdtc:raceCode`), which `find_all` cannot see - it resolves the CDA namespace only - so those are read via Clark notation. Both the reverse builder and the generator declare `xmlns:sdtc` unconditionally: an unbound prefix is a parse error, not a missing value.
  - **A guardian can be a person, an organization, or both.** An organization-only guardian still becomes a contact rather than being dropped for having no name. `guardianOrganization` and `providerOrganization` become real `Organization` resources, so they are only built when the caller passes `extra_resources` - a Reference to a resource absent from the Bundle would dangle.
  - `Patient.contact.name`/`.address` are **singular**, unlike the repeating patient-level fields the same builders produce, so their provenance is recorded by hand rather than by those builders - an indexed path (`contact[0].name[0].family`) points at a field the Bundle does not have, which is the class of bug that makes a fact resolve nowhere.
- **`app/cda/base.py`** / **`app/cda/registry.py`** — `CdaDocumentBuilder(ABC)` (`build_bundle(document) -> Bundle`) is the `MessageMapper` equivalent: document types are a "different shape dispatched via registry" situation, same as HL7v2 message types. `registry.py` holds two tables: `_DOCUMENT_BUILDERS` (`{CCD_TEMPLATE_ID: CcdBuilder(), DISCHARGE_SUMMARY_TEMPLATE_ID: DischargeSummaryBuilder(), HISTORY_AND_PHYSICAL_TEMPLATE_ID: HistoryAndPhysicalBuilder()}`) and `SECTION_BUILDERS` (`{PROBLEMS_SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_conditions, MEDICATIONS_SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_medication_requests, allergies.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_allergy_intolerances, allergies.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID_ENTRIES_OPTIONAL: build_allergy_intolerances, IMMUNIZATIONS_SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_immunizations, vitals.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_vital_signs, vitals.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID_ENTRIES_OPTIONAL: build_vital_signs, results.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_diagnostic_reports, results.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID_ENTRIES_OPTIONAL: build_diagnostic_reports, procedures.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_procedures, procedures.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID_ENTRIES_OPTIONAL: build_procedures, hospital_discharge_diagnosis.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_hospital_discharge_diagnoses, discharge_medications.SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID: build_discharge_medication_requests}`, plain functions since every section builder shares one signature — the generator-registry precedent, not the mapper one; the Allergies section is registered under **two** templateIds - the "entries required" one this app originally targeted and the "entries optional" sibling variant, both wrapping the identical entry shape, just a different section-level cardinality constraint - a real gap found by fetching a real Discharge Summary example while researching that document type (see the `discharge_summary.py` bullet below), not something CCD's own fixtures had exercised. **Registering the conversion-side dict entry alone was itself an incomplete fix, caught by a follow-up code review**: `app/cda/validation.py` has its own independent `_find_allergies_section(document)` helper (used to decide which section to run `_rule_allergies` against), and it originally checked only the "entries required" templateId - so a document using the "entries optional" variant converted correctly (via `SECTION_BUILDERS`) but silently ran *zero* allergy validation findings against it, reproduced directly (an entries-optional section with an unresolvable allergen produced no `cda.allergy-missing-allergen` finding, while the identical content under the "entries required" templateId correctly did). Fixed by extracting a shared `_find_section(document, *template_ids)` helper in `validation.py` that every `_find_*_section` function now calls, so a section type registered under more than one templateId can't have this drift between its conversion-side and validation-side dispatch again; `app/cda/generator.py`'s `_random_allergies_section` was also updated to emit the "entries optional" templateId ~25% of the time (it previously always emitted "entries required"), since that gap was exactly why the 1000+-seed fuzz suite never caught it in the first place). `SECTION_BUILDERS` lives in `registry.py` rather than inline in `ccd.py` since section templateIds are standardized across C-CDA document types generally - both `CcdBuilder` and `DischargeSummaryBuilder` reuse this exact table via `app/cda/common.py::build_sectioned_bundle`. **Import-direction note:** `registry.py` imports each concrete document builder class at module load time (same as `app/mappings/registry.py` imports each HL7v2 mapper class), but `build_sectioned_bundle()` also needs `SECTION_BUILDERS` to dispatch each section it walks — a top-level circular import if `common.py` imported `registry.py` back at its own module level. Solved with a function-local (not module-level) import inside `build_sectioned_bundle()`: `from app.cda.registry import SECTION_BUILDERS` runs only when the function executes, by which time `registry.py` has already finished loading.
- **`app/cda/composition.py`** - the document header as a `Composition`, which makes a C-CDA conversion a real **FHIR Document** (`Bundle(type="document")`, Composition as `entry[0]`) rather than a bag of resources. **The field mapping is the FHIR spec's own, not a local invention**: C-CDA on FHIR publishes no header table (confirmed by listing `mappings/CF/` and `input/pagecontent/` - neither has one), but the base R4 `Composition` StructureDefinition carries a per-element `cda` mapping ("CDA (R2)", `http://hl7.org/v3/cda`) stating the correspondence directly, and `hl7.fhir.us.ccda` 2.0.0-ballot publishes Composition profiles (`US-Realm-Header` plus one per document type) for exactly the three types this app converts. Every field is that mapping verbatim: `.identifier`<-`setId`, `.type`<-`code`, `.subject`<-`recordTarget`, `.encounter`<-`componentOf/encompassingEncounter`, `.date`<-`effectiveTime`, `.author`<-`author/assignedAuthor`, `.title`<-`title`, `.confidentiality`<-`confidentialityCode`, `.attester`<-`authenticator`/`legalAuthenticator`, `.custodian`<-`custodian/assignedCustodian`, `.event`<-`documentationOf/serviceEvent`, `.section`<-each `structuredBody` section (title, code, narrative, and references to exactly the resources that section produced). **`.identifier` is `setId`, not `id`** - a distinction worth keeping: `id` identifies this instance and is already `Bundle.identifier`, `setId` identifies the series. **Only a source that IS a document gets one.** The v2-to-FHIR IG settles HL7v2: its MSH map assigns `Bundle.type` `"message"` ("no expectation that this is the actual Bundle.type"), and MDM^T02 - a document *notification* - maps its TXA and its content-bearing OBX to `DocumentReference`, never `Composition`; the content is an opaque blob, so Composition sections could only be fabricated. X12 carries no clinical document at all. **Three R4 invariants bind at `type="document"` and are checked rather than assumed**: bdl-9 (identifier with system *and* value), bdl-10 (timestamp), bdl-11 (Composition first). `Bundle.timestamp` is `instant`, so a date-only `effectiveTime` cannot supply one - such a document stays `collection` rather than being given a fabricated time (~15% of generated documents, by design). `Composition.date` is `dateTime` and *does* keep date-only precision, which is why the reverse builder reads `<effectiveTime>` back from it rather than from `Bundle.timestamp` - reading the latter dropped the element entirely and cost the next forward pass its whole Composition. **`Composition` eagerly requires `author`, `type`, `date`, `status` and `title` at construction** - `model_fields` reports only `author`/`type`, the same "don't trust the declared cardinality" trap this app has hit repeatedly - and author/type/date have no honest default, so a document missing any of them stays a collection rather than getting invented content. `.status` is fixed `"final"` (C-CDA has no document-level statusCode, and of the four values only that one describes a rendered, exchanged document); a missing `<title>` falls back to the document code's own display, recorded inferred. **`.confidentiality` is mapped even though US Realm Header prohibits it (0..0)** - the base mapping is the spec's own statement of the correspondence and the value is real data that would otherwise be dropped, so it is carried *and* the caveat is stated twice: as a `cda.composition-confidentiality-not-us-realm-conformant` validation finding, and as a citation on its own crosswalk row. That second one needed a small widening: `transform_citations.py` gained `MAPPING_CAVEATS`, and a citation now shows for any row that has one rather than only for a *transformed* value - a value copied unchanged can still carry a caveat worth reading. **Showing more citations then needed the note to stop rendering inline**: a citation's `note` is a full sentence (the longest past 300 characters), and inlining it made 16 of 58 crosswalk rows 3-6x taller than the rest, pushing the table past 2300px inside an 870px pane. The title renders; the note is a tooltip, marked with a dotted underline so it reads as hoverable. Caught by driving the page, not by review - the same way the `button[hidden]` and stale-asset bugs were. **The page itself is wider for the same reason**: `--page-width` (1440px) replaced the 960px body, which had the correlated panes at ~430px each and the crosswalk table needing 2.7 screens of horizontal scrolling inside an 870px wrapper. Panes are now ~670px and the table 1.8 screens. Prose does not widen with it - `--prose-width` (68ch) caps the intro and panel text, since a 1400px line of body text is unreadable; the subtitle is exempt, being a one-line label rather than running text. `white-space: nowrap` on the table cells is left alone deliberately: an unbroken path is what makes the table scannable, and reflowing them would trade one problem for another. **`ClinicalDocument/languageCode` -> `Composition.language`**, and only there. `Resource.language` is "the base language in which the resource is written", and the Composition *is* the document; every other resource in the Bundle is a clinical fact the document narrates, so stamping each one would assert a language the source stated once, about the whole document. Carried verbatim - C-CDA's `languageCode` holds a bare BCP-47 tag with no codeSystem of its own, the same direct carry `Patient.communication` already uses. A document that builds no Composition has nowhere to put it and reports it as a drop, citing that reason. **The generator now varies the language** (`_DOCUMENT_LANGUAGES`), because with `en-US` everywhere the reverse builder hardcoded the value and round-tripped perfectly while carrying nothing - which is exactly what it did.

**Two verdicts had gone stale, and the second one caught the first's replacement being wrong too.** `ClinicalDocument/author`/`custodian` cited "this app builds no Composition - it emits a collection Bundle, not a FHIR Document" - true when written, false the day `app/cda/composition.py` shipped, and nothing re-checked it. **The same trap that once froze an implementation choice into the Discharge Medications disclosure as though it were a standards constraint**: a verdict asserting what this app *does* is a claim with an expiry date, and nothing expires it. The replacement blamed a date-only `effectiveTime` blocking `type="document"` - also wrong, confirmed against a real Bundle: those documents keep their Composition and the Bundle merely stays a `collection`. The condition that actually fires is `build_composition` returning `None`, which needs a document code, a parseable `effectiveTime` and an author. Only the confirm-against-the-Bundle rule separated the second wrong answer from the first.

**Why no Composition is built without an author, confirmed against the specs rather than asserted.** `Composition.author` is `1..*` in R4, and R4 §2.5.0.2.2 closes the obvious escape hatch twice over: `data-absent-reason` applies to *primitives*, not complex elements or References, and "it is not valid to create a fictional piece of data... and then add an extension indicating that the data has been constructed to meet the data rules". So a placeholder author would be non-conformant, not a pragmatic workaround. The gate is forced, not chosen. `ClinicalDocument/author` is `1..*` in base CDA too (with `custodian`, `code`, `effectiveTime`, `id`, `confidentialityCode` all 1..1 and `recordTarget` `1..*`), so it can only ever fire on a source document that is already invalid.

**What was actually wrong was that it was silent.** A document missing one required element lost its entire Composition - type, date, title, confidentiality, custodian, attesters, `documentationOf` and every section's metadata - and the validator, whose whole job is to say "this is missing something required", had no rule for any of them. `cda.header-missing-required-element` (`error`) now covers all seven base-CDA requirements plus the two US Realm Header tightens to 1..1 (`title`, `languageCode`), the latter checked only when the document declares that templateId - they are genuinely 0..1 otherwise. The only rules in this file that check a *cardinality* rather than a value.

**Seventeen C-CDA fixtures were non-conformant at the header and are now fixed.** Almost all lacked `author` and `custodian`; several lacked `effectiveTime`. They were testing the converter against input no real system produces, and it is why the whole Composition path rested on `ccd_basic.xml` alone. `ccd_minimal.xml` is deliberately left as-is: it is the fixture that proves the no-author gate, and it is now also the only place `ClinicalDocument/code` still reports as a drop. Making them conformant shifted every `Bundle.entry[N]` index (a Composition leads each document now) and added a Practitioner and Organization per fixture - the affected tests resolve their index from the resource type instead, so the next header addition does not break them again.

**Disclosed scope limit**: `.relatesTo` (`relatedDocument`) is named by the mapping but not built - it points at *another* document, which a single-document conversion has nothing to resolve against.
- **`app/cda/ccd.py`** — `CcdBuilder` (`CCD_TEMPLATE_ID = "2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.1.2"`): `build_bundle()` is now just `return build_sectioned_bundle(document)` (see the `common.py` bullet above) - CCD is the "header + generic sections" shape that helper was extracted from. **An unrecognized section is silently skipped** — deliberate and disclosed, not a bug: this slice's section coverage is intentionally partial (Problems, Medications, Allergies, Immunizations, Vital Signs, Results, and Procedures; other sections - e.g. Social History, Family History, Plan of Treatment - remain deferred to a later slice, following this project's own established "one thing per slice" precedent — verified via git history that ADT^A01 shipped alone before A02/A03/A04/A08 arrived together in the next commit).
- **`app/cda/problems.py`** — `build_conditions(section, patient_id) -> list[Condition]`, per the official "C-CDA on FHIR" IG (`build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/ccda-on-fhir/`, current v2.0.0 continuous build). Entry shape: `entry/act[templateId=...4.3]/entryRelationship[typeCode=SUBJ]/observation[templateId=...4.4]/value[xsi:type=CD]`. Both the Concern Act and Problem Observation templateIds are checked via `has_template_id` before use, not assumed from position alone — a real gap caught by re-checking a draft against the approved plan before any test ran against it. `negationInd="true"` on the Problem Observation ("no known problem" pattern) makes the entry skipped entirely, not mapped as if the coded value were asserted — a disclosed limitation, not modeled as its own resource this slice. **`clinicalStatus` is resolved from two separate vocabularies that must not be merged into one lookup table**: a nested Problem Status Observation (LOINC `33999-4`)'s SNOMED CT value, checked first and taking priority when present, falls back to the Concern Act's own act-lifecycle `statusCode` (`active`/`suspended`/`aborted`/`completed`) only when no status observation resolves - see `_resolve_clinical_status`. `effectiveTime` low/high → `onsetDateTime`/`abatementDateTime`, resolved via `ivl_ts_bounds` which itself handles all three legal `IVL_TS` shapes (bare `@value` point-in-time, `<low>`/`<high>` children, or `nullFlavor` for fully unknown). **`entryRelationship/@typeCode` was originally never checked**, only the nested element's templateId - despite CLAUDE.md's own entry-shape description above always having specified `typeCode=SUBJ`. A `<entryRelationship typeCode="REFR">` (or any non-`SUBJ` code) whose nested `<observation>` happened to carry the Problem Observation templateId was incorrectly mapped as an asserted problem; the same gap existed for the Status Observation lookup (should require `typeCode="REFR"` per the IG pattern, not just match on the nested code alone). Caught by a follow-up multi-agent code review and reproduced directly (a `REFR`-wrapped Problem Observation produced a `Condition` it shouldn't have) - fixed by checking `relationship.get("typeCode")` against the expected code (`"SUBJ"` for the act-level loop, `"REFR"` for the status-observation loop) before descending into the nested element in both `build_conditions` and `_resolve_clinical_status`. **`build_condition(act, problem_observation, patient_id)`** (the per-entry builder `build_conditions` calls in its own loop) is public - promoted once `app/cda/hospital_discharge_diagnosis.py` became a second real consumer of the identical Problem-Observation-to-`Condition` logic, wrapped in a different outer Act template.
- **`app/cda/medications.py`** — `build_medication_requests(section, patient_id) -> list[MedicationRequest]`, per the official "C-CDA on FHIR" IG's `CF-medications.html` guidance and its underlying `CCDA-FHIR MedicationRequest.csv` mapping table (`github.com/HL7/ccda-on-fhir/blob/master/mappings/CF/`), verified by fetching both directly rather than assumed from field-name similarity to Problems. Entry shape: `entry/substanceAdministration[templateId=...4.16]` (a Medication Activity is a `substanceAdministration` directly - no wrapping Act, unlike Problems' Concern Act). Two published ConceptMaps, kept as separate dicts since they're genuinely different vocabularies: `STATUS_MAP` (`statusCode`, HL7 `ActStatus` → `MedicationRequest.status` - `active`→`active`, `suspended`→`on-hold`, `aborted`→`stopped`, `completed`→`completed`, `nullified`→`entered-in-error`; unrecognized/absent falls back to `"unknown"`) and `_MOOD_TO_INTENT` (`moodCode` → `MedicationRequest.intent` - `INT`→`"order"`, `EVN`→`"plan"`; this reads backwards at first glance but is the IG's own mapping, both rows marked "maps loosely to" since CDA mood codes are broader than FHIR intent codes). **`intent` turned out to be FHIR-required despite not showing up via `model_fields` introspection** - confirmed by constructing `MedicationRequest` directly (`status` too; only `subject` appears as `is_required()` in the model's own field metadata, the same "don't trust the library's declared cardinality" lesson `Appointment.status` already taught, but this time in the *stricter*-than-declared direction rather than the looser one) - an unrecognized/absent `moodCode` falls back to `"order"` rather than leaving a required field unset. Structured dosing (`routeCode`→`Dosage.route`, `doseQuantity`/`rateQuantity`→`Dosage.doseAndRate`, `effectiveTime` low/high→`Dosage.timing.repeat.boundsPeriod`) and free-text SIG (a nested `entryRelationship[typeCode=COMP]/substanceAdministration[templateId=...4.147]` "Medication Free Text Sig" whose `<text>` → `Dosage.patientInstruction`) are alternatives in real C-CDA, not both always present - `_build_dosage` returns `None` (omitting `dosageInstruction` entirely) only when neither is present, matching `Dosage` having no required fields itself. `doseQuantity`/`rateQuantity` are read as simple PQ (`@value`/`@unit` directly on the element) only - an `IVL_PQ` range shape (`<low>`/`<high>` children instead) is left unmapped rather than guessing which bound to report as a fixed dose, since the IG's own CSV doesn't disambiguate which shape governs this target field (noted `"Is SXPR used?"` in its own comments column). `negationInd="true"` (this specific administration/order did NOT happen) skips the entry entirely, mirroring Problems' negation philosophy. `consumable/manufacturedProduct/manufacturedMaterial/code` with no resolvable coded value also skips the entry -
