`medicationCodeableConcept` (or `medicationReference`, not produced by this converter) is required by `fhir.resources.R4B.MedicationRequest`, confirmed by construction the same way `intent`/`status` were. **Deliberately not mapped, per the CSV's own disclosed gaps** (each row explicitly marked "not supported" or "best pattern unclear" in the source table, not silently dropped): `entryRelationship[indication]` (target field genuinely undecided in the IG itself), performer/requester Practitioner references (would need a CDA-side XCN-equivalent practitioner-builder this slice doesn't add), Supply Order / Medication Dispense / Reaction Observation / Drug Monitoring entry relationships. **`build_medication_request(substance_administration, patient_id)`** (the per-entry builder `build_medication_requests` calls in its own loop) is public - promoted once `app/cda/discharge_medications.py` became a second real consumer of the identical Medication-Activity-to-`MedicationRequest` logic, wrapped in a different outer Act template.
- **`app/cda/allergies.py`** — `build_allergy_intolerances(section, patient_id) -> list[AllergyIntolerance]`, per the IG's `CF-allergies.html` guidance and `CCDA-FHIR Allergy.csv`. Entry shape mirrors Problems' Concern-Act wrapper (`entry/act[templateId=...4.30]/entryRelationship[SUBJ]/observation[templateId=...4.7]`), but the observation itself nests notably more sub-structure, each gated by its own `typeCode` per the same "check the relationship type, not just the nested templateId" discipline `app.cda.common.find_nested_observation` centralizes (promoted there from this module once `app/cda/problems.py`'s own `_resolve_clinical_status` became a second real consumer of the identical entryRelationship-walk shape - Problems matches its nested Status Observation by LOINC code rather than templateId, so the shared primitive is actually `iter_nested_observations(parent, type_code)`, with `find_nested_observation` as the templateId-matching convenience wrapper Allergies itself uses): a Status Observation (`entryRelationship[REFR]`, templateId `...4.28`, LOINC `33999-4`) for `clinicalStatus`, a Criticality Observation (`entryRelationship[SUBJ, inversionInd=true]`, templateId `...4.145`, LOINC `82606-5`) for `criticality`, and any number of Reaction Observations (`entryRelationship[MFST, inversionInd=true]`, templateId `...4.9`) each optionally carrying its own nested Severity Observation (`entryRelationship[SUBJ, inversionInd=true]`, templateId `...4.8`) for `reaction.severity` - the allergen itself lives at `participant[@typeCode=CSM]/participantRole/playingEntity/code`, a shape neither Problems nor Medications has (verified against a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples allergy entry, not assumed from the CSV alone). Four small ConceptMaps, each its own dict since they're independently-keyed vocabularies: `_TYPE_MAP`/`_CATEGORY_MAP` (both keyed off the *same* Allergy-Intolerance Observation `<value>` SNOMED code, but targeting `type`/`category` respectively - a code can be type-mapped without being category-mapped, e.g. "Allergy to substance" `419199007`, an intentional fuzz case), `CLINICAL_STATUS_MAP` (public, reused by the generator; the *values* are identical to `app.cda.problems.STATUS_OBSERVATION_VALUE_TO_CLINICAL_STATUS` since both sections share the one generic Status Observation template, but kept a separate dict/CodeableConcept construction since `AllergyIntolerance.clinicalStatus` binds to a different terminology system, `allergyintolerance-clinical`, than `Condition.clinicalStatus`'s `condition-clinical`), `CRITICALITY_MAP` (local `HL7ObservationValue` codes `CRITH`/`CRITL`/`CRITU`, not SNOMED), `SEVERITY_MAP` (SNOMED). **A real, disclosed divergence from Problems' negation handling**: Problems skips a `negationInd="true"` entry entirely, but the IG's own `CCDA-FHIR Allergy.csv` gives negated Allergy Observations a real target - a "no known allergy" `AllergyIntolerance` - so this module builds one rather than skipping, via `_resolve_allergen_code`'s two negation-only text fallbacks (a still-resolvable allergen → `code.text = "No known allergy to <display>"`; unresolvable/absent → the IG's exact `"No known allergies"` wording). The IG's *fully coded* negation crosswalk (`CF_AllergyIntoleranceAbsentCode`, mapping specific negated allergens to their own "no known allergy to X" SNOMED codes) was not found published in a verified, fetchable form - disclosed and left unimplemented rather than guessed at, same "don't guess at an unverified crosswalk" philosophy as MDM's TXA-17. `AllergyIntolerance.patient` (not `.subject`, unlike every other resource this app builds) and `.recordedDate` (not `.recorded`) were both confirmed by inspecting `model_fields` directly rather than assumed from naming-convention pattern-matching to `Condition`/`MedicationRequest`. `type`/`category`/`criticality` are bare `code` strings (not `CodeableConcept`, unlike `clinicalStatus`/`code`) - also confirmed by inspecting `model_fields` annotations before writing the constructor calls, per this project's established "always inspect, never guess" `fhir.resources` policy.
- **`app/cda/immunizations.py`** — `build_immunizations(section, patient_id) -> list[Immunization]`, per the IG's `CF-immunizations.html` guidance and `CCDA-FHIR Immunization.csv`. **The IG's own CSV splits Immunization Activity into two target resources by `moodCode`, and both now convert**: `EVN` (already administered/refused) → `Immunization`, `INT` (ordered/planned) → `MedicationRequest`. The field sets are largely disjoint, so the two builders share only the consumable-code lookup. On the INT side `negationInd` maps to `doNotPerform` (true and false alike) rather than overriding status, `intent` is fixed to `"order"` by the moodCode, and routeCode/doseQuantity/repeatNumber land under `dosageInstruction[0]`. **Two INT rows cannot be used as the CSV writes them, and the substitutions are disclosed rather than silent**: `.statusCode` names a transform (`CF_ImmunizationReqStatus`) that is not published anywhere in the IG's maps directory, so the MedicationRequest table's own `CF_MedStatus` - same source, same target field, same resource, already implemented in `app/cda/medications.py` - is reused; and `.repeatNumber` names `protocolApplied.seriesDosesPositiveInt`, which does not exist on `MedicationRequest` at all (it is an `Immunization` field, reading like a copy-paste from the EVN row above it), so the MedicationRequest table's `dosageInstruction.timing.repeat.count` is used instead. `app/cda/validation.py` walks both moods, with INT getting its own `cda.planned-immunization-missing-medication-code`/`-status-unrecognized` rules against that path's own resolution - conversion and validation must never see a different entry set. `performer`/`author` stay unmapped on both sides: this module has no CDA-side `assignedEntity` → Practitioner builder. Entry shape has no wrapping Act (`entry/substanceAdministration[templateId=...4.52]` directly, like Medications) - `consumable/manufacturedProduct/manufacturedMaterial/code` → `vaccineCode` (CVX-coded in practice) and `.../lotNumberText` → `lotNumber`, `routeCode`/`doseQuantity` map the same way Medications' structured dosing does. `STATUS_MAP` (public, reused by the generator) is the `CF_ImmunizationStatus` ConceptMap - but `negationInd="true"` overrides it unconditionally to `"not-done"` regardless of `statusCode` (per the ConceptMap's own comment that `completed`→`not-done` applies "only... when CDA negation is present and true" - every other source status already maps to `not-done` on its own, so checking negation first is equivalent and simpler than consulting the table for every code). `Immunization.status` has no `"unknown"` value (unlike most other status maps in this app - only `completed`/`entered-in-error`/`not-done` exist), so an unrecognized/absent non-negated `statusCode` defaults to `"completed"` rather than an artificial unknown, matching the same "default to the most common real value" judgment already made for Medications' `moodCode` fallback. **Two more required-but-not-`is_required()` surprises, both confirmed by constructing `Immunization` directly rather than trusting `model_fields`** (same lesson `MedicationRequest.intent` already taught): `status` is eagerly required despite not appearing in `model_fields`'s own required list, and `occurrence[x]` (`occurrenceDateTime`/`occurrenceString`) is a required one-of choice enforced *at construction*, not settable via attribute assignment afterward (like `Encounter.class_fhir`) - when `effectiveTime` doesn't resolve, this falls back to `occurrenceString = "Unknown"`, FHIR's own conventional representation for "administered, exact date unrecorded" (e.g. US Core's Immunization guidance), not a local guess. `primarySource` (the CSV's `_primarySource → Fixed value: "unknown"` row) is left unmapped entirely - `primarySource` is a plain FHIR `boolean`, and `"unknown"` isn't a valid boolean value, so passing the IG's own stated fixed value through would be invalid; disclosed and skipped rather than guessing at what was actually intended. Performer materialization (the CSV's `performer.actor → Reference`) is also disclosed-and-skipped, matching Medications' equivalent omission - this app has no CDA-side `assignedEntity → Practitioner` builder yet (the C-CDA equivalent of `app/mappings/common.py::build_practitioner_from_xcn` for HL7v2's XCN fields).
- **`app/cda/vitals.py`** — `build_vital_signs(section, patient_id) -> list[Observation]`, per the IG's `CF-vitals.html` guidance (`github.com/HL7/ccda-on-fhir/blob/master/input/pagecontent/CF-vitals.md`) - **no CSV mapping table is published for Vital Signs** the way Problems/Medications/Allergies/Immunizations/Procedures each have one, so this module's field mapping is drawn directly from that markdown page's own tables and worked examples, fetched and read directly rather than paraphrased from a secondary source. Section/Organizer/Observation templateIds were confirmed against a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples CCD document, not assumed from the IG page's own abbreviated XPath (LOINC code only, no templateId OID). C-CDA groups vitals into a Vital Signs Organizer (one per reading session) wrapping one or more individual Vital Sign Observations; the IG maps this to a FHIR `Observation` **panel** (`code` fixed to `85353-1` "Vital Signs Panel" - the organizer's own narrative-only `/code` is not carried into any FHIR field) whose `.hasMember` references one `Observation` per individual vital sign, mirrored here with every resource returned as its own separate, top-level Bundle entry (this app's established convention, never FHIR `.contained` - the same shape `app/mappings/oru.py` already uses for `DiagnosticReport`+`Observation` on the HL7v2 side). Both the Organizer's and each Observation's `statusCode` are fixed to `"completed"`→`"final"` per the C-CDA spec itself (not just this app's own disclosed default) - the IG's own tables state this plainly, so there's no ConceptMap to build here, unlike Results'/Procedures' own genuinely-variable `statusCode`. **The IG's own Blood Pressure and Pulse Oximetry Panel special cases now ship too**, as a follow-up slice - see this file's own dedicated bullet below (`_build_blood_pressure_panel`/`_build_pulse_oximetry_panel`) for the full design: systolic (`8480-6`)+diastolic (`8462-4`) grouped as `.component` under one fixed `85354-9` Observation (no top-level `.valueQuantity`, both components required - an incomplete pair falls back to an ordinary flat Vital Sign Observation instead), and the O2 saturation reading (`59408-5`/`2708-6`, both IG-documented synonymous codings always emitted regardless of which one the source used) becoming the Pulse Oximetry Panel itself (with its own top-level value, unlike the BP Panel) plus optional inhaled-oxygen-concentration (`3150-0`)/flow-rate (`3151-8`) `.component` entries when their own sibling codes are present. Every other LOINC-coded vital still maps 1:1, the same "map the general case now, disclose only the genuinely remaining special case" precedent Medications' own IVL_PQ dosing ranges and Immunizations' INT-mood entries already established. An organizer whose every child observation lacks a resolvable code produces no panel either (nothing to group), matching the "no resolvable code -> skip" convention at the organizer level too, not just the leaf level. **Registered under both its "entries required" (`...2.4.1`) and "entries optional" (`...2.4`, no trailing `.1`) templateIds** in `app/cda/registry.py`/`app/cda/validation.py` - the same two-templateId pattern Allergies already established (see that module's own bullet), found this time while researching the History and Physical document type: a real official HL7 example declared both together on its own Vital Signs section (legal - a section conforming to both variants at once), and its sibling Procedures section was found using the "entries optional" one **standalone**, with no paired `.1` declaration at all (see `procedures.py`'s own bullet) - Vitals/Results are registered defensively alongside it rather than waiting for a third incident to prove the same gap applies to them too.
- **`app/cda/results.py`** — `build_diagnostic_reports(section, patient_id) -> list[Observation | DiagnosticReport]`, per the IG's `CF-results.html` guidance plus its two published ConceptMaps (`ConceptMap-CF-ResultReportStatus`, `ConceptMap-CF-ResultStatus`) - again no CSV mapping table is published for Results, so field mapping is drawn directly from the markdown page's tables and the two fetched ConceptMaps. **The two ConceptMaps are genuinely value-identical** (both map the same six CDA `ActStatus` codes to `registered`/`final`/`cancelled`), confirmed by fetching both directly rather than assumed from covering the same source vocabulary - kept as one shared `STATUS_MAP` dict here since they really are the same crosswalk, just feeding two different target fields (`DiagnosticReport.status` and `Observation.status` both happen to accept the exact codes this map produces); unrecognized/absent falls back to `"unknown"`. C-CDA groups results into a Result Organizer (one per panel, e.g. a CBC) wrapping one or more Result Observations - the IG maps the organizer to a `DiagnosticReport` whose `.result` references one `Observation` per child Result Observation, mirrored here the same separate-top-level-resource way as `vitals.py`. `/value`'s `xsi:type` (via the existing `app.cda.parser::xsi_type` helper) drives which `Observation.value[x]` choice is populated - `PQ`→`valueQuantity`, `CD`/`CE`/`CV`/`CO`/`CS`→`valueCodeableConcept`, `INT`→`valueInteger`, `REAL`→`valueQuantity` (unit-less, since REAL elements never carry a `unit` attribute, matching the IG's own "leave unit fields empty" instruction), `ST`/`ED`→`valueString` (as a follow-up slice, `ED`'s own plain-text/narrative-reference case turned out to be structurally identical to `ST` once a genuine binary/attachment value was confirmed out of scope - see this file's own dedicated bullet below for why); `IVL_PQ` now maps too, per `mappingGuidance.md`'s own "Ranges of Physical Quantities" section - both bounds present → `.valueRange`, one bound only → `.valueQuantity` with a `comparator`. `referenceRange`'s `IVL_PQ` low/high is mapped (`referenceRange.text` is deferred - narrative-referenced, not resolvable without this app's disclosed narrative-`<text>` gap). `specimen` now maps too, via a real `Specimen` resource - see the dedicated bullet below. **Disclosed scope limits that remain**: `category` (both organizer and observation) still needs a live LOINC CLASSTYPE terminology-server lookup this app has no integration for, so it defaults to a disclosed `"laboratory"` constant instead (confirmed with the user directly - bulk-embedding the full licensed LOINC database to do a real per-code lookup is a genuinely different category of dependency than anything else in this app); `author`→Provenance is deferred - this app has never built a CDA-side Provenance resource. **Registered under both its "entries required" (`...2.3.1`) and "entries optional" (`...2.3`) templateIds** - see `vitals.py`'s own bullet for how this two-templateId gap was found and why Results is registered defensively alongside Vitals/Procedures.
- **`app/cda/procedures.py`** — `build_procedures(section, patient_id) -> list[Procedure]`, per the IG's `CF-procedures.html` guidance and its `CCDA-FHIR Procedure.csv` mapping table, plus its own published `ConceptMap-CF-ProcedureStatus`. **Template selection follows the IG's own explicit guidance, not this app's own judgment call**: C-CDA 2.1 defined three Procedure templates (Procedure Activity Act, Procedure Activity Observation, Procedure Activity Procedure); the IG maps only Procedure Activity Procedure ("the most complete... most vendors successfully and exclusively use" it, per the C-CDA 2.1 Companion Guide the IG itself cites, and notes the Act/Observation variants were removed entirely as of C-CDA 3.0) - a Procedure Activity Observation (better modeled as a Results-shaped `Observation` per the IG) is out of scope, not silently mismapped. `STATUS_MAP` (`ConceptMap-CF-ProcedureStatus`, public, reused by the generator) maps CDA `ActStatus` → `Procedure.status`; `"held"`/`"new"`/`"obsolete"`/`"suspended"` have no row in the published ConceptMap and fall back to `"unknown"`, same fallback philosophy as ORU's OBX-11/`DiagnosticReport` status map. `negationInd="true"` ("this procedure did NOT happen") overrides `statusCode` unconditionally to `"not-done"`, the same "check negation before consulting the status table" precedent Immunizations' own `negationInd` handling already established. **A genuine mutually-exclusive-source-shape constraint, per the IG**: `performedDateTime` is used when `effectiveTime@value` is populated (a point-in-time `TS`), else `performedPeriod` from `effectiveTime`'s `low`+`high` (an `IVL_TS` range) - resolved via the same `ivl_ts_bounds` distinction every other section's `effectiveTime` handling already uses. `Procedure.code` is genuinely optional per FHIR R4 (confirmed via `model_fields`, unlike most of this app's coded resources) - a procedure with no resolvable code still gets constructed rather than skipped, since status/date/bodySite can still be meaningful without one. **`performer`/`participant` (Service Delivery Location) now map too**, as a follow-up slice - see this file's own dedicated bullet below for the full design (`Procedure.performer.actor` → a real `PractitionerRole` wrapping `Practitioner`+`Organization`+`Location`; `participant[@typeCode=LOC]` → a separate `Procedure.location`). **Indication, Comment Activity, and `author`→`Procedure.recorder` have since shipped, as a follow-up slice** - see this file's own dedicated bullet below for the full design (`_build_reason_codes`/`_build_notes`/`_build_procedure_recorder`). **Bidirectional-transform reverse support and three new validation rules for all three additions shipped as an immediate follow-up** - see `app/transform/cda_ccd.py`'s own Procedures reverse-transform bullet below and `app/cda/validation.py`'s own bullet below (`cda.procedure-indication-missing-value`/`cda.procedure-comment-missing-text`/`cda.procedure-recorder-missing-author`). **Disclosed scope limits that remain**: `author`→Provenance (a real, separate Provenance *resource*, as opposed to the plain `Procedure.recorder` reference that DOES now ship) is still deferred - this app has no CDA-side Provenance builder anywhere. **A real, evidenced "entries optional" gap, not a defensive guess**: while fetching a real official HL7 History and Physical example (`HL7/C-CDA-Examples`, `Documents/History and Physical`) to research that document type, its own Procedures section was found declaring **only** `2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.2.7` (no trailing `.1`) - unlike that same document's Vital Signs/Results sections, which each declared both templateIds together, this one had no paired "entries required" declaration at all. This is the identical class of gap Allergies already shipped and fixed once (see that module's own bullet): the original single-templateId registration would have silently skipped this section's Procedure entirely. Fixed by registering `SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID_ENTRIES_OPTIONAL = "2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.2.7"` alongside `SECTION_TEMPLATE_ID` against the same `build_procedures` in `app/cda/registry.py`, updating `app/cda/validation.py`'s `_find_procedures_section` to check both (via the shared `_find_section` helper Allergies' own fix already established), and updating `app/cda/generator.py` to emit the "entries optional" templateId ~25% of the time for all three of Vitals/Results/Procedures - confirmed directly by re-running the real fetched example through `build_sectioned_bundle` before and after the fix (before: no `Procedure` resource in the output Bundle; after: one, correctly mapped) - see `tests/test_ccd_mapping.py`'s/`tests/test_cda_validation.py`'s/`tests/test_cda_registry.py`'s own entries-optional regression tests for all three sections.
- **Vitals' Blood Pressure/Pulse Oximetry Panel grouping, Results' `category`/`specimen`/`IVL_PQ`/`ED`, and Procedures' `performer`/`participant`** all shipped together as one follow-up slice, closing three sections' own longest-standing disclosed scope limits in one pass. Research was done the same "verify against primary sources, don't guess" way as every earlier slice: `CF-vitals.md`'s own construction guidance, `mappingGuidance.md`'s own "Ranges of Physical Quantities" section, the real CDA-core `Specimen`/`SpecimenRole`/`SpecimenPlayingEntity` structuredefs, the real `Procedure.csv` mapping table, and a real fetched HL7 C-CDA-Examples encounter (`Encounters/Inpatient Encounter Discharged to Rehab Location`) were all fetched directly, not paraphrased from a secondary source - fetching the raw `Procedure.csv` directly is what caught the real target shape (`Procedure.performer.actor[PractitionerRole]`, not a bare `Practitioner`), since an AI-summarized version of the same page would have been trusted otherwise.
  - **Vitals' Blood Pressure/Pulse Oximetry grouping** required restructuring `build_vital_signs`'s own per-organizer component loop into a first bucketing pass (peek each observation's own LOINC code - `_loinc_code`, which only trusts a code whose `@codeSystem` is genuinely LOINC's own OID - into systolic/diastolic/pulse-ox-primary/pulse-ox-concentration/pulse-ox-flow-rate/plain buckets) before any observation is actually built, since the systolic/diastolic pair and the pulse-ox reading+siblings never become independent top-level resources once grouped - unlike every other member, which gets built via the pre-existing `_build_vital_sign_observation` unconditionally. `_build_blood_pressure_panel` resolves **both** sides' code+value before building or recording anything, returning `None` (triggering a fall-back to two ordinary flat Vital Sign Observations) the moment either side fails to resolve - deliberately avoiding a half-recorded, orphaned panel that never makes it into the Bundle. `_apply_common_observation_fields` (effectiveDateTime/interpretation/method/bodySite) was extracted from `_build_vital_sign_observation`'s own inline logic once `_build_pulse_oximetry_panel` became a second real consumer of the identical extraction - the Pulse Oximetry Panel's own base observation needs these same fields, but its own code/value handling is genuinely different from a plain vital's (two fixed synonymous codings, always emitted regardless of which one the source used). Every new component's provenance is recorded against its own *original* `component[N]` index in the organizer (tracked through the bucketing pass, reusing the pre-existing `_member_base(index)` helper) even though it's no longer a standalone resource - the same "the source location must point at where the value REALLY came from" discipline this app's every other section already establishes.
  - **Results' `category`** is an unconditional, disclosed `"laboratory"` default (`record_inferred`) - the user was asked directly whether a small, hand-curated local LOINC-code→category table (for the specific codes this app's own generator/fixtures use) was worth building instead of a blanket default, and chose the blanket default; bulk-embedding the real LOINC database for a genuine per-code lookup was ruled out from the start as a different category of dependency than anything else in this app (a licensed, large external terminology dataset, not a small local judgment-call table). **`specimen`** builds a real `Specimen` resource (`_build_specimen`) from CDA's own `Specimen`/`SpecimenRole`/`SpecimenPlayingEntity` classes (confirmed via their own real, directly-fetched structuredefs - `specimenPlayingEntity/code` → `.type`, falling back to `.type.text` from `specimenPlayingEntity/name` only when no code resolves per the IG's own stated rule; `/quantity` → `.collection.quantity`; `/desc` → `.note`) plus the sibling **Specimen Collection Procedure** (`component/procedure[code/@code="17636008"]`, found via `_find_specimen_collection_procedure` scanning the organizer's own `component` children for that fixed SNOMED code) for `.collection.bodySite`. Per the IG's own explicit attachment rule, an organizer-level `<specimen>` becomes the default for every child result Observation (threaded through `_build_result_observation`'s new `default_specimen_id` parameter) *unless* that specific observation carries its own `<specimen>`, which builds a separate `Specimen` and overrides the default for that one Observation only - both `build_diagnostic_reports` and `_build_result_observation` needed their own return shape widened (`tuple[Observation, Specimen | None]`) to carry a newly-built observation-level `Specimen` back up to the flat resource list. **`IVL_PQ`** (fetched `mappingGuidance.md`'s own "Ranges of Physical Quantities" section, with its own worked examples) splits into `.valueRange` (both bounds resolve) or `.valueQuantity`+`comparator` (`<=`/`<` for a high-only bound, `>=`/`>` for a low-only bound, based on that bound's own `@inclusive` attribute, default `true`) - confirmed via direct `fhir.resources.R4B` introspection that `Observation.valueRange` and `Quantity.comparator` both exist and accept exactly these shapes. **`ED`**'s own literal IG target (a formal FHIR R5→R4 backport extension, `http://hl7.org/fhir/5.0/StructureDefinition/extension-Observation.value`) 404'd on every verification path tried (direct fetch, a FHIR JSON `Accept` header, the community FHIR extensions registry) - confirmed with the user directly that shipping an extension URL this app couldn't independently verify resolves to a real, live StructureDefinition wasn't worth it, so `ED`'s own plain-text (narrative-reference) case instead shares the pre-existing `ST` branch outright (structurally identical once a genuine binary/attachment value - the one case this simplification doesn't cover - is off the table), rather than a separate code path.
  - **Procedures' `performer`** → `Procedure.performer.actor` → a real **`PractitionerRole`** (not a simpler `Practitioner`+`.onBehalfOf` shortcut this app considered and the user explicitly rejected in favor of matching the IG's own literal target shape) wrapping a `Practitioner` (`_build_practitioner_from_assigned_entity` - `assignedPerson/name` is genuinely optional, confirmed by a real fetched example showing a performer with only an `id` and no name at all, mirroring `build_practitioner_from_xcn`'s own HL7v2-side "id or family or given" presence rule: skip the whole performer only when *neither* resolves), an optional `Organization` (`_build_organization_from_represented_org`, from `representedOrganization/name` - skipped entirely when absent, confirmed via a real fetched example, "Result with lab location," that also revealed `addr`/`telecom` can be nested under `representedOrganization` itself rather than directly under `assignedEntity`, so both locations are checked, `assignedEntity`'s own copy preferred when both exist), and an optional address-only `Location` (`_build_location_from_addr`, referenced via `PractitionerRole.location` - FHIR has no field to embed an address directly on `PractitionerRole` itself). `assignedEntity/id` is shared: it identifies both the `Practitioner` and, per the real `Procedure.csv` mapping table, the `PractitionerRole` itself - reused directly rather than re-parsed. `build_contact_point_from_telecom` was promoted to public in `app/cda/common.py` once this module became a second real consumer of the identical scheme/use resolution `_build_patient_telecoms` already had inline - unlike that function, the promoted one does no recording of its own, since a single-telecom caller's own resource id/relative path vary per call site (the caller records against the returned object's own fields, the same division of labor `build_quantity_from_pq`/`build_codeable_concept_from_cd` already establish). A small local `_build_address` helper (mirroring `app/cda/common.py::_build_patient_addresses`'s own per-field extraction style, but for a single, not repeating, address) is shared across all three of this module's own address-needing resources (performer's own `Location`, their `Organization`, the Service Delivery `Location`) - kept module-private since none of this app's *other* files need it yet, the same "extract only once a second FILE needs it" discipline already applied elsewhere. **`participant`** (`participant[@typeCode="LOC"]/participantRole[@classCode="SDLOC"]`, confirmed real templateId `2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.4.32` against a real fetched HL7 C-CDA-Examples encounter, `Encounters/Inpatient Encounter Discharged to Rehab Location`, whose own `performer`/`participant` shapes are the identical generic CDA datatypes this module reuses verbatim) → `Procedure.location` directly - a separate `Location` (`_build_service_delivery_location`, from `playingEntity/name`+`code`+`addr`+`telecom`), unrelated to a performer's own `PractitionerRole` machinery; only the first `typeCode="LOC"` participant is used, since `Procedure.location` is a singular `Reference` (confirmed via `model_fields`), the same "first-only, disclosed" precedent this app's EDI phases already established for batched input.
  - **No cross-performer/cross-procedure deduplication is attempted** for the `Practitioner`/`Organization`/`Location` resources this slice materializes - this app's existing opt-in `app/dedup.py::deduplicate_bundle` already covers the cross-resource case generically (the identical real-world shape X12 837P/837I's own billing-vs-rendering-provider duplication already established this pass exists for), so building a second, narrower dedup mechanism here would be redundant.
  - **Bidirectional-transform reverse support and five new C-CDA validation rules shipped as an immediate follow-up pass**, closing the two gaps this slice originally disclosed as out of scope (both confirmed with the user directly via "do both" - reverse-transform support AND new validation rules, not one or the other). The two pre-existing reverse-transform round-trip tests that share these same real fixtures (`test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_vital_signs_panel_and_members`/`test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_result_report_and_observations` in `tests/test_transform.py`), originally narrowed to only assert on heart rate/temperature and WBC/culture while the reverse builder genuinely couldn't reconstruct the rest, were widened back out once `app/transform/cda_ccd.py` gained real BP/pulse-ox-panel, IVL_PQ, and Specimen reversal (see that file's own bullet above) - both now assert on every field the extended fixtures carry, plus a new `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_procedure_performer_and_participant` covering the PractitionerRole→Practitioner/Organization/Location chain and the Service Delivery Location. `app/cda/validation.py` gained `cda.vitals-incomplete-blood-pressure-pair`/`cda.vitals-orphaned-pulse-oximetry-component`/`cda.result-specimen-missing-role`/`cda.procedure-performer-missing-identity`/`cda.procedure-participant-missing-identity` (see that file's own bullet above for the full per-rule detail) - the same "surface what the mapper silently defaults/skips" philosophy every other section's rules already establish, now extended to these three newly-materialized shapes too. **A further follow-up slice added three more Procedures rules, once its own Indication/Comment Activity/recorder mapping shipped**: `cda.procedure-indication-missing-value` (`info` - an Indication Observation with no resolvable `<value>` never contributes a `Procedure.reasonCode` entry), `cda.procedure-comment-missing-text` (`info` - a Comment Activity act with no resolvable `<text>` never contributes a `Procedure.note` entry), and `cda.procedure-recorder-missing-author` (`info` - a direct-child `<author>` with no nested `assignedAuthor` never contributes `Procedure.recorder`) - each walking the identical `entryRelationship`/`<author>` elements the mapper itself reads, so the rule can never see a different set of candidates than conversion does.
  - Verified via extended real fixtures (`ccd_vitals_basic.xml` gained a systolic/diastolic pair and an O2-saturation-plus-flow-rate reading; `ccd_results_basic.xml` gained an organizer-level `Specimen` with its own Specimen Collection Procedure, an `IVL_PQ`-valued observation, and an `ED`-valued one; `ccd_procedures_basic.xml` gained a fully-populated performer (name+organization+address) and a Service Delivery Location participant on its completed entry, plus an id-only performer on its negated entry) with field-assertion coverage in `tests/test_ccd_mapping.py` and content-correctness provenance assertions in `tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`, direct fuzz-coverage tests in `tests/test_generate_cda.py` for every new branch (complete-vs-incomplete BP pair, pulse-ox present/absent/with-optional-components, specimen at both attachment levels, all three of PQ/IVL_PQ/ED value types, performer present/absent/id-only-vs-named, participant present/absent), and a 450-seed manual fuzz sweep (CCD/Discharge Summary/History and Physical, 150 seeds each) run through the real converter, validator, and provenance-recording path together with zero crashes and zero error-severity validation findings.
- **`app/cda/discharge_summary.py`** — `DischargeSummaryBuilder` (`DISCHARGE_SUMMARY_TEMPLATE_ID = "2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.1.8"`): the second document type, and the first real proof that `build_sectioned_bundle` generalizes - `build_bundle()` is, like `CcdBuilder`'s, just `return build_sectioned_bundle(document)`. Confirmed structurally identical to CCD at the header level (`recordTarget`, `componentOf/encompassingEncounter`) by fetching a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples Discharge Summary and comparing directly, not assumed from the document-type name alone. **Two of its four originally-disclosed-as-deferred required sections turned out to be directly mappable, not a redesign but a correction once actually researched**: Hospital Discharge Diagnosis Section (templateId `...2.24`) and Discharge Medications Section (templateId `...2.11.1`) do each wrap their entry in a genuinely different outer Act template than Problems'/Medications' own (Hospital Discharge Diagnosis Act `...4.33`, Discharge Medication Act `...4.35`, not Problems' Concern Act `...4.3` or a bare `substanceAdministration`) - but fetching the real official HL7 C-CDA-Examples guide examples for both Acts (not just the Discharge Summary document example) showed each wraps the byte-for-byte identical nested template Problems/Medications already parse (`entryRelationship[typeCode=SUBJ]/observation[templateId=...4.4]` and `.../substanceAdministration[templateId=...4.16]` respectively) - see `app/cda/hospital_discharge_diagnosis.py`/`app/cda/discharge_medications.py`'s own bullets below for the fix. **The remaining two required sections, originally left genuinely deferred, now also ship** - see `app/cda/narrative_sections.py`'s own bullet below: Hospital Course and Plan of Treatment both now convert to a `DocumentReference`+`Binary` pair each, closing this document type's own full six-of-six-required-section coverage. Verified directly against a real Discharge Summary example (not assumed): alongside all six of these discharge-specific sections, it also carried a plain Problem List section and a plain Immunizations section using the exact templateIds this app already recognizes - so a real Discharge Summary now converts to something comprehensive (header + every recognized section). The same real Discharge Summary example also surfaced the Allergies "entries optional" section-templateId gap fixed in the `registry.py` bullet above.
- **`app/cda/hospital_discharge_diagnosis.py`** — `build_hospital_discharge_diagnoses(section, patient_id) -> list[Condition]`. Confirmed by fetching the real official HL7 C-CDA-Examples guide example for the Hospital Discharge Diagnosis Act (`Guide Examples/Hospital Discharge Diagnosis (V3)_2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.4.33`) and quoting it verbatim: `act[templateId=...4.33]/entryRelationship[typeCode=SUBJ]/observation[templateId=...4.4]` - the identical Problem Observation template and `entryRelationship[SUBJ]` wrapper shape Problems' own Concern Act uses, just a different outer Act. Reuses `app.cda.problems.build_condition` directly (promoted from `_build_condition`, module-private, once this became its second real consumer) rather than re-deriving the same clinicalStatus/onset/abatement logic. **The one genuine difference**: `Condition.category` is set to `"encounter-diagnosis"` (a real code from FHIR's own `condition-category` CodeSystem - a diagnosis made in the context of one encounter, distinct from Problems' own general problem-list entries, which never populate `.category` at all, having no comparable signal to distinguish one).
- **`app/cda/discharge_medications.py`** — `build_discharge_medication_requests(section, patient_id) -> list[MedicationRequest]`. Confirmed by fetching the real official HL7 C-CDA-Examples guide example for the Discharge Medication Act (`Guide Examples/Discharge Medication (V3)_2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.4.35`) and quoting it verbatim: `act[templateId=...4.35]/entryRelationship[typeCode=SUBJ]/substanceAdministration[templateId=...4.16]` - the identical Medication Activity template Medications already parses. Reuses `app.cda.medications.build_medication_request` directly (promoted from `_build_medication_request`, module-private, once this became its second real consumer) rather than re-deriving dosing/free-text-SIG logic. The Discharge Medication Act's own fixed `code` (LOINC `10183-2` "Hospital discharge medication") carries no information the nested `substanceAdministration` doesn't already have, so it isn't read.
- **`app/cda/history_and_physical.py`** — `HistoryAndPhysicalBuilder` (`HISTORY_AND_PHYSICAL_TEMPLATE_ID = "2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.1.3"`): the third document type, and a second real proof (after Discharge Summary) that `build_sectioned_bundle` generalizes - `build_bundle()` is, like the other two builders', just `return build_sectioned_bundle(document)`. Confirmed structurally identical to CCD/Discharge Summary at the header level (`recordTarget`, `componentOf/encompassingEncounter`) by fetching a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples History and Physical document and comparing directly - the same fetch that surfaced the Procedures "entries optional" gap fixed above. **A real, disclosed scope limit, originally left deferred, now closed - see `app/cda/narrative_sections.py`'s own bullet below**: an H&P Note's own nine IG-required narrative sections (Reason for Visit/Chief Complaint, History of Present Illness, Physical Exam, Assessment, Plan of Care, Review of Systems, Social History, Family History, General Status) now all convert to a `DocumentReference`+`Binary` pair each. Verified directly against the real fetched example (not assumed): alongside these H&P-specific sections, it also carried Allergies, Immunizations, Medications, Problems, Procedures, Results, and Vital Signs sections - **every one of this app's seven general-purpose section types in a single document** - so a real H&P Note now converts to something genuinely comprehensive (header + all seven general-purpose sections + all nine H&P-specific narrative ones). Unlike Discharge Summary (`force_encounter=True` in the generator, since a discharge is inherently tied to one hospitalization), an H&P's own `componentOf/encompassingEncounter` is genuinely optional in the generator too - a real H&P can precede any admission (e.g. a pre-operative visit), matching CCD's own optional-encounter treatment rather than Discharge Summary's forced one.
- **`app/cda/narrative_sections.py`** — `build_narrative_document_reference(section, patient_id, recorder=None) -> list[Resource]`, the one shared builder registered under all twelve narrative-section templateIds Discharge Summary (Hospital Course, Plan of Treatment) and History and Physical (the other nine's own three-way Reason-for-Visit choice plus the remaining eight) require, closing what had been this pillar's own last standing forward-conversion gap. **Confirmed with the user directly, not assumed, that a full FHIR-Document `Bundle(type="document")`+`Composition` redesign was out of scope**: `Composition.author` is FHIR-required (1..1+) and this app has never parsed `ClinicalDocument/author` at all, and the redesign would ripple into `app/dedup.py`/`app/provenance/`/`app/transform/cda_*.py` and every existing test asserting `Bundle.type == "collection"` - a much larger, cross-cutting undertaking than one slice. Instead, each present narrative section becomes its own `DocumentReference` (`.type` read live from the section's own real `<code>` via the already-established `build_codeable_concept_from_cd`, not hardcoded - `.description` from `<title>`) referencing a `Binary` (the extracted plain-text narrative) - mirroring `app/mappings/mdm.py::build_document_reference`/`_build_binary_from_obx`'s own already-established MDM/TXA pattern exactly. Returns `[]` when a section's own `<text>` has no usable narrative at all, the same "nothing to build, skip" convention every other section builder already follows, rather than a `DocumentReference` with an empty attachment. **Verified against the real HL7 C-CDA-Examples GitHub documents, not guessed** - both the templateId/LOINC pairs and the real narrative shapes (`<paragraph>`, `<list>`/`<item>`, `<table>`, plain mixed text with no wrapper at all) `extract_narrative_text` handles. **A genuine gotcha found during that research**: four of these twelve templateIds are *not* in the `2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.2.x` namespace every other section in this app uses - Hospital Course/History of Present Illness/Review of Systems are legacy IHE PCC templates (`1.3.6.1.4.1.19376.1.5.3.1.3.x`), Physical Exam/General Status are legacy HITSP/C32 templates (`2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.2.x` - one OID segment shorter than the native ones, easy to transcribe wrong) - both confirmed directly against the real example documents' own `<templateId root="...">` values. **Plan of Treatment and "Plan of Care" are the same template, not two**: `...22.2.10` (LOINC 18776-5) is required by both document types - real documents just title it differently ("PLAN OF CARE" on H&P, confirmed directly in the real fetched example) - so registering it once covers both document types for free, the same "one shared templateId, multiple document types" shape Problems/Medications already established. `extract_narrative_text(text_element) -> str` walks the real block-level shapes a `<text>` narrative block uses - `paragraph`/`list`+`item`/`table`/plain mixed text (confirmed via `Element.iter()` plus a local tag-stripping helper, since `app.cda.parser`'s own `find_child`/`find_all` only resolve known, named paths, not "whatever's here"). **Table rows render `" | "`-joined, not flattened via a naive whole-table `itertext()` join, confirmed necessary rather than a defensive guess**: Social History/Family History/Plan of Treatment all routinely narrate via `<table>` in real C-CDA documents (a Social History row like "Tobacco smoking status | Former smoker | 20050501 to 20090227"), and flattening without separators runs the column values together into one unlabeled, misleading string - a `<content ID="..."/>` anchor inside a table cell (the real C-CDA "entries derive from narrative" pattern) is handled for free, since it's `itertext()`-scoped to its own cell already. **Three of these twelve registered templateIds - Plan of Treatment, Social History, Family History - can carry real structured entries in the wild** (a Plan of Care Activity Observation, Social History/Smoking Status Observations, a Family History Organizer with member Observations, confirmed by inspecting the real fetched examples' own entry content); this slice itself deliberately did not parse any of them - the narrative `DocumentReference` was the only representation added here, disclosed as a natural next slice rather than attempted - **and that follow-up slice has since shipped**: `app/cda/social_history.py`/`family_history.py`/`plan_of_treatment.py` each override this module's own registration for their one templateId with a combined builder that still calls `build_narrative_document_reference` internally (the narrative pair keeps being built unconditionally) *and* parses the section's own real structured entries into `Observation`/`FamilyMemberHistory`/`CarePlan` resources alongside it - see each module's own bullet below for the full field mapping and disclosed design decisions. `recorder` is accepted (matching `SECTION_BUILDERS`' own uniform-signature requirement) and, as of a follow-up slice, genuinely acted on too - `.type.coding[0].code`/`.display`, `.description`, and `Binary.data` (via a precise `xpath_location("text")` when `<text>` has no children at all, else a disclosed `"text (×N blocks)"` marker mirroring MDM's own OBX-5 join and SIU's own NTE-3 join) are each recorded, see `app/cda/validation.py`'s own `cda.narrative-section-missing-text` rule bullet and CLAUDE.md's own Data Specification section for the instrumentation timeline. Bidirectional transform (`app/transform/cda_ccd.py::_build_narrative_section`, shared by all three reverse builders) now regenerates every one of these sections on the reverse trip too, as a further follow-up slice - see that function's own bullet above for the LOINC-code-driven resolution and the disclosed table-flattens-to-paragraphs lossy simplification. Verified via 17 direct unit tests in `tests/test_cda_narrative_sections.py` (every real block shape `extract_narrative_text` handles, plus `build_narrative_document_reference`'s own construction/empty-text/missing-code/missing-title branches), extending both real fixtures (`discharge_summary_basic.xml` gained Hospital Course + a table-shaped Plan of Treatment; `history_and_physical_basic.xml` gained all eight remaining H&P-specific sections + Plan of Treatment, its pre-existing Reason for Visit section now asserted as converting rather than being skipped) with field-assertion coverage in `tests/test_ccd_mapping.py`, and a generator pass (`app/cda/generator.py`'s three new `include_hospital_course`/`include_plan_of_treatment`/`include_hp_narrative_sections` flags, mirroring `include_discharge_specific_sections`'s own established shape exactly) with dedicated presence/absence-varies-across-seeds fuzz coverage per section in `tests/test_generate_cda.py`, including a dedicated proof that none of these ever occur on a generated CCD.
- **`app/cda/social_history.py`** — `build_social_history_resources(section, patient_id, recorder=None) -> list[Resource]`, the first of narrative_sections.py's own three disclosed "can carry real structured entries" sections to gain one: the narrative `DocumentReference`+`Binary` pair (always built, unconditionally reusing `narrative_sections.build_narrative_document_reference`) plus one `Observation` per recognized Social History Observation entry - both representations coexist, neither replacing the other. **Per the real, published "C-CDA on FHIR" Social History mapping** (`github.com/HL7/ccda-on-fhir/blob/master/input/pagecontent/CF-social.md` - confirmed by fetching it directly, not assumed to not exist just because no `CF-socialhistory.html`/`CF-familyhistory.html` page exists the way `CF-vitals.html`/`CF-results.html` do): target is `Observation`, `.category` fixed to `social-history` (the FHIR `observation-category` CodeSystem), `.status` fixed to `final` (C-CDA's own `statusCode` is always `completed` for this template, confirmed against a real fetched example - the same "no status-mapping ambiguity at all" precedent `app/cda/vitals.py`'s own organizer/observation statusCode already established), and `.value[x]` dispatches on the entry's own `/value` xsi:type - `PQ`/`CD`/`INT`/`ST` mapped, `IVL_PQ` deferred, the same "map the dominant scalar/coded shape now" judgment `app/cda/results.py`'s own identical gap already established. Recognizes both the base Social History Observation templateId (`...4.38`) and the Smoking-Status-specific one (`...4.78`, a field-for-field identical specialization, confirmed against a real fetched example) against the one shared builder. **The IG's "Observations to Extensions" cases now ship**: Birth Sex (`...22.4.200`) -> `us-core-birthsex`, Sex (`...22.4.507`) -> `us-core-sex` (both `valueCode`), and Gender Identity (`...34.3.45`) -> `us-core-genderIdentity` (`valueCodeableConcept`) attach to `Patient` rather than becoming Observations, per the IG's explicit "a FHIR Observation should not be created". **Gender Identity and Tribal Affiliation specialise Social History Observation and so declare `...22.4.38` as well as their own** (`templateId min:2` over base `SocialHistoryObservation`), so excluding them from the Observation walk has to be explicit - matching the generic templateId alone converted a Gender Identity Observation into exactly the plain social-history Observation the IG forbids, which is a bug this fixed rather than a feature it added. `apply_patient_extensions` takes the `Patient` resource rather than `patient.id`, so it is called from `build_sectioned_bundle` directly rather than being part of the registered section builder - `SECTION_BUILDERS` hands builders only the id, and these entries have no resource of their own to return. **Tribal Affiliation (`...22.4.506`) is excluded from the walk but deliberately not mapped**: its extension is a complex one (a `tribalAffiliation` CodeableConcept plus an `isEnrolled` boolean) and the C-CDA template publishes no element to read the enrolled flag from, so it goes to the drop register rather than being guessed at. Pregnancy Status/Intent have their own US Core profiles and their own moodCode-driven split; not attempted. Verified via 10 direct unit tests in `tests/test_cda_social_history.py` (both recognized templateIds, all four mapped value xsi:types, missing-code skip, unrecognized-templateId skip, multi-entry, recorder-present/absent) plus field-assertion coverage against the real `history_and_physical_basic.xml` fixture's own extended Smoking Status entry in `tests/test_ccd_mapping.py`, and dedicated generator fuzz coverage (`_random_social_history_entry`, splitting ~60/40 between the Smoking-Status-specific and base templateId shapes) proving both presence/absence and both templateIds occur across seeds in `tests/test_generate_cda.py`.
- **`app/cda/family_history.py`** — `build_family_history_resources(section, patient_id, recorder=None) -> list[Resource]`, the second of the three. **No official "C-CDA on FHIR" mapping page covers Family History at all** (confirmed the same way as Social History - no `CF-familyhistory.md` file exists in the IG's own `input/pagecontent` directory), so the FHIR-side field mapping is this app's own disclosed, self-derived choice - the same "no official crosswalk exists, disclosed local mapping" precedent several EDI families already established (e.g. 276/277 -> `Task`) - `FamilyMemberHistory` being the obvious, purpose-built FHIR target, not a guess between several plausible resources. **Source shape confirmed against three real fetched HL7 C-CDA-Examples Guide Examples**: Family History Organizer (`...4.45`) wraps a `subject/relatedSubject/code` (the relationship, e.g. `code="FTH"` father, codeSystem `2.16.840.1.113883.5.111` confirmed to be HL7's own v3 RoleCode OID, newly added to `app.cda.common.OID_TO_FHIR_SYSTEM`) and a nested `subject/relatedSubject/subject/administrativeGenderCode`, plus one or more Family History Observations (`...4.46`). **A genuine gotcha found during that research**: the Family History Observation's own `/code` is a *fixed* code (`75323-6` "Condition") describing the template's own kind, not the diagnosis - the real diagnosis lives in `/value[xsi:type=CD]` instead, a genuinely different shape from every other CD-valued section in this app. Two optional nested `entryRelationship`s, both confirmed real: `typeCode=SUBJ`/`inversionInd=true` wraps an Age Observation (`...4.31`) -> `.condition[].onsetAge`; `typeCode=CAUS` wraps a Family History Death Observation (`...4.47`, confirmed to always carry the fixed SNOMED code "Dead" as its own `/value` - so only the relationship's own *presence* is the real signal, the same "presence alone, not the nested value" precedent `app/cda/immunizations.py`'s negation handling already established) -> `.condition[].contributedToDeath = True`, a real field `FamilyMemberHistoryCondition` happens to expose for exactly this (confirmed via `model_fields`). **The relative's own deceased status** (`sdtc:deceasedInd`/`sdtc:deceasedTime`, an sdtc-namespace CDA extension, confirmed present - if commented-out - in the same real fetched example) maps to `.deceasedBoolean`/`.deceasedDate` - the first module in this app to read an sdtc-namespaced element directly (`{urn:hl7-org:sdtc}...`, Clark notation) rather than through `find_child`/`find_all` (both CDA-namespace-only by design). **A real choice-type conflict caught by direct construction, not assumed**: `deceased[x]` only allows one of `deceasedBoolean`/`deceasedDate` to be set at once (a genuine `pydantic.ValidationError` otherwise) - resolved by preferring `.deceasedDate` whenever a real `deceasedTime` also resolves (strictly more informative than the bare boolean), falling back to `.deceasedBoolean` only when no date is present; a small `_format_partial_date` helper reformats whatever precision the source value actually carries (a bare year is common, confirmed against the real example's own commented-out usage) rather than discarding anything shorter than a full 8-digit date the way `app.cda.common.parse_partial_ts` would. `.status` fixed to `"completed"` (the organizer's own `statusCode` is fixed per spec, confirmed against the real example - the second real consumer of Vitals' own "no status-mapping ambiguity" precedent). A real `ElementTree.__bool__` `DeprecationWarning` (element truthiness based on child count, not identity) was caught by the test suite and fixed by using `next(..., None) is not None` for the `CAUS`-relationship presence check instead of `any(...)`. Verified via 14 direct unit tests in `tests/test_cda_family_history.py` (missing-relatedSubject/missing-condition skips, Age/Death Observation presence, gender mapping, all three deceased[x] resolution branches including the partial-precision date reformatting, multi-organizer, recorder-present/absent) plus field-assertion coverage against the real `history_and_physical_basic.xml` fixture's own extended Family History entry (father, coronary arteriosclerosis, onset age 58, contributed to death) in `tests/test_ccd_mapping.py`, and dedicated generator fuzz coverage (`_random_family_history_entry`) proving presence/absence, both deceased[x] branches, `contributedToDeath`, and `onsetAge` all occur across seeds in `tests/test_generate_cda.py`.
- **`app/cda/plan_of_treatment.py`** — `build_plan_of_treatment_resources(section, patient_id, recorder=None) -> list[Resource]`, the third of the three, and the one with the least obvious target FHIR resource of the three: C-CDA's own Plan of Treatment section allows a genuinely wide variety of entry *classes* (Planned Act/Encounter/Observation/Procedure/Medication Activity/Supply, Instruction, Appointment...), and no official IG mapping exists for this section either (confirmed the same way as Family History). **`CarePlan.activity[]` is the disclosed, deliberate choice**: FHIR's own `CarePlan` is explicitly designed to hold a heterogeneous list of planned activities without forcing each into its own separate resource - `CarePlanActivityDetail.kind` (an enum of resource types) exists specifically to hint at what a planned item *would* materialize as without this app needing to actually build that separate resource. **Scoped to exactly the two moodCode-classified entry shapes confirmed against real fetched HL7 C-CDA-Examples**: Planned Observation (`...4.44`, wrapped in `<observation moodCode="RQO">`, confirmed directly against the real Discharge Summary example's own Plan of Care section - "Colonoscopy," `effectiveTime` using `<center value="...">`, **the fourth legal IVL_TS shape**, newly added to `app.cda.parser::ivl_ts_bounds`/`app.cda.common::effective_time_location` alongside the three this app already handled) and Planned Procedure (`...4.41`, wrapped in `<procedure moodCode="RQO">`, confirmed via a second real fetched Guide Example - field-for-field identical to Planned Observation's own code/statusCode/effectiveTime shape). **Every other planned entry class now converts too**: Planned Encounter (`...22.4.40`), Planned Medication Activity (`...22.4.42`), Planned Immunization Activity (`...22.4.120`), Planned Supply (`...22.4.43`), Planned Intervention Act (`...22.4.146`) and Instruction (`...22.4.20`) - all carry the same code/statusCode/effectiveTime shape, so one extraction serves them all and `_RECOGNIZED_ENTRY_SHAPES` maps each to its wrapper element and its `CarePlanActivityDetail.kind`. **Two shapes share the `act` tag and two share `substanceAdministration`, so dispatch is by templateId rather than tag.** `kind` is a disclosed judgement call rather than a mapping - the value set is fixed and no crosswalk exists, so Planned Encounter -> `Appointment`, Planned Medication/Immunization -> `MedicationRequest` and Instruction -> `CommunicationRequest` take their one obvious counterpart while everything else keeps the generic `ServiceRequest`, **Planned Supply included**: there is no `SupplyRequest` code, and a C-CDA supply covers both durable equipment and medication, so `DeviceRequest` would be right only half the time. Planned Coverage (`...22.4.129`) stays unrecognized - it plans insurance coverage rather than a clinical activity, and no `kind` code describes one. **`.status`/`.intent`/`CarePlanActivityDetail.kind` are all disclosed, self-derived choices with no IG crosswalk to verify against**: the `CarePlan` itself gets fixed `status="active"`/`intent="plan"`; each activity's own `.kind` is fixed to `"ServiceRequest"` regardless of source entry shape, the closest general-purpose fit for "a planned clinical service" without a dedicated resource to materialize; `.status` per activity uses a small, disclosed `STATUS_MAP` from the entry's own `statusCode` (not `moodCode`, uniformly `RQO` and carrying no per-activity distinction) - unrecognized/absent falls back to `"unknown"`. `effectiveTime`'s resolved (low, high) pair maps to `.scheduledPeriod` when both bounds resolve and differ, else `.scheduledString` for a single point - confirmed via direct construction that `CarePlanActivityDetail.status`/`CarePlan.status`/`.intent` are all eagerly required despite not showing in `model_fields`, the same "don't trust `model_fields` alone" lesson this app has learned repeatedly. Verified via 11 direct unit tests in `tests/test_cda_plan_of_treatment.py` (both entry shapes, the `<center>` IVL_TS shape, `scheduledString`-vs-`scheduledPeriod` branching, unrecognized-status fallback, missing-code/missing-entries skips, recorder-present/absent) plus field-assertion coverage against both real fixtures' own extended Plan of Treatment entries (`discharge_summary_basic.xml`'s Planned Observation, `history_and_physical_basic.xml`'s Planned Procedure) in `tests/test_ccd_mapping.py`, and dedicated generator fuzz coverage (`_random_plan_of_treatment_entry`, splitting between both entry shapes and including a deliberately-unrecognized statusCode) proving presence/absence, both entry shapes, and the `"unknown"` status fallback all occur across seeds in `tests/test_generate_cda.py`.
- **`app/cda/validation.py`'s own new rules for these three sections** - `cda.social-history-missing-code`/`cda.social-history-effective-time-in-future`, `cda.family-history-missing-relationship`/`cda.family-history-missing-condition-code`, `cda.plan-of-treatment-missing-code`/`cda.plan-of-treatment-status-unrecognized` - all `info`/`warning`, mirroring the identical "surface what the mapper already silently defaults/skips" rule shape Results'/Procedures' own rules already established, each walking the section via the same entry-recognition logic its own conversion-side module uses (`_iter_social_history_observations`/`_iter_family_history_organizers`/`_iter_plan_of_treatment_entries`) so validation can never see a different entry set than conversion does. Coexists with the pre-existing `cda.narrative-section-missing-text` rule (still checking each section's own `<text>` independently of these new entry-level rules). Verified via 9 direct unit tests in `tests/test_cda_validation.py`, including a "clean entry produces zero findings" case per section.
- **Format auto-detection (`app/pipeline.py`)** — `is_xml(raw_text)` strips a leading BOM **before** whitespace (not after: the BOM character isn't itself whitespace, so a leading BOM stops a whitespace-only `lstrip()` before it ever reaches the whitespace that follows it — this exact ordering bug was caught by direct testing before shipping), then checks for a leading `<`. `convert_to_bundle`/`validate_any` dispatch to `convert_cda_to_bundle`/`convert_hl7_to_bundle` and `validate_cda`/`validate_hl7` accordingly. This keeps the single unified "paste anything, click Convert/Validate" UX every prior HL7v2 message type has used — never a new route/UI branch per input format.
- **`app/cda/generator.py`** — `generate_ccd(rng)`/`generate_discharge_summary(rng) -> str`, the C-CDA mirror of `app/generators/{adt,siu,oru,mdm}.py`, but living inside `app/cda/` rather than `app/generators/`: there are only two document types so far, so a separate `app/generators/cda.py` with its own sub-registry would be premature relative to HL7v2's real 4-type/20-trigger registry need. Both generator functions are thin wrappers around a shared `_generate_sectioned_document(rng, document_template_id, doc_code, doc_code_display, title, force_encounter=False)` - the generator-side mirror of `build_sectioned_bundle` above, extracted the same "second real consumer" way once `generate_discharge_summary` existed. `force_encounter=True` for Discharge Summary (unlike CCD, where `componentOf/encompassingEncounter` is genuinely optional and stays a 50/50 `maybe()`) - a real discharge summary is inherently tied to one hospitalization, so `_random_encounter` gained a `force: bool` parameter to skip its own presence check when set. Reuses `app/generators/base.py`'s format-agnostic primitives directly (`maybe()`, `random_person_name()`, `random_address()`, `random_phone()`, `random_sex()`, `random_identifier()`, `random_datetime_near_now()`/`format_hl7_datetime()` - the latter's `YYYYMMDDHHMMSS` output is exactly CDA's own `@value` TS digit shape) rather than re-deriving name/address pools; net-new is only a small SNOMED problem-code pool, an RxNorm medication-code pool (plus a small route-code and free-text-SIG pool for the Medications section), and the XML field builders. Built via f-string templates (same style as the hand-authored `ccd_*.xml` fixtures), not `ElementTree` tree-building, and self-checked via `parse_document()` before returning (mirrors `_assemble()`'s `parse_message()` self-check in the HL7v2 generators) - full-conversion-success verification is left to `tests/test_generate_cda.py`, matching the HL7v2 precedent. **Every random value is drawn from the passed-in `rng`, never `uuid.uuid4()`** - `uuid4()` uses its own unseeded internal randomness and would silently break the "same seed → same output byte-for-byte" guarantee every generator in this project must uphold; `_random_uuid_like(rng)` builds a UUID-shaped root string entirely from `rng.choice()` calls instead. Field-population policy deliberately exercises both branches of everything the review-caught `common.py`/`problems.py` bugs above involved (root-only vs. root+extension `id`s, date-only vs. full-timestamp `effectiveTime`, recognized vs. unrecognized encounter class, Concern-Act-only vs. nested-Status-Observation `clinicalStatus`), while keeping onset/birth date ranges constrained so no combination can ever trip an error-severity validation rule (see the file's own comments for the exact day-count bounds and why) - the same "guarantee convertibility/validity by construction" contract the HL7v2 generators already keep. `_random_medication_entry` follows the identical philosophy for the Medications section: `moodCode` splits ~50/50 INT/EVN, `statusCode` is mostly a recognized `STATUS_MAP` key (~85%) with an occasional unrecognized one (`new`/`held`, exercising the `"unknown"`-fallback validation finding), and dosing representation splits ~45/35/20 across structured-only/free-text-only/neither - direct fuzz coverage of `_build_dosage`'s three branches, mirroring how the Problems entry generator exercises `_resolve_clinical_status`'s two branches. `_random_allergy_entry` covers the Allergies section's larger branch space the same way: ~10% `negationInd`, and within that ~30% still carrying a resolvable allergen vs. a `nullFlavor` one (both `_resolve_allergen_code` negation paths); the Status Observation (~50%), Criticality Observation (~40%), and Reaction Observation with its own optional nested Severity Observation (~50%/~60%) are each independently `maybe()`-gated, so every nesting depth this section supports gets exercised in some combination across seeds. `_random_immunization_entry` splits `moodCode` ~80/20 EVN/INT (direct fuzz coverage of `build_immunizations()`'s mood-based skip - the INT branch must produce zero `Immunization` resources from that entry), and within EVN further splits ~15% negated; `statusCode` is mostly a recognized `STATUS_MAP` key with an occasional unrecognized one (`"draft"`, exercising the `"completed"`-fallback validation finding); dosing (`routeCode`+`doseQuantity`) and `lotNumberText` are each independently `maybe()`-gated. Registered in the *existing* `app/generators/registry.py::_GENERATORS` flat dict as `("CDA", "CCD")`/`("CDA", "DISCHARGESUMMARY")` (see above) rather than a parallel CDA-specific registry - `DISCHARGESUMMARY` is uppercase (unlike the human-readable `"DischargeSummary"` label) to match `generate()`'s own `.upper()`-normalized lookup, which requires its dict keys to already be uppercase, same as every HL7v2 trigger value.
- **`app/cda/validation.py`** — `validate_document(document) -> ValidationReport`, the C-CDA mirror of `app/validation/`, composed the same small-`_rule_*`-by-concatenation way. No dispatch-by-document-type registry even with three document types: every section rule walks the document generically (the same path `build_sectioned_bundle` walks), so a CCD and a Discharge Summary carrying the same section get identical findings for free. Only `resolve_trigger_event` knows the document types by name. Reuses `ValidationFinding`/`ValidationReport` as-is (a CDA finding uses a path-like `segment`, `field` left `None`) and `parse_comparable_datetime`/`not_in_future`/`is_before` directly, since CDA's `@value` shares HL7's TS digit shape.

  Rules follow one philosophy — **surface what the mapper silently defaults or skips**, never raise. Each section gets the same shape: a missing-code `info` ("the converter will silently skip this entry"), an unrecognized-status `info` reusing the mapper's own `STATUS_MAP` so the two can't drift, and a date-in-future `warning` / before-birth `error`. Each `_iter_*` walks entries the *same* way its conversion-side module does, so validation can never see a different entry set than conversion. Sections registered under two templateIds are checked under both, via the shared `_find_section` — a single-templateId helper silently ran zero rules against the "entries optional" variant once, and that shared helper exists so it can't recur. Problems'/Medications' rule bodies are shared with Hospital Discharge Diagnosis/Discharge Medications via a `rule_prefix` parameter, so the four can't drift either.

  Section-specific rules worth knowing: Allergies' missing-allergen check skips negated entries (unlike every other missing-code rule, since negation gets a real text fallback there); Immunizations excludes `INT`-mood entries entirely, matching the mapper, and skips the status rule for negated ones; Vitals reports an incomplete BP pair or an orphaned pulse-ox sibling (both fall back to flat observations); Results reports a `<specimen>` with no resolvable role; Procedures reports a performer or SDLOC participant with no resolvable identity, plus an Indication with no value, a Comment Activity with no text, and an `<author>` with no `assignedAuthor`; the twelve narrative sections share one `cda.narrative-section-missing-text` rule. **Deliberately not added**: a rule for Results' unconditional `category` default (it fires for every item regardless of input, so it isn't a problem condition) or for an unresolvable `IVL_PQ` (no equivalent rule exists for any other value type).

  `_rule_encounter` originally nested its period checks inside `if low:`, so an `IVL_TS` with only `@high` produced zero findings — `low_dt`/`high_dt` are now computed independently, with a dedicated end-in-future check. Convertibility is checked last, mirroring `app/validation/engine.py` exactly: `get_document_builder` (unregistered → `info`), then `build_bundle` (`MappingError`/`MissingSegmentError`/`ValidationError` → `error`, anything else logged + `error`). It never re-derives a parallel required-fields table.
- **Known, disclosed scope limits for this slice**: three document types (CCD, Discharge Summary, History and Physical Note); every IG-required section either document type has is now recognized - seven general-purpose ones (Problems, Medications, Allergies, Immunizations, Vital Signs, Results, Procedures) recognized on any of the three document types, two Discharge-Summary-specific structured ones (Hospital Discharge Diagnosis, Discharge Medications, see `hospital_discharge_diagnosis.py`/`discharge_medications.py`), and the ten narrative-only ones - Discharge Summary's own Hospital Course/Plan of Treatment, and History and Physical's own nine (Reason for Visit/Chief Complaint, History of Present Illness, Physical Exam, Assessment, Plan of Care, Review of Systems, Social History, Family History, General Status - Plan of Care/Plan of Treatment being the identical shared template, see `app/cda/narrative_sections.py`'s own docstring) - each converting to a `DocumentReference`+`Binary` pair rather than a full FHIR-Document `Bundle(type="document")`/`Composition` (author/custodian would become load-bearing, and a real redesign would ripple into `app/dedup.py`/`app/provenance/`/`app/transform/cda_*.py` - confirmed out of scope with the user directly, not assumed). **Structured-entry parsing for Plan of Treatment/Social History/Family History has since shipped too**, as a further follow-up slice: `app/cda/social_history.py`/`family_history.py`/`plan_of_treatment.py` each parse the section's own real structured entries (Social History/Smoking Status Observations -> `Observation`, a Family History Organizer -> `FamilyMemberHistory`, a Plan of Care Activity Observation/Planned Procedure -> `CarePlan.activity[]`) *alongside* the narrative `DocumentReference` every section still gets unconditionally - see each module's own bullet above for the full field mapping, and `app/cda/validation.py`'s own new `cda.social-history-*`/`cda.family-history-*`/`cda.plan-of-treatment-*` rules for the matching "surface what the mapper silently skips" validation coverage - provenance instrumentation and bidirectional-transform regeneration, both originally deferred alongside the narrative-sections slice itself, have likewise shipped as their own immediate follow-ups (see `app/cda/narrative_sections.py`'s own bullet above and `app/transform/cda_ccd.py::_build_narrative_section`); the three newly-structured sections' own bidirectional-transform regeneration has since shipped too, as a further immediate follow-up - `_build_narrative_section`'s own `extra_entries` parameter now injects the reversed `Observation`/`FamilyMemberHistory`/`CarePlan` structured entries alongside the narrative pair (see `_build_social_history_entries`/`_build_family_history_entries`/`_build_plan_of_treatment_entries` in `app/transform/cda_ccd.py`). **Vital Signs' Blood Pressure/Pulse Oximetry component-grouping, Results' `category`/`specimen`/`IVL_PQ`/`ED`-typed values, and Procedures' `performer`/`participant` have since shipped too**, as a further follow-up slice - see each module's own bullet above and the dedicated bullet documenting the slice as a whole. **Bidirectional-transform reverse support and five new C-CDA validation rules for those same three additions have since shipped too, as an immediate follow-up slice** - see `app/transform/cda_ccd.py`'s own Vitals/Results/Procedures reverse-transform bullets above (BP/pulse-ox panel reversal, IVL_PQ/Specimen reversal, and PractitionerRole/Service-Delivery-Location performer/participant reversal) and `app/cda/validation.py`'s own bullet above (`cda.vitals-incomplete-blood-pressure-pair`/`cda.vitals-orphaned-pulse-oximetry-component`/`cda.result-specimen-missing-role`/`cda.procedure-performer-missing-identity`/`cda.procedure-participant-missing-identity`) - the disclosed gap this paragraph used to name is now closed. **Procedures' own Indication/Comment Activity cross-references, and `author`→`Procedure.recorder`, have since shipped too** (see `app/cda/procedures.py`'s own bullet below - `_build_reason_codes`/`_build_notes`/`_build_procedure_recorder`, with full provenance recording for all three). What remains genuinely deferred: Results'/Procedures' own `author`->**Provenance** specifically (a real, separate Provenance *resource* - this app has no CDA-side Provenance builder anywhere, distinct from the plain `Procedure.recorder` reference that DOES now ship), and (**forward direction only - see below**) `originalText` resolution on a structured section's own coded field. **`originalText` -> `CodeableConcept.text` now ships on the forward side**, per the C-CDA on FHIR IG's own `mappingGuidance.html` ("when converting to a FHIR data type that contains a text field, like CodeableConcept, this is a direct map"; a `<reference value="#ID"/>` must be de-referenced, markup removed, and stored as plain text) - both real-world shapes are handled: an inline `<originalText>text</originalText>` is read directly by `app/cda/common.py::build_codeable_concept_from_cd`, and the `<originalText><reference value="#ID"/></originalText>` shape is converted into the inline shape up front by the new `resolve_narrative_references(section)`, called once per section from `build_sectioned_bundle`. **The pre-pass design is a deliberate choice over threading, disclosed rather than assumed**: `build_codeable_concept_from_cd` has ~60 call sites across 13 modules, most several frames below the section element, and stdlib `ElementTree` has no parent pointers to walk back up with - resolving once at the single choke point that genuinely has both the narrative and the entries in scope means not one of those call sites changes. The mutation is **additive only**: the `<reference>` child stays exactly where it was and only `originalText`'s own (previously empty) `.text` is filled in, so a provenance `source_location` pointing at `code/originalText` stays accurate, the raw reference is still readable, and no other pillar observes it (the parsed tree is this app's own per-request copy; validation parses its own separate document). `narrative_anchor_text(section)` builds the `{ID: plain text}` lookup, flattening nested inline markup via `itertext()` the same way `app/cda/narrative_sections.py`'s own extraction already does - note CDA's own spelling is capital-`ID`, not `xml:id` or lowercase `id`, confirmed against a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples CCD whose Encounters section carries exactly this shape. **The reverse direction now regenerates `<originalText>` too, as an immediate follow-up slice**: `app/transform/cda_ccd.py::_build_cd_element(tag, concept, prefix_attrs="")` is a new shared builder taking a whole `CodeableConcept` rather than the bare `Coding` `_build_cd_attrs` takes, emitting a nested `<originalText>` when `.text` is present and staying byte-identically self-closing when it isn't; all ~30 `_build_cd_attrs` call sites were converted to it (most of which previously extracted a local `coding[0]` purely to build the attribute string, so the conversion removed that local too). **A disclosed, deliberate simplification**: it always regenerates the *inline* `<originalText>text</originalText>` shape, never the `<reference value="#ID"/>` one - `CodeableConcept.text` keeps no record of which shape it came from (the forward side's own `resolve_narrative_references` collapses both into the same field), and regenerating a reference would additionally require inventing a narrative anchor and a matching `<text>` block this reverse builder doesn't otherwise produce; the inline shape re-parses to the identical `.text` on the next forward pass, so it's round-trip *stable* from the first reverse pass onward - the same "recoverable content, not recoverable original shape" tradeoff `_build_narrative_section`'s own table-flattens-to-paragraphs simplification already discloses. Verified via `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_original_text_as_codeable_concept_text` (both source shapes, plus a second cycle proving stability) and `test_ccd_round_trip_escapes_xml_special_characters_in_original_text`. **A real, pre-existing round-trip bug was found and fixed during that same pass, unrelated to `originalText` itself**: a resource-type-count sweep across 300 generated documents (CCD/Discharge Summary/History and Physical) showed 28 seeds losing a `Specimen` resource entirely on a reverse trip - `_build_result_organizer` only ever regenerated the *organizer*-level `<specimen>` (from `DiagnosticReport.specimen`), silently dropping a genuine *observation*-level override (`Observation.specimen` pointing at a different `Specimen`), which the forward `_build_result_observation`'s own `own_specimen`/`default_specimen_id` split explicitly supports per `CF-results.md`'s own attachment rule. Confirmed pre-existing rather than a regression by re-running the identical sweep against the pre-refactor code (28 failures before, 28 after), then fixed by having `_build_result_observation_element` regenerate its own `<specimen>` whenever the observation's own reference differs from the organizer's resolved default - dropping the sweep to **zero failures across all 300 seeds**.
