  - **Two changes that were each fine alone combined badly.** `REF*EI` attaches the *practice's* tax ID to every party, and dedup had just started merging on any shared identifier - so two different clinicians billing under one practice merged into one person, with `_rewrite_references` repointing the loser's `Claim.careTeam[].provider` at the survivor. An employer tax ID is now excluded from the identity key (it identifies the practice, not the party), and merging **unions** identifiers onto the survivor, since which resource came first in the Bundle otherwise decided whether a tax ID survived at all.
  - **A semantic error that round-tripped cleanly and was still wrong.** `Coverage.relationship` is the *beneficiary's* relationship to the subscriber, which is what `PAT01` states; `SBR02` states the *subscriber's* own relationship to the insured. Treating them as interchangeable fallbacks reported a dependent child as `"self"`, and the reverse direction wrote the child's relationship into the subscriber's own `SBR02`. A dependent claim now reads `PAT01` only, and a claim with neither states no relationship rather than guessing. **A test asserted the broken behaviour**, which is how it survived - the round trip was self-consistently wrong.
  - **`group_by_leader` skips non-members rather than stopping at them**, so a party's "own" segment group ran past the end of its 2010 loop and absorbed claim-level ones - a claim-level `REF*EI` became the dependent child's tax ID, which then fed the dedup key above. `nm1_members` now stops at the first segment that leaves the party loop, and `find_nm1_group` is built on it rather than on `group_by_leader`.
  - Also fixed: 835's reverse builder never got the address emission (its parties are `N1`-led, so they bypass the `NM1` builders where it lives) and the resource-count invariant cannot see a lost *field*; `HL04` was inverted in all three 837 reverse builders; `_record_address`/`_record_telecoms` derived the source element from the *filtered* list position, so one skipped `PER` pair shifted every later fact's location - the desync class `build_human_names` was already fixed for once; `EDI_QUALIFIER_OF` lacked `REF`/`PER`, so a qualifier fully consumed by a mapped value still reported as dropped; and `randomize_sbr` ignored its `template` argument, flattening all three 837 families onto one distribution so 837I's institutional `MB` never appeared.
  - **Two verdicts asserted a reason that contradicted the data.** `PER-3`'s cited "qualifier outside TE/FX/EM/UR" on a `PER` whose qualifier was `TE` - the real reason being that the party was the unmaterialised 1000A submitter. A key matches *every* occurrence of its element, so its reason has to hold for all of them; this is the same over-reaching-key mistake that already forced two C-CDA verdicts to be withdrawn, and the module docstring now says so.
- **`app/provenance/edi_ig_verdicts.py` gives every unmapped X12 element its own verdict**, closing the last place a "checked" claim in this app was structural rather than earned. `_dropped_edi_decisions` cited the absent X12 crosswalk *uniformly*, which was true as a general statement and told a reviewer nothing about the element in front of them - including whether it had an obvious FHIR home nobody had looked for. Several did, which is what the whole pass turned up. What remains is keyed `SEGMENT-ELEMENT.COMPONENT` / `SEGMENT-ELEMENT` / bare `SEGMENT` (most specific first; the bare form is what `HI`'s twelve repeating composite slots need, since one answer covers them all), under five kinds: `NO_R4_TARGET` (the base R4 resource genuinely has no field - checked against the published StructureDefinition, and where R5 added one the verdict says so, that being the honest reason an R4-only converter cannot carry it), `NO_PUBLISHED_CODE_LIST`, `ADMINISTRATIVE` (ACH routing detail, submission attestations), `PARTIAL` (a real target exists but not for this party shape, or only when the message supplies what the mapping needs - an 835 claim with no 2100 patient loop builds no `ClaimResponse`, so its `CLP03`/`CLP05` genuinely have nowhere to go), and `OUT_OF_SCOPE`. **None is marked authoritative** - no published crosswalk backs any of it, and the module says so. `test_every_edi_drop_cites_the_absent_crosswalk` was inverted to assert the stronger property (no drop *falls back* to the blanket citation), and a new `test_generated_edi_samples_carry_only_written_verdicts` runs the same check over generated samples - which is what surfaced the elements that only drop in particular shapes (`PER` with a qualifier outside TE/FX/EM/UR, an `SV1-07` pointer resolving to nothing, `SBR02` = `"21"` Unknown). Measured: **2463 dropped decisions across all ten families, zero on the blanket citation.**
- **Every party's address and contact now map (`N3`/`N4` -> `.address`, `PER` -> `.telecom`).** Found by the EDI verdict pass asking, of each dropped element, whether it should be dropped at all: `N3`/`N4`/`PER` were read *nowhere* in `app/edi/`, so every street address, city, state, postal code and contact number in every claim was silently discarded - while `Organization.address`, `Practitioner.address` and `Patient.address` all exist and this app already builds all three resources. They are read from the NM1's **own** segment group (`find_nm1_group`), not the whole HL loop: a 2000B loop carries both the subscriber's `NM1*IL` and the payer's `NM1*PR`, each with its own address, so a loop-wide scan would attach one party's address to the other. `apply_address_and_telecom` is applied inside the three shared NM1 builders, so every family that uses them gets it at once; `Resolved837Loops` carries each party's members for the 837 trio. `PER03/05/07` name what `PER04/06/08` carries (`TE`/`FX`/`EM`/`UR` -> phone/fax/email/url); an unrecognized qualifier is skipped rather than guessed at. The reverse direction emits them from `org_or_person_nm1`, the one choke point every 837 variant already routes its parties through - **`PER02` (the contact's own name) has no FHIR source on a bare telecom list and is deliberately left empty**, which is also the one thing that does not survive a round trip. Generating them exposed a real constraint in `EdiDraft`: `SE01` counted *list entries*, and a builder that returns an NM1 together with its N3/N4 breaks that - `_segment_count` counts terminators instead, exact here because every generated value comes from a fixed pool or a digit string.
- **Known, disclosed scope limits for these slices**: only the **first** `ST`/`SE` transaction set in the interchange is processed (every existing pipeline has a strict one-input→one-Bundle contract; real X12 files are often batched) - flagged here rather than silently, since unlike CDA's unrecognized-section skip this discards data a user might expect processed; only the first 2000D dependent loop is handled (270/271/278) / only the first `TRN` group per patient loop's *immediate* members, not a further-nested `SVC` sub-loop's own claim entries (276/277); `REF` segments (e.g. a group-number or payer-claim-control-number reference) are captured by `group_by_leader`'s member set but not yet mapped to any FHIR field, in any of 270/271/276/277/278/835; 271's `MSG` free-text segments are deferred entirely; granular benefit-amount fields (`EB06`-`EB10`) and procedure-level inquiry (`EQ02`/`EB13`) are out of scope for 270/271, Phase 1 maps category/excluded/description/network only; 276/277's `STC10`/`STC11` additional-status composites and service-line-level `STC` nested under `SVC` are out of scope for Phase 2, only `STC01` (claim-level) is read; 278's `2000F` Service-level sub-loop (per-service-line `UM`/`HCR`/`HI`) is out of scope for Phase 3, only the 2000E patient-event-level set is read, and `UM01`/`DTP`/`HSD` aren't mapped to any FHIR field; 835's `CAS` adjustments now map - each (reason, amount, quantity) triplet becomes its own `PaymentReconciliation.detail[]` carrying FHIR's own `adjustment` code from the R4 `payment-type` CodeSystem alongside CAS01/CAS02 on disclosed local systems, which is conformant because `detail.type`'s binding is *example* strength and is the same thing this module already does with CLP02; both X12 lists are published free (`x12.org/codes/claim-adjustment-group-codes`, `.../claim-adjustment-reason-codes`), the same footing as the Claim Status Category Codes `claim_status.py` cites. **Service-line attribution is lost and `SVC`'s own amounts stay unmapped**: `PaymentReconciliation` has no service-line concept, so a CAS under an SVC and one at claim level are indistinguishable (group, reason and amount all survive - only the line does not), and mapping SVC's own charge/paid amounts would double-count against the `CLP04` already on the claim's detail.detail[]`'s own documented claim-level granularity, not an arbitrary cut - and `Bundle.timestamp` is deliberately left unset for 835 specifically, since no full date+time field exists at the 835 transaction-set level (see `remittance_835.py`'s own bullet); 837P (Phase 5), 837I (Phase 6), and 837D (Phase 7) cover professional, institutional, and dental claims - the full "big five" HIPAA transaction-set suite is now shipped, with no further EDI family deferred; within 837P itself: only the first 2300 claim per transaction set is mapped (the same "only the first X" batching discipline every earlier phase already applies, extended one level further here), 2010AB Pay-to Provider/2310C Service Facility Location/the 2320-2330 Other Subscriber (COB) loops are never read, `SV1-05`'s per-line place-of-service override is not read (only `CLM05-1`'s claim-wide default is), and `CLM03`/`CLM04`/`CLM06`-`CLM11` beyond `CLM05` have no mapped FHIR target; within 837I: `CLM05-1` (a genuinely different vocabulary from 837P's, UB-04 Type of Bill rather than Place of Service) has no mapped FHIR target at all, `CL101`/`CL102` (admission type/source) are unmapped while `CL103` (patient status) is, `HI`'s occurrence/value/condition-code usages (`BH`/`BE`/`BG` qualifiers) are read (to avoid polluting `Claim.diagnosis[]`) but not mapped to any FHIR field, `SV206`/`SV207` (non-covered charges) aren't read, and 2010AB Pay-to Provider/2310B-C Operating/Other-Operating Physician/2310E Service Facility Location/the 2320-2330 COB loops are never read (the identical set 837P already defers); within 837D: only the first 2300 claim per transaction set is mapped, `SV3-03`'s per-line place-of-service override/`SV3-04` Oral Cavity Designation/`SV3-05` Prosthesis-Crown-Inlay Code/`SV3-07` through `SV3-10` have no mapped FHIR target, and 2010AB Pay-to Provider/2310C Service Facility Location/the 2320-2330 COB loops are never read (the identical set 837P/837I already defer); the X12 repetition separator is captured but not interpreted. All disclosed up front in the respective implementation plan, not discovered after the fact.
- **Post-837D consolidation pass**: once 837D shipped as the third independently-tested consumer of shapes 837P/837I had each carried privately, a follow-up repo-wide review found several of those shapes had cleared this project's own "extract on second/third real consumer" bar without the extraction actually happening - fixed in one pass rather than left as a known gap, mirroring the same discipline that already promoted `build_coverage`/`build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts`/`iter_diagnosis_hi_segments`/`POS_CODE_SYSTEM`. `Resolved837Loops`/`resolve_837_loops()` (the full 2000A→2000B→[2000C] walk, confirmed byte-for-byte identical across all three variants) and the trivially-duplicated `find_nm1_by_entity_code()`/`find_dtp_by_qualifier()`/`DTP_SERVICE_DATE` now live in `common.py`, replacing what were three independent `Resolved837{p,i,d}Loops`/`resolve_837{p,i,d}_loops()` copies; each builder's own module docstring was updated to stop claiming an independent resolver it no longer has. `common.py::resolve_837_variant(st03) -> "837P"|"837I"|"837D"` replaces the identical `is_837i_transaction → is_837d_transaction → default` decision *tree* that `registry.py::get_transaction_builder` and `validation.py`'s own 837-family dispatch had each written out independently (sharing only the leaf predicates before this) - the same "one real implementation, not two independently-drifting copies" discipline every other shared EDI helper here already follows, now covering the dispatch logic itself, not just its inputs. Two validation-rule shapes were promoted the same way, as the first `common.py` helpers that build `ValidationFinding` objects rather than conversion-side FHIR fragments (a deliberate, disclosed exception - see that pair's own comment in `common.py` for why this doesn't muddy the module's conversion-shared purpose): `build_missing_subscriber_name_finding(rule_id, segment_path, subscriber_nm1)` replaces the body-identical "subscriber NM1 has no resolvable name" check independently written in all six subscriber-bearing families (270/276/278/837P/837I/837D); `check_service_lines_for_future_date(service_lines, resolve_raw_date, rule_id, segment_path, message, now)` replaces 837P's/837I's near-identical service-date-in-future rule, with each family supplying its own `resolve_raw_date` callback (837D's own version additionally falls back to the claim-level `DTP*472` default via `resolve_line_dtp_raw_date`, a genuine per-family divergence the shared helper's callback parameter accommodates rather than papers over) - fixing, as a side effect, a real inefficiency in 837D's own rule, which had been resolving the full HL-hierarchy walk twice per call (once directly, once via its own `_iter_837d_service_lines` helper) before this consolidation. **837D also gained a validation rule it was missing entirely**: `edi.837d-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved` (`info`), mirroring 837P's `edi.837p-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved` for SV3-11 the identical way 837D's own conversion-side `_build_diagnosis_pointers` already silently skips an out-of-range pointer - the generator's own fuzz comment had already claimed coverage of this validation path before the rule preventing that claim from being false actually existed. On the generator side, `app/edi/generator.py::build_837_envelope(rng, version, billing_org_probability, sbr_segment, include_pat_segment)` replaces the identical ISA/GS/ST/BHT/HL*1 envelope + 2000A billing provider + 2000B subscriber + optional 2000C patient loop construction all three `generate_837{p,i,d}()` functions had independently rebuilt - unlike every other EDI pair's own generator module (which already shared this portion via one `_generate_<family>()` helper), the 837 family's three generators live in separate files, so the shared portion moved to `generator.py` instead (a non-universal-but-shared primitive, the same category `ICD10_DIAGNOSIS_CODES` already established there); verified byte-for-byte identical output across 300 fixed seeds per generator before and after the refactor, not just "tests still pass," since a subtly-reordered `rng` call sequence would break the "same seed -> same output" guarantee silently.

### `hl7` library gotcha (important)

The `hl7` package (PyPI: `hl7`, i.e. python-hl7) is used for HL7v2 parsing:
- `hl7.parse()` **always** splits segments on `\r`, regardless of input. `app/hl7/parser.py::parse_message()` normalizes `\n`/`\r\n` input (e.g. from a browser textarea or an uploaded file) to `\r` before calling it, and drops blank lines.
- `hl7.parse()` has **no MSH-boundary awareness at all** - a real HL7v2 file can be a batch (multiple MSH-led messages concatenated back to back, with no `BHS`/`BTS` wrapper this app recognizes), and the library happily parses every segment from every concatenated message into one single `Message` object. This is a genuine correctness hazard, not just a batching inconvenience: `optional_segments`/`group_segments_by_leader` (used for OBX/NTE/AIP/AIL/AIG) would silently pull matching segments from *every* message in the batch into one Bundle, with no way for a caller to tell the result apart from a single message that just happens to have an unusually large number of repeating segments. `app/hl7/parser.py::_truncate_to_first_message()` detects a second `MSH` in the normalized text and truncates to only the first message's segments before `hl7.parse()` ever runs - mirroring this app's own established "process only the first, disclosed rather than silent" precedent for batched input (`app/edi/parser.py::first_transaction_set`'s identical scope limit for X12). Caught by a follow-up multi-agent code review, not the original test suite - see `tests/test_hl7_parser.py::test_parse_message_truncates_to_first_message_when_multiple_msh_present`.
- When a field/repetition has no component separator (`^`), the library collapses it to a bare Python `str` instead of a nested `[component]` list. Naively indexing a second level (`field[0][0]`) on such a field does **character** indexing, not component indexing, and silently returns the wrong (truncated) value. `app/hl7/parser.py::component_str()` guards against this and is the only sanctioned way to pull a component out of a field/repetition — always use it (or `field_str()`, which wraps it) instead of raw indexing when adding a new mapper.
- The library splits on `^` **purely syntactically, with no awareness of HL7 data types** — it splits a field the same way whether the field is genuinely composite (XCN, CWE, PL, ...) or an unstructured free-text type (`ST`/`FT`/`TX`) where a literal `^` is just a character a user typed, not a component separator. `field_str()`'s `component=1` default is correct for the former but silently **truncates** the latter at the first `^`. `app/hl7/parser.py::raw_field_str(segment, field_num, repetition=0)` returns the field's full original text in both cases (it's a bare string already when the library didn't split it, and gets rejoined with `^` via `str()` when it did) — use this, not `field_str()`, for any `ST`/`FT`/`TX`-typed field (e.g. `OBX-5` when `OBX-2` is one of those types, `NTE-3`, `TXA-25`). This exact truncation bug shipped once in both `app/mappings/oru.py`'s `_build_observation_value` and `app/mappings/mdm.py`'s `_build_binary_from_obx` (both read free-text `OBX-5` via `field_str`) and was caught by code review, not the original test suite — see `tests/test_hl7_parser.py::test_raw_field_str_preserves_a_caret_that_field_str_would_truncate` and the matching regression tests in `test_oru_mapping.py`/`test_mdm_mapping.py`.

### `fhir.resources` library notes

- Resources are imported from the `R4B` subpackage (`fhir.resources.R4B.*`), not the root package — as of `fhir.resources` v7+, the root package defaults to FHIR R5. R4B did not change `Patient`/`Encounter`/`Bundle`/`Appointment`/`Practitioner`/`Location`/`Device`/`DiagnosticReport`/`Observation`, so it's a safe practical stand-in for R4 here.
- `Encounter.class` is a required field exposed as the Python attribute `class_fhir` (since `class` is a reserved word) — it must be passed as a constructor kwarg (`Encounter(..., class_fhir=Coding(...))`), not set as an attribute after construction, because required fields are validated eagerly on `__init__`. `Appointment` has no such reserved-word field and can be built incrementally (construct with just `id`/`status`/`participant`/`extension`, then set the rest via attribute assignment).
- Bundles use `Bundle.type = "collection"` (not `"transaction"`) since there's no FHIR-server interaction in this app.
- Before using a resource sub-object you haven't used before (e.g. `EncounterHospitalization`, `AppointmentParticipant`, `DeviceDeviceName`, `ObservationReferenceRange`), inspect its real `model_fields` rather than guessing field names/shapes — this was done for `dischargeDisposition` when adding A03 discharge mapping, for `Appointment`/`AppointmentParticipant` when adding SIU, and for `Practitioner`/`Location`/`Device`/`DiagnosticReport`/`Observation` when adding resource materialization and ORU. Also don't assume the library enforces FHIR's own cardinality/binding rules: `fhir.resources.R4B.Appointment` doesn't actually require `status` at construction time (only `participant`) even though the FHIR spec calls status 1..1, and it does **not** validate that status/participant-status strings are real codes from the spec's value sets — getting the *values* right (not just the field names) is entirely on the mapper. Confirm real code values against the FHIR spec pages (e.g. `valueset-appointmentstatus.html`), not memory. `Practitioner`/`Location`/`Device` have **no required fields at all** (unlike `Encounter`/`Appointment`/`DiagnosticReport`/`Observation`, which each require at least one) - they can be built incrementally with nothing but an `id`.
- `Quantity.value` (used for `Observation.valueQuantity`, e.g. from OBX-5/6) deserializes as `decimal.Decimal`, not `float` - `Decimal('7.2') == 7.2` is `False` in Python, so comparisons in tests need `float(obs.valueQuantity.value) == 7.2` or a `Decimal` literal, not a bare float equality check.
- **`model_fields` alone understates real required-at-construction fields for several resources this app builds** — confirmed by direct construction, not assumption, each time: `MedicationRequest.intent`, `Immunization.status`/`occurrence[x]`, `Observation.status`, `DiagnosticReport.status`, `Procedure.status` (the last three confirmed while adding the Vital Signs/Results/Procedures C-CDA sections), `CoverageEligibilityRequest.created`/`purpose`/`status` (only `patient`/`insurer` show as required in `model_fields`), `CoverageEligibilityResponse.created`/`outcome`/`purpose`/`status` (only `patient`/`insurer`/`request` show), `Coverage.status` (only `beneficiary`/`payor` show), `Task.status`/`.intent` (zero fields show as required in `model_fields` at all), `Claim.created`/`.status`/`.use` / `ClaimResponse.created`/`.outcome`/`.status`/`.use` (only `Claim`'s `insurance`/`patient`/`priority`/`provider`/`type` and `ClaimResponse`'s `insurer`/`patient`/`type` show), `PaymentReconciliation.created`/`.paymentDate`/`.status` (only `paymentAmount` shows), and `ClaimItem.sequence`/`ClaimDiagnosis.sequence` (`model_fields` shows only `ClaimItem.productOrService` and nothing at all for `ClaimDiagnosis` - both `.sequence` fields are eagerly required at construction regardless). `Task.for` is exposed as the Python attribute `for_fhir` (reserved word, same pattern as `Encounter.class_fhir`). `PaymentReconciliation.created` is a plain `dateTime` (has a date-only form) while `Bundle.timestamp` is an `instant` (does not) - the same field-name-looks-similar-but-type-differs trap `remittance_835.py` was built to avoid deliberately rather than rediscover, see that module's own bullet. Always construct a resource type directly the first time a mapper uses it, rather than trusting `model_fields.is_required()`.

### HL7 SCH/SIU-shaped facts worth remembering

- The `TQ` composite data type (used in the legacy `SCH-11` field) has **component 4 = start date/time, component 5 = end date/time** — not components 1/2 as might be assumed from a generic "timing" field. `TQ1` (the segment that superseded embedding TQ in SCH-11) is much simpler: TQ1-7 = start, TQ1-8 = end, both plain `DTM` fields. `app/mappings/siu.py::resolve_appointment_timing()` prefers TQ1, falls back to SCH-11 — resolved **per-field** (start and end independently), not all-or-nothing: a TQ1 segment that supplies only a start (no end) still lets a usable SCH-11 end value through, rather than leaving `Appointment.end` blank just because TQ1 supplied *something*. Get this wrong (checking `if start or end` instead of resolving each independently) and a partially-populated TQ1 silently blocks a complete SCH-11 fallback — this exact bug shipped once and was caught by code review, not by the original test suite (see `tests/test_siu_s12_mapping.py::test_partial_tq1_falls_back_to_sch11_per_field`).
- `_resolve_minutes_duration` prefers **TQ1-6 over SCH-9/10** (matching `resolve_appointment_timing`'s TQ1-over-SCH11 preference) specifically so a reschedule (S13) with a stale SCH-9 duration but a fresh TQ1-derived start/end doesn't produce a self-contradictory Appointment where `minutesDuration` doesn't match `end - start`. The SCH-10 unit check is lenient (`startswith("MIN")`) rather than an exact match, to tolerate spellings like `"MINUTES"`.
- `parse_hl7_datetime` (`app/fhir_models/builders.py`) preserves a trailing HL7 timezone offset (`+/-ZZZZ`) rather than dropping it and mislabeling the result `Z` (UTC) — silently wrong-by-hours timing is a real scheduling error, not a cosmetic issue, once this helper feeds `Appointment.start`/`end`/`created`.
- `RGS` (Resource Group Segment) is purely structural (sequencing/action-code only, no clinical content) — SIU's `AIS`/`AIG`/`AIL`/`AIP` segments are read directly via `optional_segments()` without tracking which `RGS` group they belong to, since FHIR's `Appointment.participant` is a flat list with no group-nesting concept to preserve anyway.
- `Appointment.participant.type`'s recommended value set (`encounter-participant-type`, Extensible binding) has no code for "patient" or for a location/equipment role — only practitioner-ish roles like `ATND`. Don't force a non-fitting code in just to fill the field; omit `type` when nothing in the binding actually fits (see `app/mappings/siu.py::_build_participants`).
- **AIP/AIL/AIG participants are materialized as real resources, not display text** - and each participant `Reference` carries both `reference` (the real resource, by `urn:uuid`) *and* `display` (human-readable text), per FHIR's own guidance that `display` SHOULD be populated even when `reference` is present, for consumers that render a participant list without resolving the whole Bundle. Per the official v2-to-FHIR ConceptMaps, `AIP-3` → `Appointment.participant.actor` = `Reference(Practitioner)` and `AIL-3` → `Reference(Location)` are fixed targets; both use the shared `build_practitioner_from_xcn`/`build_location_from_pl` from `common.py` for the resource, and `person_display`/`location_display` for the matching `Reference.display` text. `AIG` ("general resource") has **no single fixed target** in the official mapping - the real resource type depends on what `AIG-4` (Resource Type, a local/user-defined table) says the resource actually is. `app/mappings/siu.py::_build_aig_resource` branches on it: a location-ish `AIG-4` code (`LOCATION`/`ROOM`) builds a `Location`, anything else (the common case) builds a `Device` whose `.type` preserves the AIG-4 category (not just used to pick the branch and then discarded). Unlike AIP/AIL, this is built **directly** from AIG-3/4's components rather than routed through `build_location_from_pl` - AIG-3/4 is CWE-shaped, not PL, and the two only coincidentally look similar (both have two leading components); reusing the PL-scoped helper here worked by accident the first time this was written, not by design, and was fixed once caught by review. This is a disclosed interpretation, not something the spec hands you directly - if a real-world sender uses a different `AIG-4` vocabulary, `_AIG_LOCATION_TYPE_CODES` is the place to extend it.
- **`Appointment.comment` concatenates every `NTE` in the message**, not just the first one (`build_appointment_core` in `app/mappings/siu.py`). This replaced an earlier, buggier version that took only `nte_segments[0]` regardless of which segment it actually trailed, which could pick an AIS/AIG/AIL/AIP-scoped note over a more relevant SCH-level one just because it happened to appear first. **Known, disclosed limitation that remains:** every NTE is treated as appointment-scoped and folded into one comment (joined with `\n`) with no group-scoping - an SCH-level note and an AIP-scoped personnel note end up indistinguishable in the output, since `Appointment.comment` is a single field with no place to preserve which segment each note trailed, and no separate field exists for per-participant notes. Fixing *that* properly would require positionally tracking which segment each NTE follows (the same kind of group-tracking `app/hl7/parser.py::group_segments_by_leader` provides for ORU's OBR/OBX) and then deciding what to do with a non-SCH-scoped note, which has no home in FHIR's `Appointment` shape - a real scope decision, not a one-line fix. NTE-3 is read via `raw_field_str`, not `field_str` (see the `hl7` library gotcha above) - it's an `FT` free-text field, so a literal `^` in a clinical note must not get truncated at component 1. The join separator is `\n` rather than `"; "` specifically because a semicolon is a much more plausible substring of real clinical-note text than a newline, though neither separator fully eliminates the ambiguity of folding multiple notes into one field with no boundary markers.

### HL7 ORU/OBR/OBX facts worth remembering

- **OBR/OBX grouping is not optional, unlike SIU's RGS.** An ORU message can carry multiple `OBR` (one per ordered panel/report), each followed by its own run of `OBX` result segments. Which `OBX` belongs to which `OBR` is semantically load-bearing: `DiagnosticReport.result` must reference only the `Observation`s built from *its own* group's `OBX` segments, not every `OBX` in the message. This is exactly what `group_segments_by_leader(message, "OBR", ["OBX"])` is for - see `app/mappings/oru.py::BaseOruMapper.to_bundle`.
- **OBX-2 (Value Type) drives which `Observation.value[x]` choice is populated from OBX-5**: `NM` → `valueQuantity` (with OBX-6 as `.unit`), `ST`/`FT`/`TX` → `valueString`, `CE`/`CWE`/`CNE`/`IS` → `valueCodeableConcept`, `DT`/`DTM` → `valueDateTime`. Other value types (`SN`, `NA`, `ED`, ...) are left unset rather than guessed at - see `app/mappings/oru.py::_build_observation_value`.
- **OBX-11 (Result Status) / OBR-25 (Result Status) → `Observation.status` / `DiagnosticReport.status`** both draw from the same core HL7 status codes (table 0085/0123): `F`→`final`, `P`→`preliminary`, `C`→`corrected`, `A`→`amended`, `X`→`cancelled`, `D`/`W`→`entered-in-error`. Codes with no FHIR equivalent (`B`, `I`, `N`, `O`, `R`, `S`, `U`, `V`, ...) fall back to `"unknown"` (a real `Observation`/`DiagnosticReport` status code) rather than raising or guessing - see `app/mappings/oru.py::_RESULT_STATUS_MAP`.
- **`Encounter.status` for ORU's optional PV1-derived Encounter is honestly `"unknown"`**, not inferred the way ADT's `AdtA08Mapper` infers it - ORU's PV1 gives result-reporting context, not an admit/discharge lifecycle trigger, so there's no signal to infer a real status from.
- **OBX-16 (Responsible Observer) Practitioners are deduped within a message**, keyed by the XCN's id component (component 1), via a `performer_cache: dict[str, Practitioner]` created once per `to_bundle()` call and threaded through every `build_observation()` call for that message (see `app/mappings/oru.py::_resolve_performer`). A report panel commonly has every result verified by the same physician; without this, an N-result panel would produce N near-identical `Practitioner` resources referencing one real person instead of one resource referenced N times. An OBX-16 with no id component (family/given only) still gets its own fresh `Practitioner` each time, since there's no reliable key to dedupe on.
- **OBR-4 → `DiagnosticReport.code` and OBX-3 → `Observation.code` are both FHIR-required** (`fhir.resources.R4B` enforces this at construction). If either is genuinely empty in malformed input, resource construction raises `pydantic.ValidationError` naturally - already handled by the existing "FHIR validation error" (422) category in `app/routes/convert.py`, no special-casing needed.
- **ORU's free-text `OBX-5` values (`ST`/`FT`/`TX`) are read via `raw_field_str`, not `field_str`** — see the `hl7` library gotcha above.

### HL7 MDM/TXA facts worth remembering

- **The v2-to-FHIR IG ships exactly one MDM ConceptMap** (`MDM_T02` → `Bundle`) and treats the `TXA` segment map as trigger-agnostic — confirmed via `build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/v2-to-fhir`'s `artifacts.html`. There's no IG-published guidance distinguishing T02/T04/T06/T08/T10/T11's field-level handling, which is why `app/mappings/mdm.py::BaseMdmMapper` is non-trigger-polymorphic like ORU, not like ADT/SIU.
- **TXA → DocumentReference field map** (verified against the real IG ConceptMap page, not memory): TXA-2 Document Type → `type`, TXA-3 Content Presentation → `content.attachment.contentType` (1..1 in the IG), TXA-6 Origination Date/Time → `date`, TXA-9 Originator → `author` (Practitioner), TXA-10 Assigned Authenticator → `authenticator` (Practitioner), TXA-12 Unique Document Number → `masterIdentifier`, TXA-16 Unique Document File Name → `identifier`, TXA-18 Confidentiality Status → `securityLabel`, TXA-19 Availability Status → `status`, TXA-25 Document Title → `description`. TXA-17 (Completion Status → `docStatus`) is deliberately **not** mapped — no verified code crosswalk from TXA-17's table values to FHIR's 4-code `CompositionStatus` value set was found, and passing an unverified raw code through risked emitting a non-compliant value; omitted rather than guessed, same philosophy as ORU's OBX-11 status map falling back to `"unknown"` rather than inventing a mapping.
- **TXA-19 → `DocumentReference.status` only has one verified mapping** (`"AV"` → `"current"`); every other/absent value also defaults to `"current"` (matching the IG's own stated default) rather than guessing at a fuller crosswalk - see `app/mappings/mdm.py::_resolve_status`.
- **Document *content* is carried by `OBX` segments following `TXA`, not by `TXA` itself** — the IG doesn't show a worked content-transfer example, so this app's approach (every `TX`/`FT`-typed `OBX-5` value concatenated into a single plaintext body, base64-encoded into a separate `Binary` resource referenced via `DocumentReference.content.attachment.url = urn:uuid:...`) is a disclosed extension of the IG's intent, not something the IG hands you directly. A message with no usable OBX content still converts successfully - it just gets no `Binary` and an `Attachment` with no `url` (valid FHIR - `Attachment` has no required fields). OBX-5 is read via `raw_field_str`, not `field_str` - see the `hl7` library gotcha above.
- **TXA-3 (Content Presentation, HL7 table 0191) → a real MIME type has no IG-published crosswalk** - `app/mappings/mdm.py::_CONTENT_PRESENTATION_TO_MIME_TYPE` is a disclosed, local judgment call (same category as SIU's `_AIG_LOCATION_TYPE_CODES`) covering the most common real-world codes (`TEXT`/`FORMATTED`/`HTML`/`XML`/`RTF`/`PDF`/`JPEG`); anything unrecognized falls back to `text/plain` rather than leaving `contentType` unset, since it's a required-by-the-IG 1..1 target field.
- **TXA-9 (Originator) and TXA-10 (Assigned Authenticator) are deduped when they identify the same real person** (matching XCN id component), reusing one materialized `Practitioner` referenced from both `DocumentReference.author` and `.authenticator` rather than building two near-identical resources - same rationale as ORU's OBX-16 `performer_cache`, see `app/mappings/mdm.py::build_document_reference`.
- **`fhir.resources.R4B.DocumentReference` requires `content` at construction** (unlike `Appointment`, which doesn't enforce its spec-required `status`) - confirmed by inspecting `model_fields` and constructing directly, not assumed. `Attachment` and `Binary` have **no required fields at all** (same "no required fields" pattern as `Practitioner`/`Location`/`Device`). `Binary.data` and `Attachment.data` both accept a base64-encoded `str` at construction and store it decoded as raw `bytes` (`pydantic`'s `Base64Binary` type handles the encode/decode automatically) - don't re-`base64.b64decode()` a constructed instance's `.data`, it's already raw bytes.

## Testing

`tests/fixtures/*.hl7` are synthetic, non-PHI HL7v2 messages. They were generated by building each segment as a list of positional fields joined with the correct field index (see git history of `tests/fixtures/` if regenerating) rather than hand-typing pipe-delimited strings — it's easy to miscount field positions (e.g. PV1-19 vs PV1-18) by hand. If adding a new fixture, verify field positions by parsing it back with `hl7.parse()` and checking each field you intend to rely on.

`tests/test_generate_adt.py` / `tests/test_generate_siu.py` / `tests/test_generate_oru.py` / `tests/test_generate_mdm.py` are property-based/fuzz tests over the generators (fixed seed ranges, so failures are reproducible) - they check: the message parses and carries the right MSH-9 trigger; fields documented as "required" above are non-empty across many seeds; fields documented as "optional" are genuinely both present *and* absent across enough seeds (this is the test that would catch a `maybe()` call accidentally hardcoded to always-true/always-false); and, most valuably, that `convert_hl7_to_bundle()` succeeds on every generated message across 20+ fixed seeds per trigger - this is real fuzz coverage of the mappers using far more field-value combinations than the static fixtures alone exercise, and is exactly why the required/optional policy above guarantees convertibility rather than leaving it to chance. `test_generate_oru.py` additionally fuzzes the OBR/OBX grouping guarantee itself (every `DiagnosticReport.result` reference resolves to a real `Observation`, and every generated `Observation` is referenced by exactly one report) - the kind of correctness property that's easy to get right on a hand-built fixture and wrong on a differently-shaped one. `test_generate_mdm.py` similarly fuzzes whether OBX document content varies both present and absent, exercising the with-`Binary`/without-`Binary` mapper paths. Each of the four files also has a `test_generated_message_has_no_validation_errors` case - `validate_hl7()` must report `is_valid=True` (zero **error**-severity findings, not zero findings of any severity) across the same seed ranges; `test_generate_oru.py`'s version is the one place this distinction actually matters, since the ORU generator deliberately produces `OBX-5` values outside their `OBX-7` reference range ~30% of the time, which is a legitimate `info`-severity finding, not a bug.

`tests/test_hl7_parser.py` has direct (non-fuzz) unit tests for `group_segments_by_leader` itself - correct grouping, no-leader-present, and members-before-the-first-leader-are-dropped - independent of any specific mapper, since it's meant to be reused by future message types. It also has direct unit tests for `raw_field_str` (see the `hl7` library gotcha above) confirming it preserves a `^`-containing free-text value that `field_str` would truncate, and matches `field_str` when no component separator is present; and for `optional_segment` (present/absent).

`tests/test_validation_generic.py` / `_adt.py` / `_siu.py` / `_oru.py` / `_mdm.py` are direct (non-fuzz) unit tests, one per rule, each against a small fixture built the same programmatic, field-position-verified way as every other fixture - deliberately not reusing generator output for these (a generator's random field values aren't a reliable way to hit one specific rule condition on demand). `tests/test_validation_common.py` directly unit-tests `parse_comparable_datetime`/`has_time_precision`/`not_in_future`/`parse_comparable_fhir_datetime` in isolation (full timestamp, date-only, empty, garbage, and calendar-invalid input) rather than only indirectly through a rule. `tests/test_validation_engine.py` covers the orchestrator: `is_valid` computed correctly from mixed severities, the unsupported-message-type path (including the registered-type-unmapped-trigger wording fix above), the `MappingError`-from-`to_bundle` gotcha above, and (via `monkeypatch`ing `app.validation.engine.get_mapper`) that a genuinely unexpected mapper crash is absorbed into a finding rather than propagating - a validator must never 500 on the exact malformed input it exists to flag. The four review-caught bugs above each have a dedicated regression test/fixture (`validation_adt_same_day_date_only_discharge.hl7` and `_birth.hl7`, `validation_generic_pid7_invalid_calendar_date.hl7`, `validation_oru_reversed_reference_range.hl7`, `validation_adt_a02_lowercase_trigger.hl7`, `validation_adt_unmapped_trigger_with_type_specific_issue.hl7`).

`tests/fixtures/ccd_*.xml` are hand-authored (XML's self-describing structure makes this less error-prone than HL7v2's positional pipes) but each was spot-checked against real HL7 C-CDA-Examples structure and verified by actually running `convert_cda_to_bundle()` against it and eyeballing the result, not written from memory alone: `ccd_basic.xml` (patient + encounter + two Problems entries), `ccd_minimal.xml` (patient only, every optional field and the whole body absent), `ccd_malformed.xml` (→ `CdaParseError`), `ccd_unrecognized_document_type.xml` (well-formed XML, a document type this app doesn't recognize at all - Consultation Note - → `MappingError`; originally used Discharge Summary's own templateId, then History and Physical's, swapping each time that document type went from "unrecognized" to a real, registered one - the same fixture-maintenance pattern likely to repeat again for whichever document type is added next), `ccd_problem_negated.xml` (`negationInd="true"` → no Condition produced), `ccd_problem_status_observation_overrides_act_status.xml` (act says `active`, nested Status Observation says resolved - resolved must win), `ccd_effective_time_variants.xml` (all three `IVL_TS` shapes - bare `@value`, `low`-only, `low`+`high` - plus `nullFlavor`, one Problem entry each), `ccd_unrecognized_section_present.xml` (a Social History section alongside Problems, proving the unrecognized one is silently skipped while its sibling is still processed), `ccd_problem_edge_cases.xml` (one Concern Act with two `entryRelationship[SUBJ]` children - both must map; a Problem Observation with no `<value>` at all - must skip, not crash; a `REFR`-typed relationship wrapping a Problem-Observation-templated element - must not be treated as an asserted problem), `ccd_header_multiplicities.xml` (multiple `<name>`/`<addr>`/`<telecom>`, a root-only patient/document `<id>` with no `@extension`, an `encompassingEncounter` with two `<id>` elements, an unrecognized encounter class code, and a date-only `ClinicalDocument/effectiveTime` - the fixture that would have caught all three `common.py` bugs above had it existed before the code-review pass that found them), `ccd_medications_basic.xml` (two Medication Activity entries - one with structured dosing (`routeCode`/`doseQuantity`/`effectiveTime` bounds, `moodCode="INT"`), one with the free-text SIG `entryRelationship` (`moodCode="EVN"`) - exercising both dosage-representation paths and both `_MOOD_TO_INTENT` branches in one fixture), `ccd_medications_negated.xml` (`negationInd="true"` → no `MedicationRequest` produced, mirroring `ccd_problem_negated.xml`), `ccd_allergies_basic.xml` (one full Allergy-Intolerance Observation - allergen, Status/Criticality/Reaction+Severity Observations all present - exercising every field this section maps in one fixture, cross-checked field-by-field against a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples allergy entry), `ccd_allergies_no_known.xml` (`negationInd="true"` with a `nullFlavor` allergen → `AllergyIntolerance.code.text = "No known allergies"`, the IG's disclosed text-only negation fallback rather than a skipped entry - a deliberate contrast with `ccd_medications_negated.xml`/`ccd_problem_negated.xml`, which do skip), `ccd_immunizations_basic.xml` (three Immunization Activity entries in one fixture: an administered EVN entry with full structured dosing/lot number, a refused EVN entry (`negationInd="true"` → `status="not-done"`, `occurrenceString="Unknown"`), and a planned INT entry - proving the INT one is silently excluded, leaving exactly two `Immunization` resources), `discharge_summary_basic.xml` (Discharge Summary templateId, an `encompassingEncounter`, and three sections: a Hospital Discharge Diagnosis section and a Discharge Medications section - both now converting to a `Condition`/`MedicationRequest` respectively, having originally been built to prove they were silently skipped before `hospital_discharge_diagnosis.py`/`discharge_medications.py` shipped - alongside a plain Problems section using the exact same Concern-Act shape CCD's own Problems fixtures use, proving it converts identically regardless of which document type wraps it), `ccd_vitals_basic.xml` (one Vital Signs Organizer wrapping two Vital Sign Observations - Heart Rate with an `interpretationCode`, Body Temperature without one - exercising the panel/`.hasMember` shape and the interpretation present/absent branch in one fixture), `ccd_results_basic.xml` (one Result Organizer wrapping two Result Observations - a `PQ`-valued one with a `referenceRange`, and an `ST`-valued free-text one - exercising both the `DiagnosticReport`/`.result` panel shape and two of `_build_observation_value`'s xsi:type branches in one fixture), `ccd_procedures_basic.xml` (two Procedure Activity Procedure entries - a completed Appendectomy with a point-in-time `effectiveTime`/`bodySite`/root+extension `id`, and a negated Colonoscopy with a `low`+`high` period `effectiveTime` and a root-only `id` - exercising `performedDateTime`-vs-`performedPeriod` selection, `negationInd`'s status override, and both `build_identifier` fallback shapes in one fixture), `history_and_physical_basic.xml` (History and Physical templateId, no `componentOf` - proving it's genuinely optional for this document type unlike Discharge Summary's forced one - and two sections: an H&P-specific Reason for Visit section that must be silently skipped, alongside a Procedures section using the "entries optional" templateId - the exact shape a real official HL7 History and Physical example was found using, see `procedures.py`'s own bullet - proving both the H&P-specific skip AND the entries-optional dispatch fix in one fixture). `tests/test_cda_parser.py` has direct unit tests of the parsing primitives themselves (both templateId sibling orders, non-recursive `has_template_id`, all `ivl_ts_bounds` shapes, malformed XML, wrong root element, path auto-prefixing) against small inline XML strings rather than the fixtures, mirroring `test_hl7_parser.py`'s role. `tests/test_ccd_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite against the fixtures above, mirroring `test_adt_a01_mapping.py`. `tests/test_cda_registry.py` mirrors `test_mapping_registry.py`, plus direct assertions that `SECTION_BUILDERS` registers both templateId variants for Vitals/Results/Procedures (mirroring its existing Allergies assertion), that `get_document_builder` resolves `HistoryAndPhysicalBuilder`, and that `SECTION_BUILDERS` registers Hospital Discharge Diagnosis/Discharge Medications against `build_hospital_discharge_diagnoses`/`build_discharge_medication_requests`. `tests/test_routes.py` additionally proves the format-sniff dispatch end-to-end for all three pillars (posting `ccd_basic.xml` to `/api/convert`/`/api/validate` resolves through the same endpoints as HL7v2; `/api/generate?message_type=CDA&trigger_event=CCD` (and the same for `trigger_event=DischargeSummary`/`trigger_event=HistoryAndPhysical`) round-trips through `/api/convert`/`/api/validate` exactly like `test_api_generate_returns_convertible_message` does for HL7v2).

`tests/test_generate_cda.py` is `test_generate_adt.py`'s C-CDA mirror - fixed seed ranges checking: the generated document parses and carries the CCD templateId; patient name is always present; gender/birthTime/address/telecom/encounter/Problems section each occur present *and* absent across enough seeds; both root-only and root+extension `id` shapes occur; both date-only and full-timestamp `effectiveTime` occur; unrecognized encounter class occurs; both `clinicalStatus`-resolution paths occur; `negationInd` occurs. Medications gets the equivalent coverage: section presence/absence, entry count, `moodCode` (INT/EVN), `statusCode` (recognized/unrecognized), dosing shape (structured/free-text/neither), and `negationInd` all vary across seeds. Allergies gets the same, plus the extra branch depth the section itself has: `negationInd` further splits into still-resolvable-allergen vs. `nullFlavor` (both `_resolve_allergen_code` negation paths), and Status/Criticality/Reaction(+nested Severity) Observation presence each vary independently. Immunizations gets: section/entry-count coverage, `moodCode` (EVN-asserted/EVN-negated/INT all occurring), `statusCode` (recognized/unrecognized), and dosing/lot-number presence, each varying across seeds. Vital Signs gets: organizer/observation-count coverage and `interpretationCode` presence/absence. Results gets: organizer/observation-count coverage, `statusCode` (recognized/unrecognized, reusing `app.cda.results.STATUS_MAP` directly so the test can't drift from what the mapper actually treats as recognized), and `referenceRange` presence/absence. Procedures gets: section/entry-count coverage, `negationInd` presence/absence, `statusCode` (recognized/unrecognized, reusing `app.cda.procedures.STATUS_MAP`), `effectiveTime` shape (point-in-time vs. `low`/`high` period - direct fuzz coverage of `_build_procedure`'s `performedDateTime`-vs-`performedPeriod` branch), and `targetSiteCode` presence/absence. Vitals/Results/Procedures each additionally get a `test_*_section_templateid_variant_varies_across_seeds` case (mirroring `test_allergies_section_templateid_variant_varies_across_seeds` exactly) proving both the "entries required" and "entries optional" section templateIds actually occur across seeds - direct fuzz coverage of the entries-optional dispatch fix described in `procedures.py`'s own CLAUDE.md bullet. `generate_discharge_summary` reuses the same section generators (so gets the same coverage for free) and has its own dedicated checks: the generated document carries the Discharge Summary templateId and NOT the CCD one, `componentOf` is always present (`force_encounter=True` - see the `generator.py` bullet above), unlike CCD's genuinely-optional encounter, and its own Hospital Discharge Diagnosis/Discharge Medications sections (`include_discharge_specific_sections=True`) vary present *and* absent across seeds, never occur on a generated CCD or History and Physical document (direct fuzz coverage of that flag's own scoping), and their `statusCode` varies recognized/unrecognized (reusing `app.cda.medications.STATUS_MAP`, since Discharge Medications reuses Medications' own status resolution). `generate_history_and_physical` likewise reuses the same section generators and gets its own dedicated checks: the generated document carries its own templateId and neither CCD's nor Discharge Summary's, and (like CCD, unlike Discharge Summary) `componentOf` varies present *and* absent across seeds - a real H&P can precede any admission. `test_round_trips_through_real_converter` (`convert_cda_to_bundle()` succeeds across 20 fixed seeds) and `test_generated_document_has_no_validation_errors` (`validate_cda()` reports `is_valid=True` across the same seeds - unlike ORU's deliberate ~30% out-of-range fuzzing, the CDA generator never produces a deliberately-invalid combination by design, so this holds for every seed, not just most) mirror the equivalent HL7v2 generator tests exactly.

`tests/test_cda_validation.py` is `test_validation_adt.py`'s C-CDA mirror - one direct (non-fuzz) unit test per rule, built against small inline XML documents (a shared `_doc()`/`_patient()`/`_problem_entry()`/`_medication_entry()`/`_allergy_entry()`/`_reaction()`/`_immunization_entry()`/`_vital_sign_observation()`/`_result_observation()`/`_procedure_entry()` helper skeleton, the same inline-string style `test_cda_parser.py` already established for this package) rather than fixture files, deliberately not reusing generator output for the same reason `test_validation_generic.py` doesn't. Vital Signs/Results/Procedures each get the same per-rule shape as every earlier section: `cda.vitals-missing-code`/`cda.result-missing-code` (`info`), `cda.result-status-unrecognized`/`cda.procedure-status-unrecognized` (`info`, reusing `app.cda.results.STATUS_MAP`/`app.cda.procedures.STATUS_MAP` directly so a test can't drift from what the mapper actually recognizes), `cda.vitals-effective-time-in-future`/`cda.result-effective-time-in-future`/`cda.procedure-effective-time-in-future` (`warning`) - plus a dedicated `test_negated_procedure_with_unrecognized_status_produces_no_status_finding`, since Procedures' `negationInd` overrides `.status` unconditionally the same way Immunizations' does, so the unrecognized-status rule must not fire for a negated entry either. Vitals/Results/Procedures each also get a `test_*_rules_also_run_against_entries_optional_section_variant` case (mirroring the existing `test_allergy_rules_also_run_against_entries_optional_section_variant` exactly) proving their rules still fire against the "entries optional" section templateId, not just "entries required" - the direct-unit-test counterpart to `test_generate_cda.py`'s fuzz coverage of the same fix. A follow-up slice added dedicated tests for the five new BP/pulse-ox/specimen/performer/participant rules: `test_vitals_incomplete_blood_pressure_pair_is_info`/`test_vitals_complete_blood_pressure_pair_produces_no_finding` and `test_vitals_orphaned_pulse_oximetry_component_is_info`/`test_vitals_pulse_oximetry_with_primary_produces_no_finding` (each pair proving both the finding-fires and clean-input-produces-no-finding branches), `test_result_specimen_missing_role_is_info`/`test_result_specimen_with_role_produces_no_finding`, and `test_procedure_performer_missing_identity_is_info`/`test_procedure_performer_with_id_only_produces_no_finding` plus `test_procedure_participant_missing_identity_is_info`/`test_procedure_participant_with_type_only_produces_no_finding`/`test_procedure_participant_wrong_type_code_is_not_checked` (the last confirming a non-`"LOC"` `typeCode` is correctly out of scope for the rule, mirroring the mapper's own identical gate) - `_procedure_entry()`'s own helper gained an `extra: str = ""` parameter so these tests could inject a `<performer>`/`<participant>` element without disturbing every existing call site. **A further follow-up slice added dedicated tests for the three new Indication/Comment Activity/recorder rules**: `test_procedure_indication_missing_value_is_info`/`test_procedure_indication_with_value_produces_no_finding`, `test_procedure_comment_missing_text_is_info`/`test_procedure_comment_with_text_produces_no_finding`, and `test_procedure_recorder_missing_author_is_info`/`test_procedure_recorder_with_assigned_author_produces_no_finding` - each pair reusing the same `extra` parameter to inject an `entryRelationship`/`<author>` element. Hospital Discharge Diagnosis and Discharge Medications get the identical rule coverage Problems/Medications themselves have (`cda.hospital-discharge-diagnosis-missing-value`/`-onset-in-future`/`-onset-before-birth`, `cda.discharge-medication-missing-code`/`-status-unrecognized`), since `validation.py`'s own `_rule_problem_observations`/`_rule_medication_activities` were promoted to shared, `rule_prefix`-parameterized helpers once these two sections became each one's second real consumer - `_rule_problems`/`_rule_medications` are now thin wrappers calling the shared helper with `"cda.problem"`/`"cda.medication"` as the prefix, so a future change to the shared onset/status/period-end-before-start logic can't drift between the four sections that now depend on it. Also covers the convertibility-check paths directly: an unregistered document type → `info` finding, a `MissingSegmentError` from `build_bundle()` → `error` finding, and (via `monkeypatch`ing `app.cda.registry.get_document_builder`) a genuinely unexpected crash absorbed into a finding rather than propagating - mirroring `test_validation_engine.py`'s equivalent HL7v2 case.

The fixture-based `ccd_*.xml` tests above and these two new fuzz/direct-unit-test files are complementary, not redundant: the C-CDA generator now provides the same kind of fuzz safety net `test_generate_*.py` gives HL7v2 (and did in fact independently re-surface, via its own seed sweep, several of the same shapes the fixture-based code review had already caught and fixed - root-only ids, date-only document timestamps, the `typeCode` guard - a real signal that the generator's field-policy design is exercising the same edge space the review found by hand).

`tests/test_edi_parser.py` has direct (non-fuzz) unit tests for the X12 parsing primitives - `read_isa_delimiters` (correct byte positions against a real 106-char ISA, too-short input, non-`"ISA"`-prefixed input), `split_segments`/`element`/`component` bounds-guards, `parse_interchange` (a missing/malformed boundary segment raises `EdiParseError`; a count *mismatch* between a trailer's declared count and the actual number of things present does **not** - that distinction is exactly the parser/validator split `parser.py` and `validation.py` both document), `group_by_leader` (leader-then-members, no-leader-present, members-before-the-first-leader-dropped - the same three cases `test_hl7_parser.py::group_segments_by_leader` already covers, since `group_by_leader` is a direct port of that algorithm), and `group_by_hl_hierarchy` (a flat 2000A-only chain, a full 2000A→B→C→D chain, and an orphaned `HL02` pointing nowhere becoming a root rather than being dropped or raised on).

`tests/fixtures/edi_270_basic.x12` / `edi_271_basic.x12` (+ `edi_270_no_dependent.x12`, `edi_270_missing_bht.x12`, `edi_271_rejected.x12`, `edi_malformed.x12`) are hand-built X12 text, verified the same way `tests/fixtures/*.hl7` were (positional segment construction, parsed back and spot-checked before saving - X12's own segment-index conventions make it just as easy to miscount an element position by hand as an HL7 field). `edi_malformed.x12` is deliberately `"ISA*too*short~"` rather than arbitrary non-X12 text - it must still start with the literal `"ISA"` so `app/pipeline.py::is_x12` actually routes it into the EDI pipeline (proving `EdiParseError` specifically, not accidentally falling through to the HL7v2 default branch and raising `Hl7ParseError` instead, which non-`"ISA"`-prefixed garbage would do). `tests/test_eligibility_270_mapping.py` / `_271_mapping.py` are the field-assertion suites against these fixtures, mirroring `test_adt_a01_mapping.py`/`test_ccd_mapping.py`. `tests/test_edi_registry.py` mirrors `test_cda_registry.py`/`test_mapping_registry.py`.

`tests/test_generate_edi.py` is `test_generate_cda.py`'s EDI mirror - fixed seed ranges checking: the generated interchange parses and carries the right `ST01`; `BHT03`/`04`/`05` and the subscriber's `NM1` are always present; the 2000D dependent loop and the 2000B provider's person-vs-organization shape each occur present *and* absent across enough seeds; `SE01`/`GE01`/`IEA02` trailer-count mismatches occur (both matched and mismatched cases, with matched remaining the large majority - the same "both branches occur, correct stays dominant" shape ORU's OBX-5 out-of-range fuzz already established) for both 270 and 271 independently; 271's `AAA` rejection occurs across seeds too. `test_round_trips_through_real_converter` (`convert_edi_to_bundle()` succeeds across 20 fixed seeds per transaction set, asserting each transaction's expected resource-type set - `Patient`/`Organization`/`Coverage`/`CoverageEligibilityRequest` for 270, swapping in `CoverageEligibilityResponse` for 271 - is a subset of what's actually in the Bundle) and `test_generated_message_has_no_validation_errors` (`validate_edi()` reports `is_valid=True`, zero **error**-severity findings, across the same seeds - not zero findings of any severity, since the deliberate trailer-count-mismatch fuzzing above is a legitimate `warning`, not a bug) mirror the equivalent HL7v2/CDA generator tests exactly.

`tests/test_edi_validation.py` is `test_cda_validation.py`'s EDI mirror - one direct (non-fuzz) unit test per rule, built against a small inline segment-list `_build()` helper (an `ISA`/`GS`/`ST`/body/`SE`/`GE`/`IEA` skeleton with overridable trailer counts and `ISA15`) rather than fixture files, deliberately not reusing generator output for the same reason every other project's validation-rule test file doesn't. Also covers the convertibility-check paths directly: an unregistered transaction set (`ST01="999"`) → `info` finding, a `MissingSegmentError` from `build_bundle()` (no `BHT`) → `error` finding, and (via `monkeypatch`ing `app.edi.registry.get_transaction_builder`) a genuinely unexpected crash absorbed into a finding rather than propagating - mirroring `test_validation_engine.py`'s/`test_cda_validation.py`'s equivalent case in each of the other two formats. `tests/test_pipeline.py` directly unit-tests `is_x12`/`is_xml` in isolation (including the case-sensitivity regression above). `tests/test_routes.py` additionally proves the format-sniff dispatch end-to-end for all three formats the same way it already did for CDA - posting `edi_270_basic.x12`/`edi_271_basic.x12` to `/api/convert`/`/api/validate` resolves through the same endpoints as HL7v2/CDA, `edi_malformed.x12` returns a 400 "Parse error" through both endpoints, and `/api/generate?message_type=EDI&trigger_event=270` (and `271`) round-trips through `/api/convert`/`/api/validate` exactly like the CDA and HL7v2 generate-and-round-trip tests do.

**Phase 2 (276/277) testing** mirrors Phase 1's file shapes exactly, as separate files rather than folded into the Phase-1 EDI test files (`test_claim_status_*.py`, not `test_eligibility_*`/`test_edi_validation.py` additions), matching this project's established "own file per transaction-set family" testing precedent (e.g. `test_cda_validation.py` staying separate from the HL7v2 `test_validation_*.py` files). `tests/fixtures/edi_276_basic.x12` (subscriber + dependent, one claim each), `edi_276_no_dependent.x12`, `edi_276_missing_bht.x12`, `edi_277_basic.x12` (STC-populated response to the 276 basic fixture's two claims - one `F1` Finalized/Payment, one `P1` Pending), `edi_277_error_status.x12` (`E1` Response Error category, proving the `E`→`failed` Task-status mapping) are hand-built and spot-checked the same way Phase 1's fixtures were, with SE01 trailer counts computed programmatically and verified via `parse_interchange()` before saving - not hand-counted. `tests/test_claim_status_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite (mirrors `test_eligibility_270_mapping.py`/`_271_mapping.py`): both patient loops' Tasks correctly reference their own patient (not one "the patient" the way 270/271's dependent-precedence rule does), `businessStatus`/`status` resolve correctly from `STC01`'s composite for both fixtures, the `MissingSegmentError` paths (`BHT` absent, zero `TRN`-led claim-status entries across both patient loops, provider loop absent - the one loop level 270/271 never had to test since it doesn't exist there), and the `is_person_entity` branch on both the 2000B receiver and 2000C provider loops (270/271 only has one such branch, on 2000B). `tests/test_generate_claim_status.py` mirrors `test_generate_edi.py`'s structure exactly, with one Phase-2-specific addition: `test_277_status_category_varies_across_recognized_and_unrecognized_prefixes` directly asserts that every `STC_CATEGORY_PREFIX_TO_TASK_STATUS`-recognized prefix (A/P/F/R/E) *and* the deliberately-unrecognized `"D0"` fallback case each appear somewhere across a fixed seed range - the fuzz-coverage proof that `edi.277-unrecognized-status-category`'s "completed" fallback path is actually exercised, not just theoretically reachable. `tests/test_claim_status_validation.py` mirrors `test_edi_validation.py`'s per-rule unit-test shape, including a convertibility-check case specific to this phase's own deeper HL chain (`edi.would-not-convert` when the 2000C provider loop itself is missing - a failure mode 270/271 structurally cannot have, since it has no such loop).

**Phase 3 (278) testing** follows the same `test_prior_auth_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern. `tests/fixtures/edi_278_request_basic.x12`/`edi_278_response_certified.x12` are built directly from the raw segment text quoted verbatim from real X12.org-published examples (not hand-invented), `edi_278_request_with_dependent.x12` and `edi_278_response_denied.x12` (HCR01="A3", exercising the deny path) extend that shape to cover the dependent-precedence and non-certified-outcome branches, and `edi_278_missing_bht.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path - all with SE01 counts computed and verified programmatically, same as every other EDI fixture. `tests/test_prior_auth_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite: the request fixture proves zero `ClaimResponse` is produced (BHT02="13"), the response fixtures prove `ClaimResponse.request` resolves to the real materialized `Claim` via `urn:uuid` (not a by-identifier fallback like 271's, since 278 responses restate their own request data), `HCR01`→`.outcome` resolves for both a recognized certified code and the `"complete"` fallback for an unrecognized one, and `Claim.item[].productOrService` is asserted in both its coded (`UM03` populated) and text-only (`UM03` empty, matching the real example fixture) shapes - a mistake in the test itself (assuming the real X12.org example's own `UM03` was populated when it verifiably wasn't) was caught by simply running the test against the real fixture rather than trusting the assertion on sight. `tests/test_generate_prior_auth.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 278-specific additions: `test_response_hcr_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving `_build_claim_response()`'s "no HCR → no ClaimResponse" branch is actually exercised, not just theoretically reachable), `test_request_never_carries_hcr`, and `test_response_hcr_action_code_varies_across_recognized_and_unrecognized` (mirroring 277's equivalent STC-category coverage proof). `tests/test_prior_auth_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape, including `edi.278-response-missing-hcr` (asserted absent for a *request*, not just present for a response missing its HCR, to prove the rule's own BHT02 gate isn't accidentally unconditional) and the `edi.would-not-convert` case specific to a missing 2000E Patient Event loop - a failure mode no earlier EDI phase has, since none of them nest a loop this way.

**Phase 4 (835) testing** follows the same `test_remittance_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern. `tests/fixtures/edi_835_basic.x12` is built from a real, internally-consistent raw 835 example (charge/paid/patient-responsibility amounts that actually sum correctly, `BPR02` matching the claim's own paid amount) with every field position independently verified against X12 segment references before trusting it, not just copied wholesale; `edi_835_multi_claim.x12` covers two claims (one paid, one denied, `CLP02="4"`); `edi_835_missing_bpr.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path. `tests/test_remittance_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite: asserts `Bundle.identifier` resolves from `TRN02` (not a `BHT`-derived field, since none exists), one `PaymentReconciliationDetail` per claim with the correct `CLP01`/`CLP02`/`CLP04` mapping, and the `N1`-shaped identifier builder's reuse of `common.py`'s now-public `NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` table (asserting the payee's `XX`-qualified id resolves to the real NPI canonical system, not a local placeholder) - plus, added during the follow-up code review, `test_missing_trn_raises_missing_segment_error` and the two `MappingError`-not-`MissingSegmentError` regression tests for `BPR02`/`BPR16` unresolvable-but-present values (see `remittance_835.py`'s own bullet above for why these were wrong the first time). `tests/test_generate_remittance.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 835-specific additions: `test_bpr02_equals_sum_of_clp04_paid_amounts` (direct proof that the generator's "build claims first, sum into the header" discipline actually holds across seeds, not just by construction) and `test_cas_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving the `CLP`→`CAS` leader/member association is exercised in both directions). `tests/test_remittance_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape; its `_build()` helper composes a flat segment list directly (no HL-hierarchy skeleton to build, unlike every other EDI validation test file's helper), reflecting 835's own genuinely flatter structure - plus, added during the same review, `test_835_non_finite_clp_amounts_do_not_crash_validation` (a `CLP03`/`CLP04` value spelled `"NaN"`/`"Infinity"` must not crash `edi.835-claim-paid-exceeds-charge`/`edi.835-bpr02-total-mismatch`, the regression proof for `parse_decimal`'s `.is_finite()` guard), `test_835_bpr16_in_future_is_warning`, and `test_835_missing_payer_name_is_info`.

**Phase 5 (837P) testing** follows the same `test_claim_837p_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern. `tests/fixtures/edi_837p_basic.x12` is adapted directly from the real X12.org example this phase's own module docstring cites (trimmed to this phase's actual scope - no pay-to provider, no COB) with a subscriber-is-the-patient shape and two service lines, one of which deliberately carries a resolvable multi-position diagnosis pointer (`SV1-07="1:2"`); `edi_837p_with_dependent.x12` covers the 2000C patient-loop precedence case (dependent ≠ subscriber) with an organization billing provider and no rendering provider; `edi_837p_missing_bht.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path - all three built programmatically with SE01 counts computed and verified via `parse_interchange()` before saving, same as every other EDI fixture. `tests/test_claim_837p_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite, plus several inline-built (non-fixture) cases for shapes the two fixtures don't cover: `CLM` absent, `CLM02`/`BHT04` present-but-unresolvable (`MappingError`, not `MissingSegmentError` - matching every sibling builder's own precedent), a `2000C` loop present but missing its own `NM1*QC` (proving `claim_loop` still finds `CLM` structurally while `Claim.patient` correctly falls back to the subscriber - the regression test for `Resolved837pLoops`' own two-independent-gates design, written proactively rather than only after a review caught the alternative), an out-of-range `SV1-07` pointer, and a service line with `LX` but no `SV1`. `tests/test_generate_claim_837p.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 837P-specific additions: `test_rendering_provider_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving `Claim.careTeam`'s present/absent branch) and `test_diagnosis_pointer_out_of_range_occurs_and_is_the_minority` (fuzz-proving `edi.837p-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved`'s own deliberate ~10% fuzz is actually exercised, mirroring 277's/278's own recognized-vs-unrecognized-code coverage proofs). `tests/test_claim_837p_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape. A follow-up code review (run before commit, same as every other phase) caught three issues, each with its own regression test: a service-line `DTP` lookup that ignored `DTP01` (fixed via the now-public `find_dtp_by_qualifier`/`DTP_SERVICE_DATE`, shared between `claim_837p.py` and its matching `validation.py` rule so both sides read the identical DTP - see `test_wrongly_qualified_dtp_is_not_used_as_serviced_date`/`test_837p_wrongly_qualified_future_dtp_does_not_trigger_service_date_rule`); `edi.837p-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved` re-implementing the `HI`-counting loop instead of reusing `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` (fixed to call it directly - the exact duplication risk that promotion was extracted to eliminate); and non-contiguous `Claim.item[].sequence` numbers when a malformed middle service line has no `SV1` (fixed by numbering only the items actually built - see `test_item_sequence_numbers_stay_contiguous_when_a_middle_line_has_no_sv1`). The same pre-existing `"BF"`-qualifier bug fix documented in the `common.py`/`prior_auth.py` bullets above has its own direct unit tests in `tests/test_edi_common.py`, independent of any fixture.

**Phase 6 (837I) testing** follows the same `test_claim_837i_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern. `tests/fixtures/edi_837i_basic.x12` is adapted directly from the real X12.org example this phase's own module docstring cites (trimmed to this app's disclosed scope - no COB, no pay-to provider) and deliberately keeps the example's own two-separate-`HI`-segments shape (Principal + Other diagnosis) rather than simplifying it away, since that shape is exactly what caught the `iter_diagnosis_hi_segments()` bug (see `claim_837i.py`'s own bullet); `edi_837i_with_dependent.x12` covers the 2000C patient-loop precedence case with a person billing provider, no attending provider, and a procedure-code-less revenue-code service line (`SV2*0120**250.00*DA*1~`); `edi_837i_missing_bht.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path - all three built and verified the same programmatic way as every other EDI fixture. `tests/test_claim_837i_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite, plus several inline-built cases: `CLM` absent, `CLM02`/`BHT04` present-but-unresolvable (`MappingError`), a claim carrying `HI` segments for occurrence/value/condition codes alongside a real diagnosis one (proving none of the three pollute `Claim.diagnosis[]` - the direct regression test for the `iter_diagnosis_hi_segments()` fix), a `2000C` loop present but missing its own `NM1*QC` (the same two-independent-gates regression `claim_837p.py`'s own test already established, proactively written here too rather than re-derived from a second incident), and a service line with `LX` but no `SV2`. `tests/test_generate_claim_837i.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 837I-specific additions: `test_attending_provider_presence_varies_across_seeds`, `test_procedure_code_presence_on_service_lines_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving both branches of `ClaimItem.productOrService`'s coded-vs-revenue-code-text fallback), `test_occurrence_value_condition_hi_segments_occur_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving all three non-diagnosis `HI` qualifiers the filter must skip actually get generated, not just the diagnosis-qualified ones), and `test_generated_message_dispatches_to_837i_builder_via_st03` (an end-to-end proof, through the real `convert_edi_to_bundle` pipeline, that a generated 837I sample's `ST03` actually routes it to `Edi837iBuilder`, not `Edi837pBuilder` - the regression test for the `ST01="837"`-shared-by-both-variants dispatch problem this phase had to solve). `tests/test_claim_837i_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape, plus `test_837p_rules_do_not_fire_for_837i_transaction` (proving the `ST03`-based dispatch inside `validate_interchange` itself, not just the registry's own dispatch, correctly separates the two rule sets). `tests/test_edi_registry.py` gained direct unit tests for `get_transaction_builder`'s own `st01`+`st03` dispatch: professional/institutional resolution by `ST03`, and the disclosed default-to-professional behavior when `st03` is absent or unrecognized (`ST03` being situational, not required, per the TR3s). A follow-up code review caught two issues, each with its own regression test: `Claim.item[]` entries never carried `careTeamSequence` back to the attending-provider `careTeam` entry, unlike the identical 837P shape (fixed by threading `care_team_sequence` through `_build_service_line_item()` - see the `careTeamSequence` assertions added to `test_claim_837i_mapping.py`'s existing fixture tests); and `_build_hi_837i`'s own return-type annotation claimed `str` when it actually returns `list[str]` (harmless at runtime since callers already used `.extend()` correctly, but misleading to a reader or type checker - fixed to `list[str]`).

**Phase 7 (837D) testing** follows the same `test_claim_837d_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern, completing the full EDI suite's testing coverage. `tests/fixtures/edi_837d_basic.x12` is adapted directly from the real X12.org example this phase's own module docstring cites (trimmed to this app's disclosed scope) and deliberately keeps the example's own no-`HI`-segment shape (dental claims commonly carry no diagnosis at all) rather than adding one just to force "zero findings" parity with sibling families' basic fixtures - it legitimately produces one `edi.837d-missing-diagnosis` info finding, a disclosed design choice, not an inconsistency; `edi_837d_no_dependent.x12` covers the subscriber-is-the-patient shape with a resolvable diagnosis pointer and a per-line (not claim-level) `DTP*472`; `edi_837d_missing_bht.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path - all three built the same programmatic way as every other EDI fixture, with the `SV3` segment specifically built via a positionally-indexed fields list rather than hand-counted asterisks after an initial hand-typed version put a diagnosis pointer at the wrong element position (caught immediately by parsing the fixture back and checking the field, not by a later review). `tests/test_claim_837d_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite (13 tests), plus inline-built cases mirroring 837P's/837I's own precedents: `CLM` absent, `CLM02`/`BHT04` present-but-unresolvable (`MappingError`), a `2000C` loop present but missing its own `NM1*QC` (the same two-independent-gates regression `claim_837p.py`'s test already established), an out-of-range `SV3-11` diagnosis pointer, a service line with `LX` but no `SV3`, and an unrecognized procedure/tooth-number qualifier each falling back to its own disclosed local system. `tests/test_generate_claim_837d.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 837D-specific additions: `test_diagnosis_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving `edi.837d-missing-diagnosis`'s own present/absent branch, the one family where "absent" is expected to be common rather than rare), `test_diagnosis_pointer_out_of_range_occurs_and_is_the_minority` (mirroring 837P's identical precedent for `SV3-11`), `test_tooth_information_presence_varies_across_seeds` (`TOO`'s own present/absent branch per line), and `test_service_date_source_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving all three of `resolve_line_dtp_raw_date()`'s own outcomes - per-line DTP wins, claim-level DTP fallback, neither present - actually occur across seeds, the one family-specific date-resolution shape no sibling generator needs to exercise). `tests/test_claim_837d_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape, plus `test_837d_line_level_dtp_takes_precedence_over_future_claim_level_dtp` (a dental-specific regression proving a line's own past-dated `DTP*472` suppresses the future-date warning even when the claim-level fallback `DTP*472` would itself be in the future - the precedence order, not just the fallback's existence) and `test_837p_and_837i_rules_do_not_fire_for_837d_transaction` (the third-family instance of the `ST03`-dispatch-isolation check `claim_837i_validation.py`'s own equivalent test already established). `tests/test_edi_registry.py` gained `test_get_transaction_builder_resolves_837_dental_by_st03`, alongside the existing professional/institutional-by-`ST03` tests. `tests/test_generator_registry.py`'s `_EXPECTED_TYPES` and `tests/test_routes.py`'s dropdown-label/parametrized-generate/fixture-based-convert assertions were extended the same mechanical way every earlier phase's own route-test additions were.
